# House decisions

Where a rulebook leaves a mechanic open and the engine has to pick a behaviour, the choice is written here, never made
silently in code. Each entry names the mechanic, the books or rulesets it affects, the options, the choice and why.
A ruleset's own decisions live in its manifest; a table's live in its `house_rule` rows.

| # | Mechanic | Decision | Why |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Who rolls | The app never rolls a Test unless the ruleset opts in; players roll physical dice and report. It may roll on a random table when the GM asks. | Carried over from Auspex: the table keeps its dice. |
| 2 | Initiative ties (`initiative.ranked`) | After the score and the manifest's tiebreaks, a tie goes by actor id order. | Deterministic, so every seat shows the same order; a ruleset that wants a roll-off declares it as a tiebreak. |
| 3 | Removing the actor whose turn it is | The turn passes to the next in order; if they were last, a new round starts. | The table never stalls on a turn nobody holds. |
| 4 | `rerollEachRound` | At the end of a round the order clears and nobody is current; the GM starts the next round, which ranks again. | Rolling is the table's, with physical dice; the app waits for the scores. |
| 5 | Damage past a track's minimum (`health.tracks`) | It is lost. | No ruleset in scope carries overflow on the same track; one that does declares an overflow track. |
| 6 | Dropping to a track's minimum | Starts "dying" only when that track declares death saves. | Tracks without death saves (Shock-like) mean nothing at their floor by themselves. |
| 7 | Damage to an actor already dying | Changes nothing by itself; no failed save is added. | Books differ (5e adds a failure); a ruleset adds it explicitly (`deathSaves.failuresOnDamage`) instead of the engine guessing. |
| 8 | Healing above a lowered max | Healing never lowers a value above a max that has since dropped. | A heal must never hurt. |
| 9 | Healing the dead | Refused; undoing death is a GM edit to the field. | Death is a story beat, not a counter. |
| 10 | Condition stacks and effects | An effect applies once per active condition, whatever its stack count. `ponytail:` a ruleset with stacking effects needs a `perStack` flag. | No ruleset in P1 stacks an effect's value. |
| 11 | Co-GM view in the engine (replaced by #31) | A co-GM sees what a GM sees in actor values; their scopes limit actions and the codex. `ponytail:` per-scope actor views if a table needs a combat-only co-GM blind to secrets. | Running combat needs the stat blocks. |
| 12 | Damage to a stable actor (`health.tracks`) | Any damage that reaches the track starts "dying" again, with both death-save tallies reset to 0. | A stable actor sits at the track's minimum, so a hit is the same as dropping to it; a ruleset that keeps the tallies, or adds a failure as in #7, declares it explicitly. |
| 13 | Instant death (`health.tracks`, `instantDeathOverflow`) | Overflow is the damage left once the track is at its minimum, after temporary points. The actor dies when overflow is **at least** the value of the track's `instantDeathOverflow` expression, whether it was healthy or already dying. A book that says "exceeds" writes `max + 1`. | Books phrase the threshold differently; the manifest's number decides, the engine only compares. A ruleset without the parameter has no instant death. |
| 14 | Counted expiry (`conditions.expire`) | A condition row counts down at the end of each of its holder's own turns (`ownTurnEnd`) or at each round end (`roundEnd`) and goes when the last tick is used; no count (`expiresAfter` null) is one tick. The turn a condition is applied on counts as a tick: "until the end of its next turn" applied during its own turn is entered as 2. `encounterEnd` ignores the count and `manual` never expires. `ponytail:` an expiry tied to the applier's turn needs the applier on the row. | The book's wording ("end of its next turn", "start of the caster's turn") varies; the table sets the count when it applies the condition, the engine does not guess. |
| 15 | Order at a turn change | The caller runs `initiative.ranked` `next`, then `conditions.expire` `turnEnd` for the actor whose turn just ended, then `roundEnd` if the round wrapped. Holders are visited in initiative order, then by id. At `encounter.end`: `conditions.expire` `encounterEnd`, then `rest` `encounterEnd`. | One order for every seat and server; delta order is the undo order. |
| 16 | Rest events (`rest`) | An event resets only the resources whose `resetOn` lists that event. A long rest does not run the short-rest resets, so a resource that restores on both lists both. A reset value outside a resource's bounds is held inside them. | Books differ on whether a long rest includes a short one; the manifest says it per resource. |
| 17 | Pack cites and long text | A pack entry needs a `cite` unless the pack's license is `original` (the author's own work) or the ruleset is homebrew. In a `private` pack, a `text` field value over 280 characters is a warning, never a refusal. `srd`, `cc-by` and `orc` packs must name an attribution. | Pack licenses have no `homebrew`; `original` is its analogue. The warning nudges a summary in the importer's own words (principle 2). |
| 18 | Creation answers | A `choose` step takes exactly its `count` of distinct picks, and `copy` only with `count` 1. `assign` offers a manifest table (each value once) or a point buy; a score rolled on real dice is a `fields` step on the int field, bounded by its min and max. No field has two writers. A skipped step needs `optional`. | The builder never invents a method; a ruleset that rolls its scores says so in its field bounds and `decisions[]`. |
| 19 | Re-applying a condition | `refresh` restarts the duration: the count is the one given, and null when none is given (a manifest declares no default count yet, so there is nothing else to restart from). `none` is a no-op. `stack` adds a stack up to `maxStacks`. | The books say "the duration restarts", never "extends"; one rule for every ruleset. |
| 20 | Reporting a called Test (`test.report`) | The player of a called character reports the physical roll once; that report stands. Only the GM side (the GM, a co-GM with the session scope) reports for anyone and may replace a report while the call is open. The server resolves the outcome with `dice.sumVsTarget`, from the target and modifier computed for the character when the call was made. | A player who could report again could retake a roll that failed once the outcome is shown. The numbers are kept at call time so a condition applied meanwhile does not change what the phone was told. |
| 21 | Who sees a called Test's numbers and results | A called player sees only the calls naming a character they hold, and in them only their own character. The target number and its inputs are sent only if the GM did not hide them **and** the ruleset lets the owner see what the target is computed from (`CompiledTest.targetVisibility`); the modifier and the total likewise (`modifierVisibility`). Their own outcome (success, failure, critical) is sent when the target is shown, or when the GM chose to tell them with a hidden one. Closing with `reveal` also shows the other called characters' outcome and die, never their modifier, total or the target. The table screen sees only that a Test is open and how many have reported. A hidden target never leaves a GM seat. | Conservative: knowing pass or fail narrows a hidden target, so the GM opts in; a derived number is as hidden as its most hidden input. A ruleset where players see each other's rolls can reveal on close. |
| 22 | Building a character (`actor.build`) | A character is built once: it counts as built as soon as it has any field or choice row, and a second build is refused ("Already built"); the GM corrects values with `field.set`. An untyped character is built as the only entity the ruleset's `creation` builds (more than one: the GM creates it with its type). The `name` field, when a step sets it, also becomes the actor's name. Values the creation decided skip `editableBy` (a player writes their own build even into a `gm` field); their types and ranges still apply. The creation's picks are stored as `actor_choice` rows, outside the delta log, so undo does not remove them. | Re-running a build over existing values would silently overwrite the GM's corrections. The creation is the ruleset's own, checked by the server, so the field's later `editableBy` is not the builder's concern. |
| 23 | Starting resources | When a character or creature is first written, each resource it carries starts at its ceiling when it has one, otherwise at its floor. A resource whose bounds cannot be computed yet (a field it reads is still unset) is not created; the GM sets that field and the resource then. | Books say a character starts at full hit points and with full uses; a resource with no ceiling (spare points, a counter) starts empty. |
| 24 | Creatures from a pack (`actor.spawn`) | Each creature is a new `creature` actor named "<entry name> N", counting on from the highest number already used with that exact name (never reusing a freed number's gap). Its values are the entry body copied as rows, the `name` field included as written; the pack version is immutable, so the rows are the snapshot and later edits are the creature's own. The entry must be in a pack attached to the room and served to it (not taken down, and a `private` pack only while its owner is a GM here). `temporary` is kept on the actor for the encounter's end. Undoing a spawn or a build reverts the logged values only: the spawned actors and a build's choice rows stay. | Numbering from the highest keeps "Goblin 3" unambiguous when "Goblin 2" has been killed and removed. |
| 25 | Sheet layout and the Breakdown of a number | A sheet reads `layout[<entity id>]`, else `layout.sheet`, else lists derived values, resources, then fields. A `show` path is a field id, or `derived.<id>` / `res.<id>`; a bare id is a field, else a derived value, else a resource. A path the seat holds no value for is left out. The Breakdown of a derived number is its base (the server's total minus the visible conditions' shares) plus one line per condition the seat may see; a condition it may not see is folded into the base, never named. A condition's share is the value with it minus the value without it. | The manifest does not say which key a layout hangs on. Folding keeps the lines adding up to the number the server sent without telling a player a hidden effect exists. |
| 26 | End of an encounter (`encounter.end`) | `conditions.expire` `encounterEnd` and `rest` `encounterEnd` run as one logged action for every actor that stays; then each combatant that is a `temporary` creature and not marked `keep` is deleted with its rows. The deletes are not undoable: Undo skips the steps of a deleted actor and notes them, and skips the round and turn steps of an ended encounter. | A creature spawned for one fight has no life after it, and an undo that could resurrect rows would need a copy of every one. | 
| 27 | Running the order (combat tracker) | The GM types every initiative; the order is highest first, combatants with none last, a tie keeping the place it came in, and the GM settles a tie by moving one place past an equal (never past a different score). `turn.prev` only moves the marker (and the round back from the top); expiries already ticked come back by Undo. When the combatant whose turn it is leaves, the turn passes to whoever follows, or to nobody if they were last. A creature spawned during a fight joins it as a `foe`, hidden. A ruleset that re-rolls initiative every round is refused by `turn.next` until the tracker can take new scores mid-wrap. | The books give the score, not the tie; the table settles it, and the app never rolls. Hidden by default so a spawn never spoils an ambush. |
| 28 | Encounters across sessions | An encounter may stay open across sessions; with no session running it is paused. Every `encounter.*` action except `encounter.start` (which still says "Start a session first"), every `turn.*` action and the turn-driven `rule.op` ops (`initiative.ranked`, `conditions.expire`) are refused with "Start a session to continue", `encounter.end` included. The snapshot carries `encounter.paused`; the TV and phones show the order marked Paused, with no turn banner and no vibration. Undo stays within the running session. Undoing a turn pass whose previous holder has left the fight skips that step and notes it (`gone`). | The books say nothing about a fight that spans two sittings; the order, rounds and conditions should survive the break without anything ticking while the table is away. |
| 29 | Dice pool (`dice.poolSuccesses`) | The pool is `pool` plus the call's `modifier`, in dice, held from 0 up to 100 (the engine's dice cap); the difficulty number is the call's `target`. A special die is one of the pool's dice: it scores in the bands like any other and also flags `critical` or `complication` when its face is in that flag's range; a pool smaller than the declared special dice has fewer of them. A pass is `score ≥ DN` (`score > DN` with `ties: beat`). Shifts are the dice of the `shift` band, special die included, that can come off the score with the roll still passing, one band die at a time. A report sorts the ordinary dice into bands (dice in no band are left out) and names the special dice's faces; it may count fewer dice than the pool but never more. Bands may not overlap, and a face range must fit the die. The manifest shape is `bands[]` and `special{}` (not the `faces` map sketched in design §4.2): a range with a worth says more than a face map for a die with many faces. | The books say how a face scores and what a special die flags, not how a Test Call's modifier or a smaller pool treats it. Counting a special die in the bands keeps the score what the players add up at the table. |
| 30 | What a player sees of a Dice pool | The pool size follows the modifier's visibility (it is told as one number). A player's own flags are always told: they come from the dice the player read out. Pass or fail follows `showOutcome` as for a sum Test. The margin and the shifts give the difficulty number away (score minus margin), so they are sent only when the number is shown or the GM closes with `reveal`, and then also for the other called characters. Closing with `reveal` therefore reveals the difficulty number to a player who knows their own score, though `target` itself is still not sent. | Conservative like #21: a hidden number is not worked out from the margin. A table that wants players to learn it reveals the Test when it closes. |
| 31 | What a co-GM sees and writes (replaces #11) | A co-GM sees and writes hidden parts only where one of their scopes covers them. `combat`: hidden Combatants, the values of Creatures and NPCs, the whole Encounter, `gm` pack entries. `session`: hidden Test targets and inputs (a hidden difficulty number and a Dice pool's hidden fields, as in #30) and the session notes, which the same scope may edit. `codex.secrets`: secret codex entries and the notes on them. `party`: the `gm` fields and resources of Characters, and the invites. `codex`: every codex entry, `gm` ones included, with each entry's whole audience (so a co-GM's own new entry stays in their view and saving it never drops other readers); the notes on entries still need `codex.secrets`. A co-GM with no scope sees as a player who holds nothing, plus their own held Characters. The GM, the room's owner and an admin who opened the room without a seat see everything; an admin who holds a seat sees by that seat. A `gm` value is written under the same scope that shows it: `party` for a Character's, `combat` for a Creature's or NPC's. Values everyone can see (`public` or `party` visibility) a co-GM may write on any actor, as far as their action permission goes: a combat co-GM may damage a PC's public HP but never touches that PC's `gm` values. A Combatant's health numbers follow the same rule as the actor's values, so the Encounter and the sheet never disagree. The manifest is read whole by a co-GM with `party` or `combat` (`ponytail:` per-kind definitions if a ruleset needs them kept). | A combat-only co-GM can run Creatures without reading codex secrets or hidden Test targets; #11 gave every co-GM the GM's sight, so the scopes only limited actions. |
| 32 | No accounts until a provider is chosen | Every browser is an anonymous user: an `it_device` cookie (random secret, minted by the web proxy, only its hash stored in `device`) names a `app_user` made on its first write (a visit that only looks makes none), and every such user is a `creator`. Admin is the shared `ADMIN_KEY` (header `x-admin-key`, or the socket `adminKey`), not a user. The deployment is local or LAN only (`docs/deploy.md`). | A lost cookie is a lost identity and one key is all admin there is, which a private table can live with; neither belongs on a public host. A provider later replaces `deviceUser` and the key, and the seat rules stay as they are (`ponytail:` in `packages/auth`). |
| 33 | Accounts through our own identity provider (replaces #32 once shipped) | An OpenID Provider (`oidc-provider`) in its own private repository, with its own server-rendered pages (ADR 0006), at `auth.<domain>`; the api is its confidential client and keeps its own opaque sessions (30 days idle, 90 days at most; admin 1 hour idle, 24 hours at most). Only GMs and room owners need an account; players stay anonymous through invites and QR. Sign-in is a passkey plus ten single-use recovery codes, no email; an admin may issue new recovery codes after checking in person, logged. Accounts exist only on HTTPS deployments. The first admin is granted by a CLI on the server; admins sign in with a user-verified passkey, never a recovery code. Clients are registered statically and our own skip consent. Deleting an account needs every room with other users handed over first. | A second app needs single sign-on, and the user store stays ours at no cost. ADR 0005. |
| 34 | Runtime of the identity provider | Node 22 LTS, not Bun; Bun stays the package manager and test runner. | `oidc-provider` warns "Unsupported runtime" on Bun, and the identity service must run where its library is supported. |
| 35 | Auth outside the single mutation path | Signing in, signing out, back-channel logout and managing one's own passkeys and recovery codes are REST writes, added to the exceptions of principle 6. | OIDC is redirects and HTTP endpoints by design; none of these writes touches campaign state. |
| 36 | Seated order (`initiative.seated`) | The order is the seat order the table sets when the game starts, kept in `combatant.ord`; nothing is scored. The first player is a logged turn value (`firstId`), so Undo takes it back. `fixed`: every round starts at the first seat. `rotate`: at each round start the first player moves one place along the order. `manual`: a seat that runs the table sets it (`turn.first`). A combatant who joins mid-game takes the place the table gives it. One who leaves is skipped; if it was first, first passes to the one after it, and if it was acting, as #3. | Rulebooks give the direction of play and how the first-player marker moves, not what happens when a seat leaves. Logging the first player keeps Undo exact, as the order itself is never logged. |
| 37 | Phases (`phases`) | A round runs the manifest's phases in order. In a `turns` phase each combatant acts once, in order from the first player; after the last, the next phase starts again from the first player. A `simultaneous` phase has no current turn: each seat marks its own characters done, and the phase moves on when every combatant in the order is done. A seat that runs the table may move on without waiting, and the log names who was not done. `turn.prev` stops at the first turn of a phase and anywhere in a simultaneous one ("Use Undo to go back a phase"). After the last phase the round ends. `ownTurnEnd` expiries tick only at the end of a turn in a `turns` phase; `roundEnd` ticks as always. At a phase change: `turnEnd` for the actor whose turn ended, then `roundEnd` if the round wrapped, as #15. | Books name the phases but not how a shared phase closes when a player is slow or away. A game with only simultaneous phases counts its expiries by round, so nothing ticks twice. |
| 38 | End of a game (`scoring.endWhen`) | After every action the server evaluates each `endWhen` and tells every seat which are met. It never ends the game itself: a seat that runs the table ends it with `game.end`, which may come before any trigger is met (the table stops early, and the result says so). Ending closes the game's encounter as #26 does and needs a running session, as #28. It is not undoable; a score is corrected afterwards with `result.set`, which ranks again, logged. Which triggers are met is told to every seat, even when a trigger reads a hidden value; a ruleset keeps a trigger to itself by not declaring it. | Rulebooks differ on whether a game ends at once or at the end of the round; the table picks the moment, and the app only says the condition is there. |
| 39 | Final score and ranking (`scoring`) | At `game.end` every combatant is scored with `score`, an automa or NPC included. Ranked by score in `order`, then each tiebreak in its own order. Still tied after every tiebreak: they share the place, and the next place is skipped (1, 1, 3). A cooperative game (`scoring.mode: coop`) is not ranked: each `endWhen` names an outcome (`win`, `lose`) and the whole table gets it; ended early, it is `lose`. | A shared win is a printed outcome in many rulebooks, so a tie is not broken by actor id as an order is (#2). |
| 40 | Hidden scores at the end | While the game runs, a running total is as hidden as its most hidden input, as #21: one built from an `owner` value reaches only that owner and the seats that see everything. At `game.end` every seat is sent each combatant's total, place and tiebreak values; the values the total is built from keep their visibility. | The totals are what the table compares at the end; turning over a hidden objective is done at the table, with the physical card. |
| 41 | Reported Tests (`kind: reported`) | A Test the table rolls and works out itself: the manifest lists 2 to 8 outcomes, each `{ id, label, cite }`, best first (a homebrew ruleset may leave the cites out, as for every rule entry). The GM calls it naming the characters, with no target, dice or modifier. Who may report follows #20: the character's player once, the GM side for anyone and to correct. Who sees the result follows #21: a player sees their own outcome, the others' only once the GM closes with `reveal`, the table screen only how many have reported; with no number to hide, the result is always told to the called player. The server refuses an outcome id the Test does not list ("That outcome is not one this Test lists."), a dice report on a reported Test and an outcome on a dice Test, and records the id; it does no arithmetic and the app never rolls it. | The dice and the working-out stay on the table (principle 5). Most story games (three result bands, partial success, levels of success) then run without a primitive each. |
| 42 | Degrees of success (`degrees` on a `sumVsTarget` Test) | A `sumVsTarget` Test may declare `degrees: { step: 1..20, naturalShift?: boolean, cite }`. With it the outcome is one of `criticalSuccess`, `success`, `failure`, `criticalFailure`: total at least target + step is `criticalSuccess`; total at least the target is `success`; total at most target − step is `criticalFailure`; anything else is `failure`. With `naturalShift`, a natural highest face of the single die (the kept die, with advantage) moves the result one level up and a natural 1 one level down, never past the four levels; it needs a 1dX Test and a reported natural. Ties follow the Test's own `ties` rule at the success/failure line. `degrees` replaces `critOn` (a manifest with both is refused), is a rule entry that needs a cite unless the ruleset is homebrew, and without it the Test is exactly as before (`success`, `failure`, `critical`). The server computes it; the player still reports the dice. Also: `initiative` is optional in a manifest. A ruleset without it has no turn order, `encounter.start` is refused ("This ruleset has no turn order.") and the TRACK tab says so instead of showing the order controls. | Four-level systems (beat the DC by 10 or roll a natural 20) are common, and the margin is arithmetic the server already does (ADR 0004). A game with no turn order (a pure story or one-shot-roll game) should import without a fake order. Other dice mechanics (roll-under, highest of a pool, fate dice) are not part of this. |
| 43 | Spend requests (`request.*`) | A player may ask to spend 1 to 99 from a resource of a character they hold, with a note of up to 140 characters, while a session runs. Never from a `gm`-visibility resource, a `gm` or `campaign` pool ("Players can't spend from that."), a health track (it takes damage through its own op), or a resource they may already spend themselves (`editableBy` `owner` or `ownerSpendOnly`: "You can spend that yourself."). At most 5 pending per character since house decision #169 ("This character has too many requests waiting."). The GM side (`request:resolve`; a co-GM with the `session` scope) approves or denies, with an optional reason. Approval applies `-amount` through the same op and pipeline as a GM's own resource change, so the server clamps it and the log keeps it for `event.undo`; a spend that no longer applies refuses and the request stays to be denied. A request answered, cancelled by its asker or already answered refuses a second answer ("That request was already answered."). Pending requests are cancelled when the session ends or the game is archived. Seats get the pending requests (the GM side all, a player their own, a screen none) and a player their own answers from the last 10 minutes, so the phone can say what the GM answered. | Players can still ask for what the rules keep with the GM without a chat channel, and the spend stays one logged, clamped change (principle 6). Not built yet: a request from a shared party pool (the table allows a NULL `actor_id`; the request is refused until pools have storage, `ponytail:` in `internal/request`), and in a GM-less room a request will apply directly and be logged (group D). |
| 44 | GM-less permissions (`table_mode` `gmless` and `solo`) | With no GM seat, every player seat (a member, or a guest's token) holds `session:run`, `encounter:run`, `clock:run`, `test:call`, `codex:write` and `codex:reveal`, and nobody holds `codex:read_secret`, `character:edit:any` or `request:resolve`. The owner (still `campaign.owner_user_id`, now a player seat) also keeps the room: `campaign:update`, `campaign:archive`, `member:invite`, `member:remove`, `token:issue`, `character:create` and the owner-only ones, and a character the owner creates is theirs to play. A Test is called by any seat for characters only, its target always shown (a call with `hideTarget`, or whose ruleset hides the target, is refused); the permission to call is not the permission to report for another seat's character, and `phase.done` and `test.report` no longer fall back to the table permissions. Nothing is GM-only: no seat receives a `gm` codex entry, GM notes, session notes, a `gm` pack entry or a `gm` clock, a new entry is `party`, revealing to `gm` is refused, and a clock made `gm` is the party's. The whole Encounter and every Test call are shared, and a Creature's or NPC's hidden values are NOT: every seat reads them as a player does. Undo takes back a step of one's own (the seat that logged it, `session_event.seat_user_id`); the owner takes back any; a guest has no user to match and undoes none. A solo room seats one: the owner's seat is the one, so a stranger's accept is refused. Switching to `gmless` or `solo` turns every GM and co-GM into a player and revokes open invites to a GM seat; switching back makes the owner the GM. A new owner (a transfer) and an admin's seat sit as players while there is no GM. | A GM-less table has to run itself, and what would be kept from the players has no one to keep it: nothing is secret rather than secret from everyone but the owner (p2-plan decisions 1 to 3). The sight is not read from the permissions, which would hand every player the GM side; it is the player's, plus the shared fight and calls. The seat view reads the mode from the campaign row loaded with the snapshot, and an action reads it from the row in its own transaction, so a socket opened before the switch keeps nothing. Added after review: creatures and NPCs prepared under a GM become visible to the table after the switch (names and public fields; `gm` values stay hidden); `actor.spawn` of a `gm` entry is refused as `entryNotInPack`; a Test without a GM is refused when its target or modifier reads an `owner` or `gm` field (`testTableOnly`); a solo room issues no guest token; only the owner announces a book source; a player's first report stands, the owner corrects. Not decided, and left as it is: rotating the person who narrates, and a hidden Test target for a table that wants one (both would be new options). |
| 45 | Freeform | A homebrew ruleset (`id` `freeform`, every source `homebrew`, so no cites) with only the engine's structure and no game's numbers or outcome names: a character with a name and notes, a `Health` track (ceiling 10, uncapped so the GM raises one character past it by hand: a field cannot default, and a missing one blocks the sheet), and one reported Test `check` with outcomes `Success`, `Partial`, `Failure`; turns go round the table (`initiative.seated`, the first player rotating), so a made-up game can still run a fight. `game` is `Freeform`, 1 to 12 players, all three modes. The api does not serve it: the web holds the manifest as a constant (`apps/web/lib/freeform`) and "Use Freeform" (Choose game or Quick game) imports it into the user's own library through the ordinary owner-scoped `POST /api/rulesets`, once (the same manifest answers `unchanged`; a library that already has `freeform` is reused). It ships no content, since the three words and a ten are the engine's own scaffolding, not a book's (ADR 0001). | Design §3.3, p2-plan group E |
| 46 | Ruleset templates | The library's import panel offers "Start from a template" for five families, named generically (never a trademark): "d20 roll vs a number" (`sumVsTarget`, the number and the modifier supplied when the Test is called), "2d6 bands" and "Clocks and position" (a reported Test with three placeholder outcomes; the story's clocks are the room's, not the ruleset's), "Dice pool counting hits" (`poolSuccesses`) and "Percentile" (a reported Test with two outcomes: roll-under is not an engine primitive, the table reads the dice and reports). A template holds only engine structure: one entity, one `Resource 1` with no ceiling, and the Test. No number, outcome name or entry name of any game: the names are `Outcome A/B/C` and `Resource 1`, the only numbers are the dice shape the engine needs (`1d20`, one `d6` scoring on its top face). Every `cite` is `""` and the source's `license` is `""`, so the validator's problem list is the checklist of what the user must fill in (a cite and a license, and the placeholders to rename) and the template can never pass for a rule taken from a book. Degrees of success are not in the d20 template; the note beside the picker says to add them. The templates are web constants (`apps/web/lib/templates`); picking one only fills the import box. | p2-plan decision 4: the skeleton saves the user the engine's grammar, not a game's content (principles 1 and 2, ADR 0001). |
| 47 | The recap draft (`session:draftRecap`) | The draft is plain bullet lines of the running session, at most 40, grouped (Tests, entries shown, encounters, clocks, resources at zero, games) and oldest first within a group, for the GM to edit; no summarising. It tells only what the whole table saw: a Test's outcome label per party character (never a number or a target); an entry shown to every seat (never a `gm` or listed one); encounters started and ended with their round count; `party` clocks filled to the last segment; party resources that fell to 0 where the resource is `party`-visible; a game's ranking of party characters. Undone deltas are left out. Later `result.set` corrections are not told. | The recap is read aloud to the table, so a draft that quoted a secret would hand it over; a draft that only counts what everyone saw is safe to read as is. |
| 48 | `@mention` in a body | A mention is stored as `@[Title](entry-id)` in an entry's `body` (GM notes are GM-side already). A seat that cannot see the entry receives `@[…](hidden)` in the delivered body, so neither the title nor the id of a hidden entry leaves the server; the stored body is not changed. An id with no entry (deleted) is treated the same, since the server cannot tell it from a hidden one: a player sees `…` where the GM, whose body is whole, sees a plain title without a link. The two co-GM sides that read every entry (`codex`, `codex.secrets`) receive the body whole. | An id or a title in a body would otherwise be a way to learn that a hidden entry exists (principle 4). |
| 49 | Dealing from a table (`tables`, `table.roll`, `table.deal`, `table.reshuffle`) | A manifest `tables` entry is a rule entry (cite, except homebrew) with `visibility` `gm`, `party` or `owner`. With a `die` (`NdX` summed, or `d66`, 11-66) its entries' `range`s tile the die's outcomes exactly: no gap, no overlap, nothing outside. Without one each entry has a `weight` (default 1). `count` (default 1) is only the copies an entry adds to a deck. **Roll:** the server rolls with `crypto/rand`, whoever runs the table (`session:run`; every seat without a GM). A `party` roll reaches every seat and the TV, a `gm` roll the GM side only, and an `owner` table is dealt, never rolled; without a GM a `gm` table is refused ("Nobody runs this room as GM, so nothing can be GM-only."). The last 10 rolls are shown. **Deal:** each listed character (a pc or companion of this room, one card per table at a time) is dealt one card uniformly at random from the deck, each entry `count` times, minus the cards already dealt and not returned; a short deck refuses the whole deal ("Not enough left to deal."). A `party` card is seen by all, a `gm` card by the GM side, an `owner` card by the seat that holds the character, and by the GM side with a GM; without a GM by nobody else, the one who ran the deal included. **Reshuffle** returns every dealt card of a table. A roll, a deal and a reshuffle are session events of their own type, not deltas: Undo never reads them, so an undo cannot let a seat draw again. | The book decides what the deck holds and who may know it; the table decides who deals. Dealing without replacement is a deck the app keeps the count and the secret of, which is what principle 5 leaves it. A secret role must stay secret from a GM-less table's own runner, so nothing a deal wrote can be taken back. Design §3.4 and §4.8, p2-plan group F. |
| 50 | Actor types of a Test and of a health state (`entities`) | A Test (`tests.<id>.entities`) and a display health state (`health.states[].entities`) may name the actor entity types they apply to, as a resource already does. Absent: every actor type, as before. A named type must exist and be an actor type. The expressions are checked against the named types only, so a Test or a state may read a value only those types have. A named Test's visibility is worked out for those types alone. `test.call` for an actor of another type is refused (`testNotForType`), the whole call with it; a health state never holds for an actor of another type. | A game whose characters and creatures are built differently (a stat block prints numbers a character derives) could not declare a character-only Test: the manifest was refused as soon as a second actor type existed. `ponytail:` the GM's call form still lists every built actor; the server's refusal names the one that cannot take the Test. |
| 51 | Moving a room with built characters to another version of its ruleset (`campaign.setRuleset`) | Allowed between two versions of the **same** ruleset, between sessions (no running session: `changeBetweenSessions`; no open encounter: `endEncounterFirst`), and only when nothing stored would have to change. Each actor's rows are read under the new version: its entity type must still be an actor type; a field it has a value for must still exist with the same type and pass its checks (min, max, length, options); a resource must still exist on that entity type (its value is not checked against bounds, see #8); a condition must still exist; a creation step it chose from must still exist. For the table: a pool must keep its scope, and an open Test call's Test must still exist. Anything else is refused with `versionNotCompatible`, listing the first 5 lines, sorted, plus "and N more". On success the characters follow the room's pin and the room's builder drafts are deleted. Going back to an older version follows the same rule. A different ruleset is still refused while characters are built (`charactersUseOtherVersion`). | Product owner, 2026-11-19: compatible upgrades now, declared migrations later. Nothing is rewritten, so no dry-run screen is needed, and a ruleset that only gains things can be revised while a table plays it. `ponytail:` a version that renames or removes something in play waits for the declared migrations (design §4.5, ADR 0010 decision 7). A draft written under another version is never read again, so it is dropped. |
| 52 | A resource's starting value (`resources[].start`) | A resource may declare `start`, an expression that can name `max` and `min`, the resource's own bounds, like `resetOn[].to`. A new holder begins there, never below `min`, and never above `max` when the resource is `capped` (an uncapped resource may start above its max). A `start` that cannot be evaluated yet leaves the resource uncreated, like bounds that cannot be evaluated. Absent, #23 decides (a ceiling: full, else the floor). Pool resources (`party`, `gm`, `campaign`) get no first value from #23, so `start` does not apply to them. | A track that counts up from zero toward its maximum, such as damage taken, would start full under #23. The field is optional with no default, so existing rulesets and their hashes are unchanged. |
| 53 | What a table event resets or expires | The resets and expiries a table event brings (`session.start`, `session.end`, `encounter.end`, `game.end`, `actor.rest`) are the rules' writes, not a seat's edit: they are applied without asking `editableBy`, for every actor the event reaches. The permission to run the event is the authority (`session:run`, `encounter:run`). A hand edit of the same value is still checked. | `editableBy` says who may change a value by hand. With a GM the GM side passes it anyway. Without a GM no seat is on the GM side, so a reset of a `gm`-editable resource, or of another seat's character, was refused and took the whole event with it: a session could not start (ADR 0009 gives every seat `session:run`). |
| 54 | Shared pools in the primitives (`RuleState.pools`, `resource.capped.adjustPool`, `charge`) | A pool is a resource of scope `party`, `gm` or `campaign`. An op sees it in `RuleState.pools`, keyed by resource id and shaped like an actor's resource; its `max` is null when it has none or is `capped: false`. A delta targets it with `{ pool: "<resourceId>" }`, with `add` only. `resource.capped.adjustPool { resource, delta }` needs the permission `pool:adjust`, held by the GM, a co-GM with the `session` scope and every player seat (never a screen; without a GM every player holds it, like the other table permissions). It clamps like `adjust`, returns one `add` of what remains, and refuses `unknownPool` for a pool the state lacks. `rest.apply` also resets pools, only when it names no `actorIds`, and an event resets only the pools that list it. `charge(state, pool, amount)` pays a rule's cost in full or refuses `cannotPay`: it never clamps, and its message never states the pool's value. | A table shares a pool and the GM may keep a hidden one. The same clamp and the same delta path keep the server the only writer. A cost that cannot be paid is a refusal, not a smaller payment, and the refusal must not leak a hidden pool. | 55 | Campaign fields (`campaignFields`, the `campaign` root, `at()`) | A manifest may declare `campaignFields`, an optional record of at most 50 entries: `int` (whole `min`, `max`, `default` between -1000 and 1000), `enum` (1 to 20 values, a default among them) or `bool`, each with a `label` and a `cite` (except in a homebrew ruleset). No text, ref or list types. A value that reads `campaign.*` is tainted like `party.characters`: at least `party` visibility. The value in play is the stored one when it fits the field's check, otherwise the field's default; unknown stored ids are dropped. `at(group, n)` returns the member of a field group whose id ends in the number `n` (`at(form, 3)` is `form.f3`); it counts as reading every member of the group, and a missing member is the usual missing-value error. | A tier or a chapter belongs to the whole campaign and many formulas read it, so it needs a declared, cited home instead of a field copied onto every actor. The default means a missing or bad stored value never leaves a formula without a number. Every seat already knows the table's tier, hence the `party` taint. `at()` lets a creature pick one of its numbered columns by that value. | 56 | Shared pools: storage, the first value, who may change one by hand, undo | A pool is stored in `resource_pool (campaign_id, scope, resource_id, value)` and has no row until its first change; until then its value is its minimum (a resource's `start` does not apply, #52). Every write adds to the stored value in one statement, clamped to the bounds; `capped: false` has no ceiling. Bounds and resets are evaluated with `party.characters` only; a pool whose bounds cannot be evaluated is out of the state and refused as unknown. A hand adjust (`resource.capped.adjustPool`) is checked: the GM side (a seat that runs the table with a GM; never in a room without one) may add and take on any pool. Anyone else may touch only a `party` pool, and only as a holder (a seat holding a character): `owner` both ways, `ownerSpendOnly` take only (an add is refused `onlySpend`), `gm` never (`gmOnly`). A `gm` or `campaign` pool is the GM side's only, so without a GM nobody changes it by hand. One statement per write keeps the server the only writer and keeps two taps from overwriting each other. Starting at the minimum means a pool needs no seeding. | 57 | Sides order (`initiative.sides`) | Combatants belong to sides (2 to 4, listed in the order they pick). The side that is up is the first side, in the list rotated to start after the side that acted last (at the start of a round: the round's first side by `firstSide`), that has a pending member: one in the order, not done, not unable, not acting and not barred this round. That side chooses who acts (`pick`); picking while someone acts ends that turn first; `force` is a free override for a slip. A side pays a shared pool to act out of turn (`seize`, through `charge`, so a pool that cannot pay refuses it): once until another side has acted, never for the side already up, and at the start of a round never for the round's first side. `hold` ends a turn holding an action and `interrupt` plays it. `mark` sets or clears a member's done; clearing gives another turn. `bar` marks a member as taking no turn through `throughRound` (not once it has acted or while acting) and `lift` pays to free it. With a `setup` the fight starts not begun: only `bar`, `lift`, `mark` and `begin` work, and `begin` clears done and held but keeps bars. `roundEnd: auto` wraps the round when nobody is left able to act; `manual` waits for `newRound`. A wrap empties done and held, and empties bars only when the round that ended is at or past `throughRound`. The turn values written are `begun`, `lastSide`, `outOfTurnSide`, `held`, `barred` and `done`, each a whole-value `set`. Sides do not combine with `phases` yet. | Books for this style say who picks and how a side may jump the line, but not what happens to a turn in progress when another is picked, or to a held action when the round wraps. Whole-list sets keep Undo exact, as #36 does. The cost, the bar and the setup are rules of the game, so each carries its cite. The api's storage of these values, its routing and the screens are the next steps. |
| 58 | Campaign fields at a table: storage, the value in play, setting it, version changes | A value is stored per room in `campaign_field` (an int is `int_value`, a bool is `int_value` 1 or 0, an enum is `text_value`); there is no row until it is set. The value in play is always `engine.CampaignValues`: the stored value when it fits the pinned version's definition, else the field's default. Every expression scope the api builds carries it: an actor's derived values, resource bounds, starting values and resets, and a shared pool's bounds. `campaign.setField { fieldId, value }` needs `campaign:update` (the GM's seat, or the owner's in a room without a GM). It is allowed at any time, with or without a session or a fight. While a session runs it is logged as `campaign.field` (field id, old value, new value); it is not undoable, and setting the value in play again changes nothing. It is refused with no ruleset pinned, for a field the ruleset does not declare (`unknownCampaignField`), or for a value that does not fit (`campaignFieldValue`). A version change is refused (`versionNotCompatible`, lines prefixed `table: `) when the new version drops the field, changes its type, or the stored value no longer fits; a room with no built character skips that check, so its unfit values are deleted instead, and unpinning deletes them all. Every seat, the screen included, receives `campaign.fields`: each declared field at its value in play. | A campaign setting such as a tier changes mid-campaign and many rules read it, so the GM changes it when needed. Receiving it is no leak: a value that reads `campaign.*` is already tainted as `party`. Falling back to the default keeps a stale or missing row from breaking every actor. |
| 59 | Shared pools in the snapshot and the Seat view | `LiveState.pools` lists every pool the pinned ruleset declares, in manifest order, each as `{ id, label, scope, value, min, max, capped, visibility, editableBy }` (`max` null when none; `capped` false for an uncapped pool, so a screen can still draw a bar). A seat on the GM side (a room with a GM, and a seat that runs the table: the same test as `CheckPoolEditable`, #56) receives every pool. Anyone else receives `public` and `party` pools, and an `owner` pool only when its scope is `party` and the seat holds a character (never the screen); a `gm` pool, and an `owner` pool of scope `gm` or `campaign`, reach nobody off the GM side. A room without a GM has no GM side: the owner and an unseated admin receive no `gm` pool. A seat with no ruleset in its view receives none. A pool the seat may not see is dropped whole, not blanked. | One place decides what a seat receives. A screen draws a pool from its entry alone, and a hidden pool must not be detectable. `owner` has no single owner on a shared pool, so it means "a seat holding a character", and only where the pool is the party's. |
| 60 | Sides order in the api: sides, unable, storage, expiries (`initiative.sides`) | A sides order may list on each side the actor entity types that join it (`sides[].entities`; a type is on at most one side) and the health states that leave a combatant unable (`unableStates`). `encounter.add` names a side or lets each actor join the side that lists its entity type; an actor whose type is on no side is refused by name, and a creature spawned mid-fight joins the same way (still hidden, #27). A named side must be a declared one; every other order keeps `party`, `foe` and `other`. The side is fixed for the fight. With a `setup` the fight starts not begun (a flag; the round is still 1). The turn values `begun`, `lastSide`, `outOfTurnSide`, `held`, `barred` and `done` are stored on the encounter and combatant rows and undone as whole-value sets, so one Undo returns an out-of-turn move and its cost together. Every op runs through `rule.op` as one logged action: the op's deltas, then `turnEnd` for the actor whose turn they ended (an op that writes `currentId` while someone acts: `next`, `hold`, `pick`, `seize`, `newRound`), then `roundEnd` when they wrapped the round, as #15. `turn.next` is the primitive's `next`; `turn.prev` and `phase.done` are refused. When the acting combatant is removed nobody acts; the turn does not pass on. A combatant is unable while it has any of `unableStates` now; a state that cannot be evaluated counts as able. The snapshot of a sides fight carries `begun`, `lastSide`, `outOfTurnSide`, `sideUp` and each combatant's `held`, `barred` and `unable`; a hidden combatant is dropped with its flags. | Books give the sides and who may jump the line, not which creatures belong to which side, what a downed one does to the round, or what a removed actor's turn becomes. Naming the types and the states in the manifest keeps those decisions out of code. One expiry planner for every op keeps #15's order in one place. `turn.prev` would not undo a turn's expiries; Undo does. |
| 61 | Pack entries carry effects | A pack entry has `effects` (at most 20, the manifest's own `Effect` shape: path, mode, value, phase, priority, when, cite). An actor gets the effects of every entry it holds an `actor_choice` row for, when the row's step is a `choose` step of the pinned ruleset and the entry is found by that step's `pick` type and the row's key among the packs the room is served (attached, not taken down, and a `private` pack only while its owner runs the room: principle 4). Nothing else applies them: a spawned creature does not get its own stat block's effects, and an entry the room is no longer served adds nothing, with no error. They apply with the active conditions' effects, sorted by `(priority, source id)`; a condition's source id is its id and an entry's is `<type>/<key>`. Derivation reads every served pack's entry whoever looks and whatever the entry's `visibility`, so a number is the same for every seat (the lookup a player builds from stays seat-scoped). At import each effect is checked against every actor type a `choose` step picks the entry's type for, as a condition's effect is at compile; an entry type no step picks cannot carry effects. The entry's own cite covers its effects unless one has its own. The visibility check counts the effect's inputs, not the entry's `visibility`. `pack_entry.effects` is JSONB (migration 0013); an entry with none hashes and exports as before. The breakdown names an entry by its `name`. | Bonuses from a species, talent, armour or mutation had no place in a ruleset: the GM typed each into a field by hand (`docs/p3-plan.md` gap 4). Reusing the condition's effect shape and its order keeps one place for what changes a number. Deriving from every attached entry keeps numbers equal across seats; what a seat may read of the entry is still the entry's `visibility`. Choices are not undone with the build (the `ponytail:` note in `build.go`), so an undo does not remove their effects either. |
| 62 | A ruleset picks the ledger builder; pack entries carry its records | A manifest may have one `build` block (`strategy: "ledger"`, ADR 0011): the actor type it builds, the builder's `params` (parsed as `BuildParams` with its refinements; a problem inside is reported under `build.params`), `kinds` (the entity type that holds each kind of catalogue record, keyed as the builder's `Creation` is: `keywords`, `factions`, `sub-factions`, `species`, `backgrounds`, `option-groups`, `archetypes`, `talents`, `packages`, `disciplines`, `powers`, `languages`, plus `wargear`), `sheet` (one actor field per Attribute, Skill and mod of the params' vocabulary, `int`; `armourRating`, `powered`, `speed` `int`; `species`, `archetype` `text`; `keywords` `list`; optional `wealth` and `corruption`, resources the actor carries with scope `entity`), `tier` (an `int` campaign field whose `min` and `max` lie within `params.tiers`), an optional `xp` (`int` field) and a `cite`. Every kind names an existing entity that is not an actor, and no two kinds share one; no field is named twice. The actor type it builds takes no `creation` steps. An entry whose type is a kind carries `record`, its catalogue record, and no other entry may. The record parses with its kind's schema (problems under `entries[i].record`), is at most 32 KiB, and its `name` is the entry's `name`; its `id` is free of the entry's `key`, but no two entries of one type in a pack carry the same record `id`, and a reader finds a record by its type and `id`, never by key. What is stored, hashed and served is the record as its schema answers it, keys in the schema's order. `pack_entry.record` is `json`, not the JSONB the data rules name for whole documents (migration 0014). The record reaches seats with its entry (`/rules`, `null` when none, a `gm` entry the GM side only); an entry without one hashes and exports as before. | Wrath & Glory characters are bought with XP through a whole builder ported from Auspex (ADR 0011), which reads its parameters and catalogue as data: the manifest names where that data and the resolved sheet live, and the validator holds every name to what the manifest declares. Record ids are unique only within a kind (a keyword, a faction and a background table share one in real catalogues), while entry keys are unique across a pack, so the generator keys an entry `<kind>.<id>`. The builder depends on the order of a record's keys (ADR 0011, decision 6) and `jsonb` reorders them, so the column is `json` and the api decodes a pack in its order. |
| 63 | The server resolves and saves a ledger build | `build.save { actorId, build }` saves a character the ledger builder buys (ADR 0011, #62), through the one mutation path, for a seat with `character:edit:own` on that actor or the GM side. `build` is the builder's input document as JSON text, at most 64 KiB of UTF-8: a socket payload reaches the api decoded into maps, which lose the key order the builder reads, so the api decodes the text itself in order and keeps that order to the row and back to a seat. Refused: a pinned ruleset without `build`, or an actor typed as another entity (`notLedgerBuilt`); an actor with field rows and no stored build, filled another way (`alreadyBuilt`); a `tier` other than the room's value of the campaign field `build.tier` names (`buildTierMismatch`); text the builder cannot read, or a sheet out of range (`buildInvalid`, with the builder's error); a build with problems or choices left (`buildNotReady`, the first five of each, a GM's question marked as one, cut to 1,000 characters). `build.gm` is the GM's: from a seat off the GM side of the actor's kind, the stored `gm` (or none, `{}`) replaces the incoming one, silently, in its place or at the end. The server resolves with no `play` against the room's catalogue: every record of the packs it is served (#61's rule), grouped by kind through `build.kinds`, wargear by its kind, the first per (kind, id) by pack slug, in the pack's order; every source is allowed (`homebrew`, a `ponytail:`). A catalogue record's entry is `party` or `public`, never `gm` (pack validation), so one catalogue serves every seat and a sheet holds no hidden name. A ready build, in one transaction: types the actor (and its ruleset version); writes every Attribute, Skill rating and mod of `build.sheet` (one the sheet does not list as 0), `armourRating`, `powered`, `speed`, `species`, `archetype` and `keywords` as field rows through the delta pipeline, trusted as a creation's values; on the first save only, starts the resources as `actor.create` does, `wealth` and `corruption` at the sheet's values (a later save leaves every resource to play); stores the build and the resolved sheet in `actor_build` (migration 0015, `json` as in #62), one row per actor, replaced on each save. The ledger, the pending choices and the problems are not stored. The documents are outside the delta log, as `actor_choice` is: Undo takes the field rows back, not the build (a `ponytail:`). An actor's `ledger` in the state is `{ build, sheet }` as stored, for the GM side of its kind and a seat holding it, `null` for everyone else. `GET /api/campaigns/:id/catalogue` answers any seat of the room `{ params, kinds, wargear }` from the same function, 404 without a ledger builder. **The draft** (migration 0016): `build.save` takes `draft` (default false); with `draft: true` it runs the same permission, `notLedgerBuilt`, `alreadyBuilt`, Tier and GM's-part rules and the same cap, decodes the text in order (not JSON, or not an object: `buildInvalid`), and stores it as the actor's draft without resolving and without writing a field, resource or type; an unfinished draft is no refusal. A ready save writes `build` and `sheet` and clears `draft`; `build` and `sheet` are nullable together, so a row may hold a draft and no build yet, and such a row is no character "filled another way" (`alreadyBuilt` fires only for an actor with field rows and no row at all). A save or draft from off the GM side takes the GM's part from the stored draft, which is newer, else from the stored build. The state's `ledger` is `{ build, sheet, draft }`, each `null` when not stored, for the same seats. **The web:** the build page opens the ledger builder for an untyped actor or one of `build.actor` (any other keeps the creation steps); the browser reads the catalogue and uses its records as served, every source let in as the server resolves; the screens start from the draft, else the build, else an empty build, always at the room's Tier, which only the campaign settings change (the Tier step shows it). "Save character" sends a ready build; "Send to the GM" (a player) or "Save for later" (the GM side) sends a draft, and every change is kept as a draft a moment after the last one, as the creation builder keeps its own; `build` goes as `JSON.stringify` gives it. `gm` is the seat's GM side of the actor, as the Seat view decides it. The GM's Party tab gives every character the ledger builds Build, Review build (a draft waits) or Open builder (built: a GM may re-save before play); a player's phone keeps its build entry until the first ready save. The sheet lists the saved sheet's talents, abilities, weapons, gear (with the armour) and powers under the ruleset's layout, each a name, its cite (the sheet carries none for a weapon, an armour or a gear line), its numbers and its summary. | The server decides (principle 6): a client's resolution is only a preview, so the api resolves every build with the Go twin and writes only a ready one. The sheet's numbers become field rows so derived values, Tests and health read them as they read a sheet filled by hand (ADR 0011, decision 3). Starting the resources once and leaving them after keeps a rebuild from refilling what play spent. Keeping the GM's part from a player keeps approvals the GM's without a second action. Text over the socket is the smallest way to keep the order the builder depends on without replacing the socket's decoder. A build with a GM-judged item cannot be saved until the GM approves it, and the GM has to see it first: a draft stored unresolved shows it to the GM and changes nothing of the actor until a ready save. The draft holds the newest GM's part, so a player's save after the GM approved in a draft keeps the approval. |
| 64 | In play: XP, purchases and Ascension for a ledger build | **The manifest:** the `build` block (#62) gains three optional keys: `ascension { xp, cite }` (an int from 1: the XP every character must earn in a Tier before the group may Ascend, and what a milestone Ascension awards), `gone` (at most 10 ids, each a health state that applies to `build.actor`, else `build.gone[i]: unknown health state`; a character in any is left out of the Ascension count) and `award { max, suggested, cite }` (`max` 1 to 1000; `suggested` at most 6, each from 0 to `max`, strictly ascending; the XP a session's end may give and the amounts offered). `gone` is optional rather than defaulted to `[]`, so a ruleset without the three normalizes, compiles and hashes as before. **Made** means the actor has a stored build (`actor_build.build` not null); a draft makes no character. **`build.save` on a made character:** for every seat, `tier` must be the stored build's (`buildMadeAtTier`; the room's Tier may have risen since); a seat off the GM side of the actor's kind may change only `advancement` and the choices under `advance/`: `creationKey` (both engines, copied from Auspex: the build parsed by its schema, without `advancement`, `gm` and the `advance/` choices, keys sorted at every depth by UTF-16 code units where Auspex uses `localeCompare`) must equal the stored build's, else `creationLocked`. A draft save on a made character is held to both, so a player cannot park a changed creation; a build that does not parse is `buildInvalid`. The client's `advancement.boughtIn` is replaced by the stored history before resolving. The builder resolves with `play`: the room's value of the Tier field, the actor's `build.xp` value (0 unset or unnamed), and the row's `xpAtAscension` and `packageWaived`. After a ready save the history is stamped as Auspex's `stampHistory` does: every advancement ledger key still bought keeps its stored session number, in the stored order, the rest are dropped, and each new key takes the room's highest session number (the open session, else the last), or stays unstamped with no session yet; the history is set in the build's own `advancement` in its place (added only when the client sent one or it is not empty). `xp_spent` is the resolved `advancement.spent`. Field rows are rewritten from the new sheet and resources are left to play, as for any later save; the sheet document is replaced whole (a `ponytail:` until Auspex's `keepPlay` is ported). The not-made path is unchanged: no `play`, and the client's history is stored as sent (only a label; the first in-play save rewrites it). **A session's end:** `session.end` takes `award` (int 0 to 1000, default 0). A non-zero award needs `build.xp` and `build.award` (`noAward`) and at most `award.max` (`awardTooHigh`); it adds to the `build.xp` field of every actor of `build.actor` whose kind is `pc`, whatever its health, through the pipeline as a rule's own write, before the session ends so it is logged in it. **Ascension** (migration 0017: `actor_build.xp_spent`, `xp_at_ascension`, `package_waived`; an actor with no row gets one with no documents when Ascension writes to it): `build.ascend { kind: experience | milestone, waivePackage }`, permission `campaign:update` (as `campaign.setField`). In order: `build` has `ascension` and `xp` (`noAscension`); the room's Tier is below `params.tiers.max` (`topTier`; a campaign field whose own max is lower refuses the write as `campaignFieldValue`); the group is every `pc` of `build.actor` in none of the `gone` states, evaluated as the sides order's `unableStates` are (a state that cannot be evaluated does not count), and an empty group is `nobodyToAscend`; `experience` needs every member's XP less its `xpAtAscension` to reach `ascension.xp` (`ascensionShort`, naming the first 5 as "A, B and C", then "and N more"; the verb is always "have"); `milestone` awards `ascension.xp` to every `pc` of the type, gone ones too. Then the Tier field goes up by 1 (logged as `campaign.field`) and every `pc` of the type gets `xpAtAscension` = its XP after any award and `packageWaived` = `waivePackage`. A character left with an unbought Ascension slot plays on; its next save is refused until the slot is settled. **`campaign.setField` on the Tier field:** raising it restarts every such character's count at its XP and clears the waive; lowering it touches neither; no XP is awarded. **The state:** an actor's `ledger` gains `xpSpent`, `xpAtAscension` and `packageWaived`, for the same seats; a row with no documents has `build`, `sheet` and `draft` null and the three set. The api always sends them; the TypeScript type keeps them optional until the web's ledger fixtures carry them (a `ponytail:`). | The server decides (principle 6): once made, a character's creation is the GM's, its Tier is its own, and what it may spend is what the table awarded, so the server prices every purchase in play and keeps the purchase history itself. Auspex's behaviour is the reference, kept except where the platform differs: XP lives in a ruleset field and rises through the pipeline, the Ascension numbers come from the manifest, "Dead or Lost" becomes the ruleset's `gone` states, and a sort Go can reproduce replaces `localeCompare`, which only the equality of two keys depends on. Raising the Tier by hand is an Ascension without its XP, so the count starts again there too. |
| 65 | A World is an imported document a room pins | **The document** is `World` (`packages/rules-engine/src/world.ts`, ADR 0010 decision 2): `id` (kebab-case, the owner's slug), `version` (semver), `core` (a semver range, checked by its shape as a ruleset's is), `name` (1 to 80) and `theme` (colour values for the `light` and/or `dark` set by the token names of `WORLD_COLOUR_TOKENS`, each an opaque six-digit hex; picks from `WORLD_TYPEFACES`, `WORLD_TEXTURES` and `WORLD_SHAPES`). The first cut is the look only: no terms, no handout styles (issue #159), no images. **Validation** (`validateWorld` and `engine.ValidateWorld`, the same problems at the same paths, held equal by the vectors): the size as serialised, at most `MAX_WORLD_BYTES` (64 KiB), as `(root)`; the schema; then contrast. **Contrast** (principle 8): for each set the World brings, its values are laid over the base Theme's (`WORLD_BASE_COLOURS`, lantern.css as it resolves on `:root`; a web test holds the table and the token list equal to that file), and each semantic name reads its palette name as lantern.css's `light-dark()` does (`WORLD_COLOUR_SOURCES`): a World that sets only `parchment` moves the light `paper`, one that sets `paper` wins. Every pair of `WORLD_CONTRAST_PAIRS` must reach its `min` by the WCAG 2 ratio, else a problem at `theme.schemes.<set>`: `"<fg>" on "<bg>" is <ratio>:1, under <min>:1`. A set the World does not bring is not checked. The ratio is computed so both engines give the same digits: each channel's linear value is rounded once to millionths (`WORLD_LINEAR`, the 0.03928 threshold the token test uses), luminance is the integer 2126 R + 7152 G + 722 B, and the ratio is (hi + 0.05) / (lo + 0.05) in that scale, in hundredths rounded down, so a ratio of 4.4999996 reads 4.49 and fails. The cards are judged by the parchment pairs, not by a second resolution of the card scope. **Versions** as a ruleset's: the hash of the canonical parsed document (defaults applied); the same version and hash is a no-op, the same version with other content is refused (raise the version), a new version is stored beside the old. **Storage** (migration 0018): `world` (owner, slug unique per owner, `hidden_at`, `disabled_at`), `world_version` (version unique per World, hash, the document as jsonb, bytes) and `campaign.world_version_id` (nullable, restrict on delete). **REST**, owner-scoped beside the library's and behind its guards (CSRF, the signed-in and import limits, the body cap): `POST /api/worlds` (creators only), `GET /api/worlds` (the caller's own, not hidden), `GET /api/worlds/:id/export` (the newest version), `POST /api/worlds/:id/hide` (hidden; really deleted only when no room pins a version of it). At most 20 Worlds and 50 versions of one World per owner, hidden ones counted (a `ponytail:`). **Takedown:** `POST /api/admin/worlds/:id/disable { disabled }`, logged as `world.disable` / `world.enable` with the owner as target; a disabled World is not imported again, exported, pinned or served. **Pinning:** `campaign.setWorld { worldVersionId }` and `campaign.unsetWorld`, permission `campaign:ruleset`. The caller pins only a World they own (else `worldNotFound`), not taken down (`worldTakenDown`), not hidden (`worldDeleted`), and only in a room they run, as GM, or as the owner in a room without a GM (`worldRunnersOnly`): the rule a private pack follows (principle 4). Independent of the ruleset (design D4): nothing is checked against the pinned ruleset, the characters or the packs, and it changes at any time, mid-session too. Unsetting a room with no World is not refused. **Serving:** `GET /api/campaigns/:id/rules` gains `world`: `null`, or `{ versionId, slug, version, name, theme }` (the theme as stored), the same for every seat of the room, the table screen included, while it is served: not taken down, and its owner still runs the room (`actor.ServedWorld`, one SQL condition sharing its "runs the room" part with `actor.ServedPacks`). A World its owner hid while a room pins it is still served. Every seat's state carries `campaign.worldVersionId` (the pinned version, or null, whether served or not) so a client reads the rules again when it changes. | A World is look, not rules: it carries no values, so changing it needs none of a ruleset's upgrade checks, and it travels like a private pack because its owner may have drawn it from a book they hold. Integer arithmetic over one rounded table is the cheapest way to make two languages agree to the last digit at the floors; a refused World names the pair and the numbers, so the importer can fix it. |
| 66 | Harm that counts up, and mobs: `health.harm` | **The manifest:** `health` gains an optional `harm` block: `cite` (required unless the ruleset is homebrew), `wounds` (an entity-scoped resource declared `capped: false`, damage taken counting up, its max the limit), `shock` (optional, a second track like it), `mob` (optional: `members`, a capped entity-scoped resource, the members standing, and `start`, an int field on every actor type that carries it, how many it started with) and 1 to 10 `profiles`, the first whose `entities` hold the actor's type applying (a type in one profile at most): `shockless` (boolean, the actor never takes shock), `spent { when?, conditions? }` (past its shock limit: the boolean holds, or it holds any of the conditions), `felled { when, injuries?, condition?, healTo? }` (down; an int field counting injuries; a condition the hit that brings it down lays; a number healing while down stops at) and `destroyed { over, field }` (a number; a bool field). The three resources are distinct and none is a `health.tracks` resource; every expression compiles in the scope of each type of its profile, boolean or number as marked. A ruleset without the block validates, compiles and hashes as before. **The primitive `health.harm`**, params the block, both engines and vectors. **`hit { actorId, wounds?, shock? }`** (each an int from -100 to 100, at least one not zero; `encounter:run`; refused `noHarmHere` when no profile holds the type or the ruleset has no block, `neverShocked` for shock on a `shockless` actor, `badRequest` for shock where the block names no shock resource). Every expression is read on the actor before the hit, in this order: (a) on a spent actor a positive shock becomes wounds (`wounds += shock`, `shock = 0`), a negative one never; (b) a mob (the block has `mob` and the actor carries its members) loses `wounds` members, and one more when the shock as sent is over its shock resource's shown max, held to 0..the members' max; nothing else changes and step (a) does not apply; (c) one hit's wounds above `destroyed.over` set the field true and change nothing else; (d) shock becomes `max(0, current + shock)`, no ceiling; (e) on an actor down (`felled.when`), a positive wound adds 1 to `injuries` and leaves the wounds, a negative one sets them to `max(0, min(healTo, current + wounds))`; otherwise wounds become `max(0, current + wounds)` and, when `felled.when` read again with the new wounds (the rest of the scope as before the hit, derived values included: a `ponytail` until a ruleset's limit reads its own wounds) holds where it did not, `felled.condition` is laid unless held. **`muster { actorId, members }`** (0 to 500, as Auspex lets a GM set a Mob to none; `encounter:run`; `notAMob` without `mob` or on an actor that does not carry the members): the start field becomes `max(start, members)` and the members `members`; the api evaluates the members' bounds again with the new start, so the count reaches it in the same action. One hit or muster is one action in the log; Undo takes it back whole. **Laying a condition:** a delta may name a condition of the manifest (`{ actorId, applyCondition }`, `add` 1); the api applies it as `condition.add` does (its stacking rule) with no count and the expiry `manual`, and writes the row it makes, so the log and Undo see an ordinary condition row. **The op's state:** each actor carries its entity type and expression scope, a condition its id, a resource its shown max (`limit`) beside the ceiling. **The stepper:** `resource.capped.adjust` on `wounds`, `shock` or `mob.members` is refused (`hpThroughDamage`) for every seat; rests and other trusted writes still reach them. **A mob from a pack entry:** `actor.spawn` takes `mob` (2 to 500, with a count of 1, else `badRequest`): one actor named `<entry name> ×N`, its start field and members at N, otherwise a spawn like any other; `noMobsHere` when the block has no `mob` or the entry's type does not carry the members. **What a seat receives of a combatant:** `bar` for a `harm` ruleset is a mob's members over their max (none without a max), anyone else `clamp(1 - wounds / max, 0, 1)` (1 with no max); a ruleset with both a track and `harm` keeps the track's bar, and `hp` stays null without a track. `states` (`{ id, label }`, the label falling back to the id) are the ruleset's `health.states` that hold for the actor, in manifest order, for every ruleset; `members` (`{ value, max }`) a mob's count, null otherwise. The seat view is unchanged: the combat side receives every combatant whole; anyone else no hidden combatant and, at `bar`, no `hp`, the states and the count at `bar` and `full` alike. An actor outside a fight gains nothing. | Generalised from Auspex's `pcHit` and `combatant.adjust` (wrath_glory `packages/shared/src/state.ts`, `apps/api/src/encounter/encounter.actions.ts`), every number and condition left to the manifest, so the engine carries the mechanic and no book's figures. The GM enters a hit's final numbers, as in Auspex: resistance, attack dice and injury tables stay at the table. Where this differs from Auspex: a mob heals up to its members' max, which a ruleset declares as its start, rather than to the stored start itself; a mob's shock limit is its shock resource's max rather than a stat snapshot's; the condition a hit lays is a manifest condition, not a `prone` flag. |

| 69 | Codex entries nest | **Parent:** every entry kind may sit under an entry of its own kind (a place in a place, a faction in a faction, an adventure in an adventure); a scene's parent stays an adventure (`sceneNeedsAdventure`, unchanged). Migration 0019 replaces the scene-only check with one that keeps an entry from being its own parent; the kind, loop and depth checks run in the handler, inside the room's queue. **Delete:** an adventure that still has scenes is refused, as before. Any other entry with children is deleted and its children become roots, in their order, after the roots there are, as in Auspex (its foreign key sets a child's parent to null); grandchildren stay where they are, and the siblings the entry left close up. **Seat view:** unchanged, now for every kind: a seat receives `parentId` only when it may read the parent, else the entry reaches it as a root; nothing names the hidden parent. `position` orders siblings (same kind, same parent). **Web:** the GM's codex draws children under their parent, indented, each branch folds and starts open; the phone's notebook indents them and does not fold. | 70 | A picture on a codex entry | **Store:** `internal/image`, a `Store` (put, get, delete, a room's usage) whose one implementation keeps the bytes in Postgres (`image`, migration 0020); nothing above it knows. | 73 | A scene's prep is parked actions | **The document:** a scene (and only a scene) holds `prep` (`ScenePrep`, `packages/core-schema/src/prep.ts`; `codex_entry.prep`, migration 0023): five lists of lines, at most 40 each and 32 KB in all, a kind it does not know refused. With no fight running a roster line is not sent (START THE FIGHT beside it starts one and sends the lines, #75); a line `actor.spawn` cannot express (more than 50, several mobs at once; before #75 also a view other than `hidden` and more than 20) is shown and not sent; a spend the pool cannot pay is held back. **Status:** a scene is `planned`, `active`, `done` or `skipped` (new scenes `planned`), set by `codex.save`; one scene of a room is active (a partial unique index), and making one active leaves the one being played `done`. | 79 | An action's answer may carry notices | **The answer:** an accepted action's ack may carry `notices`, a list of `{ code, params, text? }` (`Notice`, `packages/core-schema/src/actions.ts`; Go `coreschema.Notice`, held equal by the core vectors through `NOTICE_PARAMS`). Only the seat that sent the action receives them, in its ack; they are not in any snapshot and not logged beyond what the action logs. The web keeps the last answer's notices (`useTable().notices`) and shows them as one slip (`ActionNotices`) under the GM's tabs and on the phone beside the spend notices, `role="status"` with `aria-live="polite"`, gone when the next action is sent or on DISMISS; a code is worded by the `actionNotices` messages, a `text` shown as it arrives. **Platform notice:** `poolFull { pool, lost }`, for any game, when a shared pool cannot take the whole of a positive change because it is at its max: a hand adjust (`resource.capped adjustPool`, whose op proposes only what fits) and any write through the delta pipeline that the SQL clamp cut short. A change to an actor's own resource that is clamped says nothing. **The manifest:** an optional top-level `notices`, at most 50 rules `{ id, when, entities?, if, text, cite? }`: `when` is one of the reset events (`sessionStart`, `sessionEnd`, `encounterEnd`, `shortRest`, `longRest`) or `crossed`; `entities` actor types (absent: every actor type), `if` a boolean in the actor's scope (fields, `derived`, `res`, `party`, `campaign`), `text` 1 to 120 characters where `{name}` stands for the actor's name; ids unique, `cite` needed outside homebrew.
