The compiled rule carries, by actor type, the visibility of what `if` reads, like a Test's numbers. Both engines and the manifest vectors. **Raising, on the server only, around one action (`table.ApplyAction`):** a rule whose `when` is the event the action runs (`session.start`, `session.end`, `encounter.end`, `actor.rest`, whose `actorId` narrows it to one actor) is raised for every actor of its types that meets `if` after the action: a level, told at every such event. A `crossed` rule is raised for an actor that did not meet `if` before the action and does after, whatever the action (an actor the action created crossed nothing). Each rule and actor once per action; an actor whose values cannot be evaluated meets nothing. A rule is told to the sender only if it may read what `if` reads on that actor: the GM side of the actor's kind, or a `public`/`party` visibility, or `owner` on a character it holds. | 81 | Reminders, a setup note, a side barred at once, a condition armed from the next turn, immunity, stacks on the chip, a rest refused in a state | **Reminders:** a top-level `reminders` list of `{ id, at: "turnStart" \| "turnEnd" \| "roundEnd", text (1 to 120, the ruleset's own words), cite, if? }` (a cite outside homebrew; ids unique). `if` is a boolean in the scope of an actor's type that also reads `round`, `held` and `barred`. A condition may carry `at` and `text` (both or neither, `a reminder needs both at and text`): its line holds for whoever holds the condition. The server works out the lines due now: while someone acts, the `turnStart` and `turnEnd` lines asked of that combatant; while the round ends after this turn or has ended (a sides order: begun and nobody pending once whoever acts is done; a ranked or seated order: the last of the order acts), the `roundEnd` lines asked of every combatant, each line once with the combatants it holds for. Declared reminders come first in manifest order, then condition lines; a reminder without `if` always shows and names nobody; a value its `if` cannot read holds for nobody. The lines travel in the encounter as `reminders`, to the combat side only (a line may name a hidden combatant); nothing changes a number. **Setup note:** `initiative.setup.note` (1 to 200), shown to the GM while the fight is not begun. **A whole side:** the sides order's `bar` takes `{ side, barred }` in place of `{ id, barred }` (exactly one); barring bars every member of that side in the order that is not done and not acting, freeing frees all of them, one action; an unknown side is `badInput`. **Armed from the next turn:** a condition with `duration: "ownTurnEnd"` may declare `from: "nextOwnTurn"` (only with that duration); `condition.add` without a typed `expiresAfter`, on the actor whose turn it is now, starts its count at 2, so it survives that turn's end and goes at the next one's; added at any other time it starts uncounted, one tick, as before (#14). A typed count wins. Undo of the add removes the row; Undo of a turn end gives its tick back (#14's machinery). **Immunity:** a condition may declare `immune`, a boolean in the actor's scope; `condition.add` on an actor it holds for is refused, `conditionImmune` naming the actor and the condition's label. A value it cannot read is not immune. **Stacks:** a `stacking: "stack"` condition already counted stacks up to `maxStacks` (100 without one) on its one row; the count now reaches every seat that sees the condition on the combatant (`stacks`, when above 1), drawn as "Label (n)" on the fight's chips. The sheet's own chip is not changed here. **Rests:** a top-level `rests` list of `{ event, refusedIn: [health state ids], cite }`, one per event; `actor.rest` naming one actor in such a state is refused, `restRefused` naming it and the state's label; resting everyone, and the session boundaries' rests, leave such actors out (shared pools still reset), and `actor.rest`'s answer names them in a `restSkipped` notice (#79), params `names` (in name order, joined with ", ") and `count`, only when someone was left out; the Room tab shows it under the rest buttons. | Auspex reminds and never automates a Condition's numbers (docs/parity rule 1): its turn-start and turn-end lines are a table keyed by Condition (`packages/shared/src/state.ts:778-786`), its round-end lines are the half-slain Mob, held actions lost and ambushes ending (`:801-818`), all on the GM's tracker only. Here the words are the ruleset's, and `held`, `barred` and `round` in scope let a ruleset say Auspex's three round-end lines itself. Auspex's "timed Condition ends this turn" needs no declaration: the combat side already has each condition's `left` and `expiresOn`. Auspex arms a timed Condition at the holder's turn start and removes only what was armed (`encounter.actions.ts:289-306, 419-423`); a count of 2 for one added during the holder's own turn is the same outcome on #14's counted rows. Auspex marks a whole side ambushed in Rounds 0 and 1 only, skipping who has acted (`:359-379`), the same as `barred.throughRound` here. Monstrous Creatures refuse Fear, Pinned and Staggered (`threat.ts:213`): an expression on the actor says that for any game. Respite is refused while Dying, Dead or Lost (`character.actions.ts:763-773`), and Auspex's party sheet sends one per Agent and lists the refusals; one action that skips and names them is the same at the table. Not expressible: Auspex's sustain lines (a count of powers sustained, an on-hit line with the damage typed) need a sustained-power record and a hit amount, which no layer has yet. |
| 82 | TURN BACK: the fight's last turn step, with everything since | **The action:** `event.turnBack` (permission `encounter:run`, as `event.undo`) takes back, in one transaction, every live logged action of the running fight back to and including its newest **turn step**: an action whose logged turn writes changed who acts (`currentId`) or the round. Only the running session's log counts, from the moment the active encounter began (its `created_at`); actions are taken back newest first, each by the same function `event.undo` uses for one action (`campaign/undo.go` `undoAction`, with its author check for a room without a GM), so what a step cost (a paid out-of-turn start, a lifted bar: the pool delta in the same action, #60) comes back with it. A refusal of any one refuses the whole step, `turnBackRefused` ("action {n} of {count}, counting back") carrying the inner refusal's text, and nothing is written; with no step in the log, `nothingToTurnBack`. No redo of a whole step: Undo after a TURN BACK takes back the next live action, never the step; each action a step took back can be redone by line in HISTORY (house decision #132). Undo stays beside it, one action a press. **What it would take** travels in the encounter as `turnBack: { kind, name?, round, later }`: `turnStart` (someone began acting; `name` is theirs), `turnEnd` (whoever acted stopped; `name` is theirs) or `round` (only the round moved; no name); `round` is the round of the step (the one it began, for one that moved the round); `later` is how many later actions go with it. The combat side's only, absent for every other seat and when there is nothing to take back (both seat views, the reference test, the table vectors). **Tracker:** a button beside Undo, "Turn back: Wren's turn (and 3 more)", disabled without `turnBack`, asking a second tap when it would take back more than five actions. | Auspex's `turn.back` (wrath_glory `apps/api/src/encounter/encounter.actions.ts:184-270`, rules-flow §11.170) keeps a snapshot before each turn-flow action and restores it: turn marks, Round, Wounds, Shock, Conditions, Mob counts, Traumatic Injuries and Annihilation, and refunds a Seize's or AWARE's Glory or Ruin. Here the session log already holds every write with its before and after, so the step is the log's actions since the boundary undone through the one undo path, instead of a second store. Different: Auspex keeps the last 30 steps in the encounter row across sessions; here the depth is the running session's log for this fight, so a fight carried into a new session turns back only to that session's first step. Auspex restores the snapshot whole and keeps what was added since (a combatant added stays, damage typed since goes back); here every logged action since goes back, whatever it was: Conditions go back in both (Auspex's snapshot holds them), and the difference is what Auspex's snapshot does not hold, a pool spent, an accepted spend's cost and any other logged write, which Auspex does not refund; what is not logged (adding or removing a combatant, a visibility or order change) stays as in Auspex. Auspex's step kinds (a hold, an interrupt, a mark) are steps of their own there; here a hold or an interrupt that ends or starts a turn is a step, and a mark that changes neither who acts nor the round goes back with the step before it. Where a write since was changed outside the log, Auspex overwrites it; here the step is refused, as Undo is. Each of these was put to the owner in #223: all kept, and BEGIN ROUND 1 made a step as in Auspex (house decision #130). |
| 83 | A layout row may name a widget; a layout has targets beyond the sheet | **The manifest** (ADR 0010, decision 3): a layout keeps its sheet `sections` by entity type (or the shared `sheet` key); a section's `show` lists rows, each a value path as before or a widget row `{ widget, variant?, label?, bind: { <slot>: <path> or [<paths>] }, steps? }`. A layout also takes `phoneTurn`, `trackerRow` and `screen` (`LAYOUT_TARGETS`), each a list of the same rows, up to 100; they are validated now and drawn by later layers, and a target left out keeps today's fixed screen. **The widgets** are a closed list, `WIDGETS` in `packages/rules-engine/src/widgets.ts` (each with its slots, variants and an importer's description; Go twin `engine.Widgets`, held equal by the vectors), named for what they draw: `strip` (slots `title` one, `values` list: a bar, then short values under their labels); `grid` (`ratings` list, `bonus` and `total` lists as long as it, `link` likewise; variant `columns`, one column per rating with a rating, a bonus and a total row, or `pairs`, two columns of rows with the linked value's label, the rating and the total in a badge, hollow at a rating of 0; `bonus` is `columns` only, `link` `pairs` only); `frames` (`values` list: framed numbers with their label and cite, a derived one opening its breakdown); `sum` (`parts` list, `total` one: `a + b = total`, the total highlighted); `track` (`value` one, `max` one optional: the counter as the sheet draws it, a resource with its steppers and its ask to spend, and a box per point of the limit, at most 40); `pips` (`value` one, and the row's `steps`, 1 to 12 of `{ label, size }` with a size of 1 to 20: pips in rows, the step the count has reached marked by weight and a sign); `cards` (no slots; variant `weapons`, `armour`, `gear` or `powers`: datacards from the saved build's sheet, as the ledger lines read it); `folds` (no slots; variant `both`, `talents` or `abilities`: named entries folded over the importer's summary, with their cite); `notes` (`fields` list of text fields: each folded under its label, one WRITE or EDIT for a seat the field lets write, saved by `field.set`). A variant left out is the widget's first. **At import** every widget row is checked against the entity types its layout is for (its own type, or for `sheet` every type without a layout of its own): an unknown widget, variant or slot, a missing required slot, a slot a variant does not read, one path where a list is wanted or the other way, lists of different lengths, `steps` on a widget that takes none or missing on `pips`, `cards` or `folds` in a ruleset with no `build`, a path that names nothing (`unknown value`), not a number where one is wanted, or not a text field for `notes`: each refused at its path (`layout.<key>.sections[i].show[j].bind.<slot>[k]`, `layout.<key>.trackerRow[j]...`). A path resolves as the sheet reads it: `derived.x`, `res.x` (an entity resource that type has), else a field, a derived value, a resource. Plain path rows stay unchecked, as they were. A ruleset without widget rows validates, compiles and hashes as before (sample-d20 0.1.6, pinned); sample-d20 0.1.7 lays out its character with seven widgets and three new values (`mightMod`, `graceMod`, `gritMod`, a `strain` field), and the rulesets built on it before keep 0.1.5. **The seat view is unchanged.** A widget draws only values the seat received: a slot it did not receive is absent, a grid column is drawn only when every row received it, a sum missing a part shows its total alone, and a widget with nothing left is not drawn, so no frame or label stands for a hidden value. `cards` and `folds` replace the ledger lines of their kinds; the rest still follow the layout as before. A sheet of four sections or more gets a strip of links to them on a phone. **Still open in ADR 0010, decision 3:** drawing `phoneTurn` (the phone on the seat's turn), `trackerRow` (a combatant's row on the tracker) and `screen` (the TV); which widgets those need beyond these (a dice-pool step, a target pick); a weapon's dice pool and a melee mark on `cards` (the saved sheet does not carry them); words for the grid's row heads beyond the platform's own; an enum field's words in a `strip`. | Auspex's sheet is one fixed component per box, which is how one game shaped its app. A closed list of widgets bound by path lets any ruleset lay its sheet out box by box while the platform keeps the drawing, the Theme and the seat view (principle 6, ADR 0010 rule 5). Checking each bound path at import catches a layout that would silently draw nothing. Plain rows stay unchecked so every imported ruleset still imports. `steps` is a widget's own setting, not a value, so it sits on the row. |
| 84 | Spends: a cost and the ops it buys, asked for or done in one tap (`spends`, `requests.max`, `health.harm` `convert`) | **The manifest:** an optional `spends` list (at most 50), each `{ id, label, cite?, entities?, cost, inputs?, if?, run?, clearOn? }`. `cost` is 0 to 5 entries `{ pool \| resource, amount, byRun? }`: a shared pool or an entity resource of the asking actor (exactly one; `a cost names a pool or a resource`, `must be a shared pool`, `must be an entity-scoped resource`), `amount` an integer expression; an empty list is a free spend. `byRun: true` marks a cost a `run` op takes itself (`seize` and `lift` charge their own pool): it is shown, checked at send and counted for who sees the ask, but `spend.do` does not take it, so it is never paid twice. `inputs` (at most 3) are `{ label, min, max }`, `max` an integer expression, read as `ctx.<name>`; `self` and `fight` are reserved. `if` is a boolean expression. `run` (at most 5) is `{ primitive, op, input }`, the primitive one the ruleset declares (`resource.capped`, `conditions.expire`, `rest`, `initiative.<its strategy>`, `health.tracks` with tracks, `health.harm` with the block; `is not declared by this ruleset`), the op one it has (`has no op`), every input key one of that op's (`is not an input`); a string input is an expression, a number or boolean is taken as it is. Every expression reads the asking actor's scope plus `ctx`: the inputs, `ctx.self` (the actor's id) and `ctx.fight` (`on`, `in`, `round`, `barred`: a fight runs, the actor is in it, its round, the actor is barred this round). `clearOn` lists `sessionStart`, `sessionEnd`, `encounterEnd` once each. A spend is a rule, so it needs a cite unless the ruleset is homebrew. `requests: { max }` (1 to 20) caps pending requests per character (house decision #169), every form; absent, the platform's five. `health.harm` profiles take `convert: true`. A ruleset without these validates, compiles and hashes as before. **Reading one** (`planSpend`, both engines, vectors): the spend (`noSuchSpend` when unknown or not for the actor's type), the inputs (`badInput`: missing, unknown, not a whole number, out of `min`..`max`), `if` (`spendNotNow`), then each cost read (a cost that reads 0 is dropped, below 0 is `badInput`) and checked against the pool or resource as it stands, costs on one target summed (`cannotPay`), then each op's input read. **`convert { actorId, amount }`** (1 to 100, `encounter:run`): `min(amount, wounds)` wounds become shock; refused `noConvertHere` for a profile without `convert`, a block without shock or an actor without it, `neverShocked` for a `shockless` actor, `wrongState` when the actor is spent (as a hit reads it) or has no wounds. It lays and lifts no condition. **`spend.do { spendId, actorId, inputs? }`** (`request:resolve`, and each op's own permission): plans the spend, takes every cost not `byRun` as a clamped `add` (a pool cost as the op's own charge, not a hand edit), runs each op as `rule.op` does (an `initiative.sides` op with its expiries), and writes it all as one logged action, so one Undo takes back the costs, the op and a paid out-of-turn start together (#60 holds). Every op reads the room as it stood before the spend. Any refusal writes nothing. **`request.send`** takes a resource and an amount (#43, unchanged) or a `spendId` and `inputs`; both or neither is `requestKind`. A spend-ask needs a running session and a seat that holds the actor, is planned as at `spend.do` and refused the same way, then: one pending per spend and actor (`spendAsked`), at most `requests.max` per character (`requestLimit`, house decision #169). Without a GM it is done at once as the table. Stored as `spend_id` and `inputs` with no resource or amount (migration 0026). **Approving** is `spend.do` under the approver's seat in the same transaction as marking it answered; a refusal leaves it pending with the reason, to be denied. **Clearing:** when a fight ends, the pending asks of every spend whose `clearOn` lists `encounterEnd` are cancelled (the answer a cancel gives); every pending request is already cancelled when a session ends (#43), and none can wait across a session start, so those two events need nothing more. **Seats:** the answering side and the asker, as #43; an ask for a spend that costs at least one pool also reaches every seat that receives each pool it costs (a party pool: the party and the screen); one that costs no pool only the asker and that side. Only the asker cancels; that side denies. | Auspex's spend kinds (wrath_glory `packages/shared/src/spend.ts`, `apps/api/src/spend/spend.actions.ts`, `spend.clear.ts`) each hard-code a cost and an effect; here they are data any game declares. Kept from #43: the generic ask of an amount of the asker's own resource, its refusals, the cap of five and the answers' notices. What a ruleset cannot say: Auspex's cap of one Determination ask per hit sheet, opened by Wounds rising (the phone's Fight tab is a later layer); asks cleared at round 2's start or when a character stops being ambushed (now house decision #169: `roundEnd` in `clearOn`, and an ask whose `if` stops holding); and limits per character (now house decision #169: `requests.max` counts per character, as Auspex counts 3 per Agent). Each ask is checked against the whole pool, at send and at approval, as Auspex does (its rules-flow §11.122: "each request is checked against the whole pool, not what the other pending ones would leave"). |
| 85 | Sessions: ending without an award, deleting the last, the GM's journal, the next session as a file | **`session.abandon`** (`{ recap? }`, `""` when absent; `session:run`, as `session.end`) ends the running session with no award step and none of `session.end`'s resets or cancellations: the session is marked `abandoned` (`session.abandoned`, migration 0027), keeps the recap sent, and the action is logged in that session as a `session.abandon` event (`{ sessionId }`). With no session running it is refused `noSessionRunning`. **`session.delete`** (`{ sessionId }`; `session:run`) deletes the room's latest session (by number) once it has ended: an older one is refused `notLatestSession`, the running one `endSessionFirst`, an id that is not this room's `sessionNotFound`. What it awarded stays with the characters. Its rows in the session log stay in the room's log with no session (the foreign key's `ON DELETE SET NULL`), as do its fights, Test calls and results; its recap leaves the recaps. The next session takes its number again. The answer carries the platform notice `sessionDeleted { number }`. **`session.edit`** refuses a recap on a running session (`recapAtSessionEnd`); the title and notes may be edited at any time, and only the fields sent are written (as before). `session.start` already takes a title. **The journal:** the live state carries `journal`, every session of the room newest first, each `{ id, number, title, startedAt, endedAt, recap, notes, abandoned }` (`JournalSession`). It reaches the seats the Test definitions reach (the session side, and every player of a room without a GM, who runs it together); the notes only the session side, so in a room without a GM nobody but an admin reads them, as with the running session's notes. Any other seat gets `[]`, and keeps `session` and the recaps as before. **Starting a session** clears the next-session date and keeps its place (as before), and closes every Test call still open (`status = 'closed'`, not revealed). Spend requests and whispers are cleared at a session's start by the layers that build them. **`GET /api/campaigns/:id/next.ics`**: any seat of the room (a member by device or a guest token); no seat is `401 unauthorized`; no date set is `404 noNextSession`. One `VEVENT` written by hand (RFC 5545: CRLF, escaped text, lines folded at 75 octets), starting at the stored UTC time and lasting a fixed 3 hours, `SUMMARY` "<room> · Session <next number>", `LOCATION` the place when one is set, `UID` "<room id>-<unix start>@inconsistable"; `private, no-cache`. Under the signed-in REST limit, like the room's other reads. | Auspex has both actions (`apps/api/src/campaign/campaign.actions.ts:72-79,221-237`): abandon "for a session that was never played or got left open: no XP, no Rank notices, pools untouched", delete "only the latest, so session.start's numbering stays unbroken, and only once it has ended", the XP staying and said so in a notice. Here delete names the session it means, so a tablet showing an old list cannot delete another; abandon takes a recap because the platform's end does (the brief's choice). Auspex has no session log; deleting the platform's log rows would lose what the fights and Tests recorded, and the foreign keys already keep them unattached. The recap of a running session waits for its end (Auspex §11.153). A Test called last session is over (§11.143); the platform keeps the call's record, closed, rather than deleting it. The calendar file is Auspex's (`packages/shared/src/calendar.ts`, `campaign.controller.ts:297-310`, issue #197): any seat, 404 until a date is set, 3 hours since the app keeps no session length. |
| 86 | Beginning a scene and taking it back; an exit begins the next; an adventure deleted with its scenes; when an entry was revealed | **`scene.begin`** (`{ sceneId }`; `codex:write`, as making a scene active with `codex.save`): an id that is not this room's entry is `entryNotFound`, one that is not a scene `notAScene`. The scene being played (status `active`, another one) goes to `done`, the named scene becomes `active` (whatever it was: planned, done, skipped, or already active), then each of its prepared reveal lines, in order, is sent as the `codex.reveal` a tap sends (`visibility: party`, `audienceMode: all`): it asks `codex:reveal`, is logged as a `codex.reveal` event in the running session as a tap's is, and a refused line (its entry gone: `entryNotFound`; no `codex:reveal`) is skipped and named in the answer as the notice `revealSkipped { line, reason }` (the line's label, else its id; the refusal's code), without failing the begin. The answer's id is the begin's, and it carries the notice `sceneBegun { title, revealed }` (the reveal lines sent). The other kinds of line still wait for a tap. The begin is a row of `scene_begin` (migration 0028): the scene, the scene it replaced (null when none; set null if that scene is later deleted), the begun scene's status before, and the entries the begin itself revealed: those that were sealed (`visibility = 'gm'`) before their line was sent. **`scene.undoBegin`** (`{ beginId }`; `codex:write`): only the room's latest begin (`notLatestBegin` otherwise, an unknown or taken-back id included) and only while its scene is still `active` (`sceneNotActive`). The begun scene goes back to the status it had, the scene it replaced is active again (none, if there was none or it is gone), and the entries the begin revealed are sealed again (`visibility: gm`, all, no readers, no time; each logged as a `codex.reveal` to `gm`); an entry revealed before the begin stays revealed as the begin left it. The begin's row is then gone, so the begin before it, when its scene is active again, is the latest and can be taken back in turn. It is its own action, not part of `event.undo`. **An exit line** is `scene.begin` of the scene it names (it amends #73, where an exit only opened its scene); an exit that names no scene, or one that is gone, stays broken and not sendable. The GM's RUN view sends `scene.begin` from PLAY THIS SCENE and from an exit, opens the begun scene, and offers TAKE BACK for the room's latest begin while that scene is active, read from the codex payload (#105), so it survives a reload and shows on every GM tablet. **`codex.delete`** takes `withScenes`: an adventure with scenes is refused `adventureHasScenes` without it, as before, and with it each scene is deleted as `codex.delete` deletes one, then the adventure. What pointed at a deleted scene stays where it is, unlinked (an exit line, an entry field), as Auspex §11.163 has it; its links and begins go with it. The GM's delete asks for the adventure's title typed first, as deleting a room does. **When an entry was revealed:** `codex_entry.revealed_at` (migration 0028), `revealedAt` on `CodexEntry` (an ISO time, or null), set to now by a `codex.reveal` that gives the entry a `party` or `public` visibility (to everyone or to listed readers, again on each such reveal), cleared by one to `gm` and by a take-back; an entry made the party's as it is made (a room without a GM) takes its creation time. It travels with the entry, so a seat that cannot read the entry receives nothing of it. Entries revealed before migration 0028 have no time. The phone's notebook lists handouts newest revealed first, one with no time last. **The room document:** a codex entry takes an optional `revealedAt` (an ISO date-time with offset). Import keeps it on an entry the document reveals, ignores it on a sealed one, and an entry revealed without one takes the import's time. | ADR 0012 decision 3, amended the same day: Auspex's BEGIN reveals the scene's ON ENTRY picks in one tap and shows SCENE BEGUN (`apps/api/src/codex/codex.actions.ts:94-135`, rules-flow §11.157), and its UNDO restores the scene left, puts the begun one back to the status it had, and seals what the begin revealed (`apps/web/lib/scene-reveal/scene-reveal.ts` `undoBegin`, which records only the entries that were not revealed before). Auspex keeps that record in the GM's browser; here it is a row, since the server decides and checks "latest" and "still active". It is a table rather than the session log, which is written only while a session runs. WHERE NEXT ends the scene and begins the next (`W/gm/scene/components/where-next/where-next.tsx`). Deleting an adventure takes its scenes, leaving what pointed at them unlinked (§11.163). The inbox orders by reveal time (`apps/web/components/player/player-app/components/intel/utils.ts:23`). An entry revealed to listed readers and then to everyone by a begin stays revealed to everyone when the begin is taken back: Auspex's reveal is one flag, and a begin's record names only what it took from sealed. |
| 87 | Lookups: a typed result reads a line whose effects apply through the ordinary operations; I ROLLED on any table | **The manifest:** a `tables` entry with a `die` may be a lookup; no parallel block. On the table: `typedOnly` (bool: never rolled by the server; `table.roll` without a `result` is refused `tableTypedOnly`), `inputs` (1 to 6 of `{ id, label, type: "bool" \| "int", min?, max?, cite? }`, read as `ctx.<id>`: a switch, or a stepper within its bounds; a switch takes no bounds), `modifier` (an integer expression over the inputs and the target, added to the typed result before a line is read), `entities` (the actor types it applies to; all of them when absent). On a line: `range: [from]` (the last line only: open-ended, it reads every total from `from` up), `apply` (1 to 8 effects), `note` (the importer's own words, at most 200 characters), `cite`, `next` (a table id: the GM goes on to that table with the same target; nothing chains by itself). An effect is `{ kind: "harm", wounds?, shock? }` (a hit through `health.harm`'s `hit` op), `{ kind: "condition", condition }` (as `condition.add` writes it), `{ kind: "resource", resource, by }` (a resource the target carries, by a clamped delta) or `{ kind: "pool", pool, by }` (a shared pool, by a clamped delta); each may carry `when` (a boolean over the inputs and the target) and `times` (an integer, 0 to 20, default 1: the effect again for each, which is how a stepper's points each add one effect, and `when: "ctx.x >= 1"` is how a first point adds one). **At import** a lookup needs a die and is never dealt (`owner`); a hit needs `health.harm` and wounds or shock (shock only where the ruleset has it); a resource effect names an entity resource that no hit moves (a track's, the wounds, the shock, a mob's members are refused: a hit is how they move); a pool effect names a shared pool; a condition, an actor type and a `next` table must exist; every expression compiles over the target's type with the inputs as `ctx`. **Reading a total:** the line whose range holds it; otherwise the line with the highest start below it, so a total past the top reads the top line (Auspex reads a Perils total above its table at the top row and caps the Mutation roll at its top row, rules-flow §11.155, `mutation.ts:83-86`), and one under every line reads the lowest. A typed result must be one the die can show (d66: 11 to 66, units 1 to 6) or it is refused `tableResultOut`. **The tiling rule** of house decision #49 still holds for the die; it is relaxed for the open-ended last line, and, on a table with a `modifier`, for lines above the die, which must tile among themselves in the die's shape (a d66 line above 66 still ends in 1 to 6), since a modified total reaches them (Auspex's Perils table prints rows to 106). **Applying** is `table.apply { tableId, result, actorId, inputs }`, permission `encounter:run`: the server reads the line (never the client's), works out each effect on the target as it stood before the apply (the tablet's preview reads the same), and runs the steps in the line's order, each reading the target as the steps before left it, all logged under one action, so one Undo or TURN BACK takes the whole line back. Any refusal refuses the whole line, as that operation would refuse it; a condition the target is immune to (#81) is left out and the answer carries a `conditionSkipped` notice. The answer carries `roll: { entryId, value, total }`. A table without effects is refused `tableNoApply`; a target outside `entities`, `tableNotFor`; a bad input, `tableInput`; a table whose modifier reads a target, without one, `tableNeedsTarget`. **I ROLLED:** `table.roll` takes an optional `result` on any table with a die, and `actorId` and `inputs` for a table with a modifier; the roll is logged as before, with the total when it differs. **The screen:** the GM's tables panel keeps one target picker (the fight's combatants first), a lookup's card has the number entry (and ROLL, which fills it in, unless typed-only), its switches and steppers, the line read for that target with its effects in the platform's words, its note and cite, APPLY, and a button for the `next` table. Phones and the table screen get nothing new. | Auspex's Critical Hit and Perils lookups are typed-only book rows applied through ordinary actions, and its Mutation tables are book tables with ROLL and I ROLLED (rules-flow §11.125, §11.155, §11.156). As data, the row is a name, numbers and a cite, its effects the structure the platform words itself, and the ordinary operations keep the clamps, the log and the refusals they already have. One action instead of Auspex's several sends means a refusal midway leaves nothing half-applied, and Undo takes the line back whole where Auspex left conditions and Corruption without an undo. Effects are worked out on the state before the apply, as Auspex plans them, so the preview is what lands. Since #226: a hit's shock its target never takes is left out with a notice (#163), a die the GM types is an input with `dice` (#164), `has(res.x)` asks for a resource a type may lack (#166), and the preview names a granted record. |
| 88 | Records an actor gains in play, with effects on its numbers and a name others do not see | **Where a record lives:** in a `list` field of the actor that holds pack entry keys, not in a table of its own. A ruleset declares `held: { <kind>: { label, entry, field, entities, unseen?, max?, cite } }` (at most 10 kinds): `entry` the entity type of the pack entries it holds (not an actor type), `field` a `list` field every actor type of `entities` declares, `unseen` the word seats other than the holder's and the GM side's read in the name's place (its field is then `owner` or `gm`, else refused), `max` 1 to 50 (default 20). A value row keyed by `actor_id` is what design §5.4 asks for; the list field is one already, so a grant and a removal are `field.set`'s own write through the pipeline (logged, undone by Undo and TURN BACK, refused as a field write is), the room document carries it with the actor's other fields, and `count(<field>)` already reads it. **Its numbers:** each record's served entry adds its `effects` exactly as a chosen entry's do (house decision #61): the same `ActiveEntry` path into derived values and base fields, sorted with the conditions' by `(priority, source id)`; a key the room is not served adds nothing and is not listed. A pack entry of a type a held kind holds may carry effects, checked at pack import against the kind's actor types, as a `choose` step's are. **Granting:** a lookup line's effect `{ kind: "grant", held, entry }` (entry a pack key) adds the key at the end of the list when the line is applied (#87); nothing is granted by reading a table or by `next`. Refused: an actor type the kind is not for (`heldNotFor`), an entry the room is not served (`heldNoEntry`), one the actor holds already (`heldAlready`, worded "roll again on this table", as the source app asks a reroll on the same table, `mutation.ts`), a kind at its max (`heldFull`). The step carries `has` so the tablet says so before APPLY. In a lookup's expressions a held kind's unset field reads as an empty list, so `count(growths)` is 0 for an actor that holds none (the source app's `+10 per mutation held`, ruling §11.136, is `modifier: "count(<field>) * 10"`, and it applies again on every table a chain reaches). **Chains:** the source app's severity roll, its forward rows and a row's own sub-table are tables the GM steps through with a line's `next`; the tables panel keeps the target and the trail of steps, offers ROLL and I ROLLED at each (book tables, not typed-only), and ends on ADD, the line that grants. A sub-table's rows grant the combined entry the pack names. **Removing:** `held.remove { actorId, held, entry }`, permission `character:edit:any` and the field's `editableBy` (the GM side by default); refused `noHeldKind`, `heldNotFor`, `heldNotHeld`. **What a seat receives:** the snapshot's actor carries `held: [{ kind, key, name, cite, effects: [{ path, mode, value, phase }] }]`; for a seat that neither holds the actor nor is on the GM side of its kind, a kind with `unseen` sends `{ kind, key: null, name: <unseen>, cite: null, effects }`, the same effects and the same derived numbers. **The sheet:** a layout row `{ widget: "held" }` lists the actor's records kind by kind: the name, each effect in words (a whole `add` as a signed number and the label of what it changes, anything else as the importer wrote it) and the cite, with CUT OFF for the GM side; refused at import on a layout whose types hold no kind. **The room document** carries the keys in the actor's `fields`; an import refuses at `actors[i].fields.<field>` a key the room is not served, a key twice, or more than `max`. sample-harm 0.1.3 adds a hero's growths, the Omen, the Lesser and Greater Growths and a hero layout; without them it hashes as 0.1.2. **Not ported:** since #226 a record that brings others with it is the kind's `includes` (house decision #165). A record's weapons and gear, and the bonus dice its text describes, stay the pack entry's summary. | The source app snapshots a Mutation onto the sheet and sums its modifiers on read; a key in a list field with the entry's effects applied on read is the same, keeps the platform's one modifier engine, and needs no new table, write path or undo. Hiding the name and showing the numbers is the source app's "Unseen" (`state.ts` 694-703). |
| 89 | A codex field holds tags or typed rows; a scene picks rows with a switch; a kind's values raise reminders | **`tags`** (a codex field type, with `label`, `visibility` `gm` unless it says `party`, `cite?`): `maxItems` 1 to 30 (ceiling 30), `entities` (1 to 10 entity types whose served entries' **names** are offered), `options` (1 to 200 ids, worded by `labels`, 1 to 60 characters each), `writeIn` (default false). Neither `entities`, `options` nor `writeIn` is `a tags field needs entities, options or writeIn`; an unknown entity `unknown entity`, a label key not an option `"<k>" is not a value`. The value is a list of words: at most the cap (`more than N words`), none blank after trimming (`a word is empty`), none over 60 characters, none twice (exact spelling; `a word is there twice`). Without `writeIn`, `codex.save` refuses a word that is neither an option id nor the name of an entry of those types the room's packs serve to the field's readers (`badEntryField`, `[i]: "w" is not one of the suggestions`). **`rows`**: `columns` (1 to 8, `{ id, label (1 to 60), type }`, ids unique) typed `int` (`min`, `max`), `text` (`maxLength` 1 to 500, ceiling 500), `enum` (`values`, `labels`, `notes`, `tones` as every enum), `bool`, `ref` (`entity`) or `test`; `maxRows` 1 to 30 (ceiling 30); `from` (1 to 5 `{ entity, name?, values? }`: `name` the text column that takes the entry's name, `values` a column id to the entity field whose value it takes; refused: an unknown or repeated entity, a `name` that is not a text column, a key that is not a column, a field the entity does not declare, a field that does not fit its column: another type, an enum value the column lacks, a ref to another entity); `pick` (`{ label }`, 1 to 40: a scene may pick this field's rows and the switch is called that). A row is `{ id (1 to 40, unique in the field), from?: { entity, key, cite? }, values: { <column>: value } }`. **A row filled from a pack entry follows it live (#230, as Auspex reads a book hazard from its catalogue):** FILL A ROW FROM stores the entry's `from` (key and cite) and no cells; each seat reads the row as the entry's current cells (its name in the `name` column, each `values` field that fits its column) with the row's stored cells laid over them, and the entry's cite (`followRow`, `entryCells` in `field-value.ts`, in the browser, no Go twin: the server stores and sends the row as it is, so the Seat view, the room document and the codex shape do not change). The entry is the one this seat's rules read serves (`SourceEntry`: the page's own entries, else the seat-scoped rules read of its key), so a pack update shows in the row and an entry gone (detached, taken down) or not served to that seat (a `gm` entry to a player, a `private` pack outside its owner's rooms) leaves the GM's cells alone, named by the entry's key: never content the seat may not read. The editor's inputs show the followed cells; a cell the GM sets is stored, one set back to the entry's value or cleared is not, and follows again. A row stored before #230 holds every cell it copied: they read as the GM's, so it shows as before, and each cell follows once the GM clears it or sets it to the entry's value (nothing is rewritten in place); a `test` cell is `{ testId, inputs }`, the two things a prepared Test line holds, inputs numbers, words (100) or yes/no. Refusals name the row and column: `[1].values.threat: above the maximum of 20`, `[0].from.entity: not a type this field is filled from`, `[0].id: "d1" is there twice`, `more than 12 rows`. `codex.save` also refuses a `from` key or a `ref` cell that no served entry of its type answers for the field's readers (`no such entry in this room's packs`) and a Test the ruleset does not have (`no such Test in this room's ruleset`). `codex.save`'s `fields` and the room document's entry `fields` take a list of row objects (at most 30) beside the field values they took. **Reminders:** a kind may list up to 20 `{ id, if, text (1 to 120), cite? }`, ids unique; `if` is a boolean over `fields.<id>` (int, bool, text for text, enum, ref and entry, a list of text for tags; a rows field is not read), compiled at import (`codex.kinds.<k>.reminders[i].if`). **`contains(list, x)`** is a new expression function, both engines, with vectors: true when an item equals `x` (exact). **Prep:** a sixth kind of line, `picks`: `{ id, label?, note?, entryId, field, rows: [{ rowId, on (default false) }] }`, at most 30 rows, each row once (`A row is picked twice`). It is never sent: the run view shows each picked row as its chips with a button named by the field's `pick.label`, and a tap saves the scene's whole prep through `codex.save` with that row flipped. A pick of an entry gone, or of a field that is not a pickable rows field of its kind, is broken; a picked row its entry no longer holds is shown broken and keeps its place. Only shapes are checked at save, as for the other lines. **Seat view, both engines:** a tags or rows field follows its field's visibility, whole; a stored rows value reaches a seat cut to what still fits: rows past the cap, rows without a sound or with a repeated id, cells of a column the ruleset no longer has or that no longer fit are left out; the rest of each row stays (`fitRows`); stored, nothing is rewritten (the compatible upgrade of #51 checks no codex value, as #71 has it). The served manifest gives a seat that does not read GM notes only the reminders whose `if` compiles over the `party` fields alone, so a reminder never tells a `gm` value; each seat's browser evaluates them over the fields it received (`codexReminders`). **Room document:** tags and rows travel as other field values; a row's `from` and `ref` cells are checked by the same `codex.save` the import runs (refused at `codex[i].fields`); a pick line's `entryId` must be one of the document's entries (`roomNoSuchEntry`) and is rewritten to the new id; row ids travel unchanged. **Screens:** the GM's editor draws a tags field as chips that remove on tap and a box offering the options and the pack names found for what was typed (exact first, then starting with it, then holding it), Enter taking the suggestion spelled exactly so else the write-in; a rows field as one card per row (its chips, then an input per column; a Test cell a select of the ruleset's Tests in their words and their inputs), ADD A ROW and FILL A ROW FROM a pack type's search. Wherever an entry is read, its reminders as lines with their cite, its tags as a group of chips, its rows as a list of chip lines (text as written, a number after its column's label, an enum by its label and tone, a true yes/no by its column's label, a ref by the entry's name, a Test by its words and inputs, the row's cite); in lists, a tags field's words and a rows field's count beside the title. **Fixtures:** sample-d20 0.1.8 (a faction's `marks` tags from lineage names with write-ins and the reminder `outsiderAlly`, a place's `dangers` rows with a Test, filled from creatures, picked as `Noticed`); sample-skirmish 0.1.3 (a quest's party `log` of two text columns, no `from`, no `pick`); both hash as before without them. | Auspex keeps a place's hazards as book-table rows (with an intensity) and the GM's own (a name, a Test, an effect), at most 30 (`packages/shared/src/codex.ts:187-203`), a scene's picks of its place's hazards each with SPOTTED, saved with the whole scene (`scene-hazards.tsx:34-38`, rules-flow §11.95), chips with Tests, Conditions, Threats and the printed result (`hazard-chips.tsx`), a faction's Keywords from the books' catalogue with write-ins, 20 at most, 60 characters (`keyword-picker.tsx`, `keywords.ts:12-32`), and the mixing hint on an allied faction by its Keywords (`faction-card.tsx`, §11.83); none reaches a phone. The platform says each in general words. Decisions taken here: a row filled from a pack entry first copied it; #230 reversed that for parity with Auspex, which keeps a book hazard's row id and reads it live (`hazard-list.tsx` 83-88 `entryFor`, 138-144 `hazardView` and "not in the loaded tables" when the row is gone; `hazards.ts` 49-79; `packages/shared/src/codex.ts` 188-203), so it now follows the pack with the GM's cells over it (Auspex stores only the intensity a GM picks); a pick names its entry and field rather than "the scene's place", and a stale pick is shown broken, not dropped on save as Auspex drops it; tags are compared by exact spelling and kept as typed (Auspex folds case and accents and upper-cases a write-in); a column is one entity type, not several; there is no `entry` column. Not expressed: the ×TIER roll that adds rows from a pack table (a random table in a row field is the tables layer's), an intensity whose choices come from the row's own entry, DN derived from the campaign's Tier, one printed Test that expands into a chip per skill, and a chain of reminders where the first that holds hides the rest (each reminder's `if` excludes the others instead). |
| 90 | The setup checklist a ruleset declares | **The manifest:** an optional top-level `checklist`, at most 30 steps in the book's order, each `{ id, label (1 to 80), cite, done, goto?, note? (1 to 200), optional? }`; ids unique; `cite` needed outside homebrew (`a rule entry needs a cite`). `done` is one of a closed list, by `count`: `entries` (`kind` one of the nine codex kinds; `field?` a field the ruleset declares for that kind, else `"<f>" is not a field of <kind>`; `equals?` a word, whole number or yes/no that must fit that field, else the field-value check's words, and `equals needs a field` without one; `prep?` one of the six prep kinds, scenes only, else `only a scene holds prepared lines`; `min?`), `actors` (`entity` an actor type, else `unknown entity` or `"<e>" is not an actor entity`; `built?`, only for the ledger builder's actor type, else `"<e>" has no build`; `min?`), `campaignField` (`field`, else `unknown campaign field`), `pool` (`pool` a party, gm or campaign resource, else `"<p>" is not a shared pool`; `min?`), `sessions` (`min?`), `packs` (`slug?`; `min?`), `safety`. `min` is 1 to 100. `goto` is `{ tab, kind? }`, `tab` one of `track`, `prep`, `codex`, `party`, `tests`, `map`, `rules` (another is refused by the schema), `kind` a codex kind and only with `codex` (`only the codex tab takes a kind`). Both engines, crafted manifest vectors; a ruleset without the block validates and hashes as before. **The ticks** are worked out in the GM's browser by one function (`checklistTicks`, rules-engine `checklist.ts`) over what the GM seat already receives (entries with their fields and prep, actors with their type and saved build, campaign fields, pools, the journal's sessions, the safety lines, and the room's attached packs from the rules route), never stored and with no Go twin: `entries` counts the entries of the kind whose field is set (text not blank, a list not empty, any number or yes/no) or equals the value, or (with `prep`) the scenes holding a line of that kind; `actors` those of the type (with `built`, those with a saved build); `campaignField` is done while the field has a value (a declared field always has one: Auspex's always-ticked Tier); `pool` is done when the pool exists and (with `min`) holds at least that much; `sessions` counts the journal, `packs` the attached packs (of that slug), `safety` lines and veils. Each needs `min` (one when it does not say). **Next** is the first step not done that is not `optional`. **Who sees it:** the served manifest carries the block only to the side that reads GM notes; a player and the table screen never receive it, and nothing draws it for them. **The screen:** at the top of the GM's PARTY tab (where a new room lands), titled SETTING UP with "n of m steps done": each step a row with DONE or TO DO, NEXT on the next step, OPTIONAL on an optional one not done, its label, "Now: n" and its cite, its note, and a GO TO button named by its place (a tab, or CODEX › a kind, which opens the codex listing that kind), primary on the next step. It is folded to its heading and count while every step that is not optional is done, and the GM can open or fold it at any time (the fold is not stored). Not shown for a ruleset without the block. **Fixtures:** sample-skirmish 0.1.4 adds the chapter campaign field and eight steps covering every count; without them it hashes as 0.1.3 did; sample-pool has no block. | Auspex's setup panel (`apps/web/components/gm/setup/components/setup-panel/setup-panel.tsx`, `lib/setup/setup.ts:30-53`, `setup-panel/constants.ts:11-23`): eleven steps in the Core's order, each ticked from the campaign and the codex and never stored, NEXT on the first unticked step that is not optional (the patron step is optional), a NOW column with the count, GO TO a tab or the codex at a kind, and the panel starting folded when every step is done. Each of its steps is said here: kind of game, framework and patron as a field set on the premise entry (patron `optional`), Tier as a campaign field, setting, factions and NPCs as entries of a kind, characters as actors of the character type, scenes as scene entries, combat as a scene with roster lines, a session as the journal. Decided here: the panel folds whenever the steps are done, not only on arrival; GO TO opens a tab, and the codex at a kind, and since #200 a place inside it (Auspex focuses the framework box or the session band); "Now" shows the count only, not the value (Auspex's TIER n · XP and the kind tags joined). Not expressed: a step whose words carry a value of the room (the Tier and its starting XP), and an anchor inside a tab (#200). |
| 91 | Once per round, the table a use opens next, and what an actor holds up | **A use limited per round is a spend** (#84) with `limit: { per: "round", max }` (`per` only `"round"`, `max` 1 to 10): its inputs and costs are the spend's, so the level a use is made at and what it costs are `inputs` and `cost`, and asking for it, approving it and doing it in one tap are the spend's paths. It is counted per actor in a round of the running fight the actor is in; outside a fight, or for an actor not in it, nothing is counted (a use only for fights says `if: "ctx.fight.in"`). Once `max` uses stand this round, `planSpend` refuses with **`usedThisRound`** (its detail names the use's label and the round), at the ask, at the approval and at the one tap alike. **Where the count lives:** a turn key **`used`**, a list of `"<round>:<actorId>:<spendId>"` (`usedKey`), held on the running encounter (`encounter.used`, migration 0033). `spend.do` writes it whole, as one more delta of its own action: the current round's entries and the new one, so an earlier round's are dropped only by a later use. A new round needs no reset: a use counts only under the round it names, so the count is free again the moment the round moves, and TURN BACK over a round finds the earlier round's entries still there. Undo and TURN BACK take the write back as they take back any turn key. The tracker's snapshot gives each combatant **`used`**: the spend ids it used this round, once per use, absent when none. **The table it opens next:** `then: { table, inputs? }` names one of the ruleset's `tables` (`unknown table` otherwise) and maps a table input (the key; `is not an input of the table` otherwise, both engines) to one of the spend's inputs (the value; `is not an input of the spend` otherwise). It is only a link: the GM's spend buttons offer OPEN <table> beside the spend, which opens that table in the tables panel as a lookup's chain is opened: the spender becomes the panel's target (a new chain, as picking one is), and each mapped table input starts at the spend's input as picked (its min when none was), a stepper held to its bounds, a switch on when not 0; nothing is rolled or applied by the spend. **Held up:** a `list` field may say `chips: true` (its items show as chips beside the Condition chips on the GM's tracker rows, the phones' and the table screen's order, with end one, end all and add on the GM's combatant panel and on the holder's phone sheet) and `picks: { entity }` (quick picks: the names of the attached packs' entries of that type, read when asked for; an unknown type is refused). Every change is an ordinary `field.set` of the whole list, so the field's `editableBy` decides who may change it, and Undo takes it back. **An unset list reads as empty** wherever an expression reads it, in both engines, so a derived value may count one (`2 * count(wards)`) without a creation step writing it, and a Test's `target` reads that derived value as it reads any other: the derived pass reads the actor type's own unset (or null) list fields as `[]`, and a scope built from stored fields (a lookup's target, and on the web a spend's, a resource's bounds, a health state's) goes through `withLists`, which reads every actor type's list field as `[]` where it is unset. It replaces #88's `withHeld`, which did the same for held kinds' fields only. **Fixtures:** sample-skirmish 0.1.5 (wards with chips and picks, `wardLoad` on the check's target, Nerve, Channel once a round, the backlash lookup it opens with its push as `extra`); without them it hashes as 0.1.4 did. | Auspex's MANIFEST (`apps/api/src/encounter/encounter.actions.ts:605-613`, rules-flow §11.181) refuses a second power in a Round, is cleared by the new Round with the turn marks, and is restored by TURN BACK; it is a mark per combatant, with the power and the level said, not tracked, and the Perils lookup opened from a count the GM enters. A spend already carries inputs, costs, the GM's one tap and a player's ask, so the limit is one more line on it rather than a new block; it is kept in the turn because Undo and TURN BACK already reach every turn key, and a round-stamped entry needs no reset that each turn order would have to make. Auspex's sustained powers are `Sustaining: <power>` conditions on the character, +2 DN each on that character's Tests, dropped one by one or with END ALL, with the character's own powers as quick picks (`packages/shared/src/psychic.ts`, rules-flow §11.180); a list field, a derived count and the Test's target say the same with no new block. Quick picks from the character's own build lines, and the Willpower reminders after damage, are not built. |
| 92 | Spawn options, adjustments fixed at spawn, `party.seated`, a combatant's marker, the tracker row's tag and steppers, the encounter budget | **The manifest:** an optional `spawn` block. **`spawn.options`** (at most 20) are `{ id, label, cite, entities?, when?, effects }`: chosen at spawn (`actor.spawn` `options`, ids each once, at most 10), additive, and kept by the actor (`actor.spawn_options`, migration 0034). Their `effects` are the Condition effects' shape and run in the same pass, by `(priority, source id)`, with source id `spawn/<id>`; they count as `gm` for the visibility check, and only the GM side (and a seat holding the actor) is sent `spawnOptions`. `when` reads the **spawn scope**: the entry's values as fields, `campaign`, `party.characters` and `party.seated`, and `ctx.mob` (spawned as a mob); an option not for the actor's type, or whose `when` is false or cannot be read, is refused **`optionNotHere`**, an id the ruleset does not declare **`noSuchOption`**, and nothing is spawned. **An override effect now sets an unset field**, in both engines (any other mode still skips one), on the one path every base-phase effect takes, so a condition's, a chosen entry's and a held record's (#88) override does so too: with `has()` an option says "a value the entry does not list lands on a default plus the bonus" (`when: "!has(x)"`, `mode: override`). **`spawn.adjust`** (at most 10) are `{ option?, entities?, when?, add, cite }`: applied once, at spawn, when its option is chosen (always without one) and its `when` holds, each `add` key an integer field (added to the entry's value; a field the entry does not list stays absent) or an entity resource (added to its starting value; a resource the actor does not get stays absent), read in the spawn scope and never again: a later seat or campaign change does not move it. An `add` that cannot be read adds nothing. **`party.seated`** is the room's active `pc` actors that a member's seat holds (`membership_character`); a guest token's character does not count, and a room where nobody holds a seat reads 0 (the source app falls back to every character then; a ruleset says that fallback itself, `party.seated > 0 ? party.seated : party.characters`, house decision #193). It exists only in the spawn scope and the budget's, read when they are. **A resource's `start` (#52) reads the spawned entry's values** already, since a spawn writes them as the actor's fields first: Spite `start: "grudge"` needs nothing new. **The marker** is a closed platform list, `Marker`: red, amber, green, blue, purple, white (the source app's, matching the tags on the miniatures; Theme tokens `--marker-*`, the same in every Theme, not a World's to set). It belongs to the combatant (`combatant.marker`), set by `actor.spawn` `marker` when the creatures join a running fight (without one, like the view, nothing is kept) and by **`encounter.mark { combatantId, marker | null }`** (`encounter:run`), which, like `encounter.setVisibility`, is a fight setting and not logged. Every seat that sees the combatant is sent it, drawn as a square beside the colour's word on the tracker row, the phones' and the table screen's order. **The tracker row** draws, from the plain paths of the layout's `trackerRow` target (#83) until that target is drawn whole: an enum field as the row's tag, its `notes` (#71) shown on a tap, and an entity resource (`res.<id>`) as a stepper beside the row, a delta through `resource.capped` `adjust`. Widget rows of `trackerRow` are still not drawn. **The roster line** (#73) takes `marker` and `options`; START THE FIGHT and a single send carry them on the `actor.spawn`; a line naming an option the pinned ruleset no longer has is broken (`missing: "option"`); its options are offered by actor type, their `when` read by the server when it is sent. They travel in the room document with the line. **`spawn.budget`** is `{ cite, lines: [{ id, label, cost, budget }], switches?: [{ id, label, by }] }`: a line's `cost` is read for each pick in the spawn scope with `ctx.count` (how many, a mob's members) and `ctx.mob`, its `budget` in the room's (`campaign`, `party.seated`), and a switch that is on sets each line's budget to its `by`, which reads that budget as `ctx.budget`. The add-creatures panel counts the creatures in the fight and the entry picked; the scene's roster editor its lines; both show each line against its budget, saying `over` in words, and the switches, which live on that screen. It refuses and stores nothing. A cost that cannot be read counts 0. **Fixtures:** sample-skirmish 0.1.6 (a creature's `kind` and `grudge`, Spite, the Peril campaign field beside the checklist's chapter, the tracker row, two options, scaling and a budget) and the `sample-raiders` pack; without them it hashes as 0.1.5 did. | The source app's Threat Profiles, Scaling Threats and markers (`packages/shared/src/threat-scale.ts:35-162`, `apps/api/src/encounter/encounter.actions.ts:94-154`, rules-flow §11.82, §11.103-108): profiles are additive, chosen at add time, an unlisted Skill landing on Default plus the bonus; scaling adds the seated Agents and the Tier, nothing to a Troop or a Mob, a Shock of "–" stays "–", and is fixed at add; the six marker colours; FORTIFIED doubles every target and is a toggle never stored. Options run as effects, not baked into the stat block, so the platform keeps one modifier path (#61) and the sheet can name them; adjustments are baked, because the source app fixes them at add time. Scaling is an option with an adjustment because the source app's SCALE is a tick the GM chooses. The source app's budget counts by Threat class; here each line's cost says what it counts, so a class line is a `cost` that reads the entry's class. |
| 93 | Whispers: the GM's private line to chosen characters, one reply each, cleared each session | **A single reply to a GM's note is not chat** (principle 8): there is no thread, nothing goes from player to player, and nothing outlives the session. **`whisper.send`** (`{ to, text }`; `session:run`): 1 to 30 distinct characters of the party (`pc` or `companion`) of this room, else `whisperRecipient`; the text trimmed, 1 to 500 characters; at most 100 whispers in the room, else `whisperLimit` (`{max}`). Each send is a new whisper: none is edited or resent. **`whisper.reply`** (`{ whisperId, actorId, text }`; the new permission `whisper:receive:own`, a player seat's on a character it holds, never the GM side's): the text trimmed, 1 to 200 characters; a reader's one reply, which a new one replaces; replying marks it read. **`whisper.read`** (`{ whisperId, actorId }`; the same permission): the reader's read mark, the first standing. A whisper that is gone is `whisperGone`, one not to that character `whisperNotToThem`. **`whisper.delete`** (`{ id }`; `session:run`): the GM takes it back; one already gone is no refusal. **Without a GM** every whisper action is refused `whispersNeedGm` (it is checked in the handler, so a table-run player seat gets that code, not `noPermission`), and changing the room to a mode without a GM deletes its whispers. **Storage:** the tables `whisper` and `whisper_reader` (migration 0035), a reader row per character with its reply, reply time and read time; a deleted character's row goes with it. **Cleared** in the transaction of `session.start`, `session.end`, `session.abandon` and `campaign.archive` (which ends the running session). **The seat view:** `LiveState.whispers`, newest first, each `{ id, text, createdAt, readers: [{ actorId, reply, repliedAt, readAt }] }`. The side that runs the session with a GM (`session:run`: the GM, a co-GM with the session scope, the owner, an admin) receives every whisper whole; any other seat only whispers to a character it holds, with only those readers, so it never learns another reader's reply, name or existence; the table screen and a seat holding no addressed character receive none. Whispers are not written to the session's log, so they are in no recap and no player's log. **The room document** does not carry them: they do not outlive a session. **Screens:** the GM's header has WHISPER, with a count of replies this tablet has not seen (kept in the tablet's storage); the panel picks characters of the party (with an ALL toggle), one line, the session's list with each reader's read mark and reply, and a take-back. A phone holding an addressed character shows an unread whisper over any tab, after a called Test, buzzing once as the turn does; closing or replying marks it read. The phone's notebook lists the session's whispers to reread and answer. | Kept from Auspex (rules-flow §11.164; `apps/api/src/whisper/whisper.actions.ts:22-60`; `packages/shared/src/actions.ts:40-42, 121-132`): 1 to 30 characters, 500 and 200 characters, 100 a campaign, a second reply overwrites the first, the GM deletes, a phone never learns who else got it, cleared at a session's start or end, a campaign file without them. Read marks are the server's here (Auspex keeps them in each browser) because the GM sees who has read. `session.abandon` did not exist when Auspex's rule was written; it ends a session, so it clears them. The ALL toggle is the platform's (Auspex taps each character). A repeated character is refused, not silently merged, as `table.deal` refuses one. |
| 94 | The admin console: users, their seats, and a room's controls | **`GET /api/admin/users`** (the admin key, as every `/api/admin` route: no key 401, a wrong one 403, the same rate limit and CSRF check as its neighbours): the 200 most recently seen users, each `{ id, name, role, account, lastSeenAt, seats: [{ campaignId, campaignName, role, owner, characters }] }`, where `account` says an InconsisID identity is linked (else an anonymous device), `role` is the platform role and a seat's `role` the seat's, `owner` whether they own that room and `characters` the names the seat holds. Only the display name: no email, no device secret. Each read is logged as `user.list`. **`DELETE /api/admin/users/:id/seats`** (an id of 1 to 100 characters, else 400; an unknown user 404): `member.remove` at each room the user sits in, as its own action through the table, so their sockets there are kicked and the room rebroadcast, and their characters stay. A room the user owns keeps them, refused `ownerNotRemovable` without being tried, since the owner hands a room on and is never removed; any other refusal keeps that seat too. The answer is `{ removed: [{ campaignId, campaignName }], kept: [{ campaignId, campaignName, code }] }`; each room is logged as `user.unseat` with the user as target and `removed` or `kept: <code>`. **The admin's actions** (`AdminAction`, both engines, held equal by the core vectors) gain `session.abandon` and `session.delete`, with their own refusals. **`GET /api/admin/campaigns`** gains `tableMode`, `latestSession` (`{ id, number, endedAt }` or null) and `gms` (the GM seats' names); **`GET /api/admin/events`** takes `?campaignId=` for one room's rows. **The console** has Rooms and Users tabs. A room's MANAGE dialog seats a known user (picked from the users list) as GM in a room with a GM, ends the running session without an award or deletes the last ended one (a second tap confirms), archives or unarchives, deletes an archived room once its name is typed, and shows the room's rows of the admin log. The Users tab lists the users and removes one from every room once their name is typed. A room's export is not here. | Kept from Auspex (`apps/api/src/admin/admin.controller.ts:39-83`, `game-card.tsx:15-123`, `users-tab.tsx:14`): the users with their roles and seats and no email, REMOVE FROM ALL GAMES as one `member.remove` per seat with the failures reported and the rest done, the card's ADD GM from a list of known users, END SESSION with no award, ARCHIVE, UNARCHIVE and a typed delete. Where the platform's own rules differ they win: an owner is never removed (Auspex has no owner beyond a GM seat), a room is archived before it is deleted, the console is the shared admin key rather than a signed-in admin, so there is no "yourself" to refuse, and a removal is confirmed by typing the name, as deleting a room is. The list is capped at 200 by last seen, as the admin log is, because every anonymous device is a user here. Auspex has no control for deleting a session; the platform's admin gets one since `session.delete` is in both lists. The room's event log is the admin log's rows for that room: the platform has no other log to link. |
| 95 | Items a character holds in play: GIVE and TAKE BACK, fitting, counted stores, EDIT CARRIED | **Core, for every game:** a row per item an actor holds (`actor_item`, migration 0037: it is filtered by holder, joined to pack and codex entries and referenced by the item it is fitted to, so a table, not a list field as #88's records are), naming a served pack entry by type and key or hand-written with a name; a GM's `note` (at most 200); a counted kind's `quantity` (1 to 999; a row reaching 0 is deleted); `fitted_to` another item of the same actor; `source` `give`, `hand` or `acquire`; the codex item a GIVE handed over (unique, so an item entry has one holder, and the entry's holder is read from the row, not stored on the entry); and `rev`, from a sequence on every write. Every write sets a row whole as a delta whose target is the item (`DeltaTarget.item`, Go only like a clock's), logged under one action; Undo puts the row back while it still holds what the step left (`undoChanged` otherwise). The ruleset declares the rest. **The manifest:** an optional `items: { kinds, give? }`. `kinds` (1 to 20) are `{ id, label, entry, tag?, entities, card?, max?, unique?, stacks?, spend?, fits?, cite }`: the pack entries of type `entry` (not an actor type) carrying `tag` (when given; an entry's kind is the first in the list it matches, `itemKindOf`), held by the actor types `entities`; `card` the `cards` variant that draws it (`weapons`, `armour`, `gear`); `max` per actor (1 to 200, default 60); `unique` one of an entry per actor; `stacks` a quantity, one row per entry or hand-written name; `spend` (only with `stacks`) the holder may lower it; `fits: { to, max, slot?, gmTag? }` an item of this kind is fitted to a loose item of a kind in `to`, at most `max` on one host, one per value of the entry's `slot` field (a field of the entry type, read from the entry's `body`), an entry tagged `gmTag` by the GM side only; a counted kind takes no `fits`. `give: { ref, loose }`: the codex `item` field (a `ref` to a kind's entry type) a GIVE reads, and the kind (not counted) an item entry with no ref is given as, by its title. A pack entry of a kind's type may carry effects (#61), checked against the kind's holders. Problems: `duplicate kind`, `unknown entity`, `is an actor`, `unknown actor type`, `only a counted kind is spent`, `a counted kind is not fitted`, `unknown item kind`, `<type> has no field`, `the item entry has no field`, `not a ref to an item kind's entry type`, `a counted kind is not given`. Absent, a ruleset validates, compiles and hashes as before. Both engines and the manifest and pack vectors. **Effects:** a served item's entry effects join the actor's derived values and base fields through #61's path, sorted with the rest; a kind with `fits` counts only while fitted to an item its holder holds. A hand-written item and an entry no longer served add nothing; the latter keeps its key as its name. **Actions** (each one logged action; Undo takes it back): `item.give { entryId, actorId }` (`character:edit:any`; a session must run, `noSessionRunning`): refused for an entry that is not an item (`itemNotFound`), one held already (`itemGiven`), a ref the room is not served (`itemNoEntry`) or matching no kind (`itemNotAnItem`), a counted kind (`itemCounted`: counted, not given, as the source app refuses ammunition), a type the kind is not for (`itemNotFor`), a second of a unique entry (`itemAlready`), a full kind (`itemsFull`); a given fitting is loose, as the source app makes it a gear line. `item.take { entryId }` (`character:edit:any`, any time): the given row is deleted, the items fitted to it first set loose; `itemNotGiven`. `item.add { actorId, kind, entry? \| name?, note, quantity }` (`character:edit:any`; ADD BY HAND): exactly one of `entry` and `name` (`itemEntryOrName`), the entry served (`itemNoEntry`) and of that kind (`itemNotAnItem`); a counted kind adds to the row of the same entry or name; `noItemKind`, `itemNotFor`, `itemAlready`, `itemsFull`. `item.edit { itemId, rev, name?, note?, remove }` (`character:edit:any`; EDIT CARRIED): refused `itemChanged` when `rev` is not the row's now (the source app compares the line's text; a revision also catches an undo that put an older row back); `name` only on a hand-written line. `item.fit { itemId, to \| null }` and `item.adjust { itemId, by }` (no declared permission: the handler asks `character:edit:own` of the item's holder, the holder's seat or the GM side): fit refuses `itemNoFit` (kind without `fits`), `itemFitted` (fitted already), `itemFitsNot` (a host of another kind, fitted itself, or the item), `itemFitGm`, `itemAlready` (that entry on that host), `itemFitFull`, `itemSlotTaken`; off refuses `itemNotFitted`; a player may take off a GM's fitting (the source app does not check it on removal). Adjust: `itemNotCounted`; off the GM side only a negative `by` on a `spend` kind (`itemOnlySpend`); at 0 the row is deleted. `itemNotFound` for a row the room does not hold. **What a seat receives:** the actor's `items`, `[{ id, kind, entry, name, cite, note, quantity, fittedTo, source, entryId, rev, tags, effects }]`, whole for the seat holding it and the GM side of its kind, `[]` for every other seat, the rule the saved build's lines follow (#63) (both engines, the seat view vectors; a note does not reach another player). **The room document:** an actor's `items` (at most 200) `{ id, kind, entry? \| name?, note?, quantity?, fittedTo?, source?, entryId? }`, imported after the codex: refused by path for a kind the ruleset lacks, a type it is not for, an entry not served or of another kind, a second of a unique one, past the max, a fit to a kind it does not fit (`itemFitsNot`) or to an item not of that character (`roomNoSuchItem`), an entry not in the document (`roomNoSuchEntry`) or given twice; every id is a new one. **Screens:** the sheet lists what a character carries under its kinds (Carried), except the kinds whose `card` variant a layout's `cards` widget draws, which it draws under the build's own lines; a line shows its quantity, what is fitted to it, the note and the cite. The holder spends (−) and fits or takes off; the GM side also tops up (+), takes back a given item, edits or removes a line, and adds by hand from the room's packs or by name. A codex item shows its holder, GIVE with a character picker, and TAKE BACK. **The ledger samples** take 0.1.1 (unpinned): a hero's Guard, gear tagged by its record's kind with a slot and effects, two stores, a GM-only pair fitting, the codex item field and the items block. **The existing fixed counters stay:** a ruleset's declared counters of fixed types (entity resources with `ownerSpendOnly`) keep their steppers, resets and bounds; the write-in types the source app adds by name are a counted kind's hand-written rows, so nothing a converter writes today is lost or moved. **Not composed here:** acquiring an item by a Test (the source app's requisition) is the next layer of the stack. **Not ported:** fitting in play to a weapon or suit the saved build gives (the build's lines are its sheet document, not items: the GM adds the item by hand to fit to it); a per-host `slotOn`, `copies` and the exemptions from the count; the given item's place or NPC holder fields are not cleared (they are the ruleset's own declared fields); a fitting's own number changes to its host's profile (only effects on the actor's values are applied). | The source app keeps gear in the character's sheet document and changes it with GIVE, TAKE BACK, fit, remove, supply and EDIT CARRIED (`character.actions.ts` 457-709, rules-flow §11.112-114), refusing duplicates, ammunition and a full list, three upgrades a host and one a slot, a pair the GM's. Here the shape is data any game declares, each item a row the one mutation path writes, logs and undoes (the source app has no undo for any of them), and a rebuild never touches the rows, so what play gave is kept (`build.save` writes fields and the sheet document only). The source app removes an empty counter on the next minus; here a counted row goes at 0, so a phone's minus reaches nothing in one tap. |
| 96 | A GM's override of a derived value; the advance field raised by hand; a build over a sheet made by hand | **An override is a declared field, not a new action.** A derived value may name `override`, an `int` field of the same entity (`"<f>" is not an int field of <entity>` otherwise; both engines and the manifest vectors). Set, the field's value replaces the worked-out one after every effect (derived values that read it were worked out before, as they read a value before its derived effects); unset, the value is worked out. Fail-closed: a derived value without `override` is never overridden. The field is an input of the derived value's visibility, so a `gm` override field makes a looser derived value a load error. The GM writes it through `field.set` (the field's `editableBy`, `gm` by default; logged; Undo and TURN BACK take it back); `null` clears it, and the worked-out value returns. A typed value row keyed by `actor_id` (design §5.4), the room document carries it with the actor's fields, and every seat that receives the field reads why the number is what it is. **The breakdown** (`breakdownOf`): with the override set, the worked-out value's own lines, then `{ source: <the field's label>, amount: <override − worked out>, cite: null, override: true }`, which the sheet words as "<label>: the GM's override". **Screens:** the breakdown sheet of a derived value with `override` gives the GM side the number, SET and CLEAR (a blank box clears); the ledger builder page gives the GM side a box of the character's overrides, saved at once and not with the build, so "save details only" on a made character is RENAME (`actor.rename`), the details (`field.set`) and these, with no build save (the source app sends a separate `character.update` for overrides). **The advance field raised by hand:** `campaign.setField` raising the field `build.tier` names (house decision #64) answers the notice `advanced { field, from, to, steps }` (the field's label, the values, `to − from`). What an advance resets is reset once: every character's count starts at its XP and the waive clears, which a second step would set to the same values (the source app tests a rise as a yes or no too); the builder already offers one advance's slot per step from each character's own Tier. Lowering it resets nothing and says nothing; setting the same value is no change. **A build over a sheet made by hand:** the `build` block may declare `keep: { fields, resources }` (fields of `build.actor`, at most 50, `<actor> has no field`; entity resources it carries, at most 20; both engines). `build.save` takes `keepSheet` (default false). An actor with field rows and no `actor_build` row is still refused `alreadyBuilt`, unless the save is a ready one (not a draft) with `keepSheet` and the ruleset declares `keep`; from a seat off the GM side of the actor's kind it is `keepSheetGm` (the source app's keepSheet is the GM's). Then the build is saved as a first save, except that the fields in `keep.fields` are not written (their hand-set values stay) and the resources in `keep.resources` are not started (their values stay); everything else the build writes replaces the hand-made values, and the items the actor holds (#95) are never touched by a build.
