The ledger sample (0.1.2) keeps `armourRating` and `wealth`. **By-hand steps beside a build on one actor type (lifted by #199):** the platform refuses `creation` steps on the type `build.actor` names, and lifting it is not a small change: an untyped character is claimed by both (`BuildEntity` types it as the creation's single actor type, `build.save` as `build.actor`, and the web's `usesLedger` sends every untyped or `build.actor` character to the ledger builder), the creation's `choose` rows and the ledger's sheet would both write the same fields, and the build page would need a choice between the two. A hand-made sheet on a ledger type is made with `field.set` (or a room document) and built over with `keepSheet`. | The source app overrides nine Traits from REVIEW, replacing the derived value, blank keeping it (`state.ts` 239, `review-step.tsx` 65-70 and 272), saved apart from the build (`builder.tsx` 194-206); reads a Tier raised through `campaign.update` as an Ascension (`campaign.actions.ts` 122-133, rules-flow §11.55); and lets the GM keep armour, gear, Wealth and extra weapons when a first build goes over a hand-made sheet (`actions.ts` 294, `character.actions.ts` 344-376, `build.ts` 2362). As declarations: an override field reuses the one field write, its log and undo (the source app has neither for overrides), and the derive pass in both engines; `keep` names what is kept in the platform's terms. Not ported: the source app's floor of 1 on an override (a game's number, not the platform's; a ruleset can bound the field with `min`); keeping the better of two armour suits by their printed rating, gear lines and extra weapons merged by name (`keepPlay`): declared since #199 (`keep.greater`, `keep.merge`); the platform's items survive any build. |
| 97 | Entries made from pack entries; a text field or a title rolled on tables; a codex reminder reads the campaign | **The manifest:** a codex kind (#71) takes `from`, 1 to 5 `{ entity, name?, values?, children?, tests?, roster? }`: the mapping a row's prefill has (#89; `name` a `text` field of the kind that also takes the entry's name, `values` a field of the kind to the entity field it takes, which must fit it as a column must; the title always takes the name), plus `children`, a `ref` field of the entity naming its own type, and, on a scene only, `tests: { field, inputs? }` (a `list` field of Test ids; `inputs` a Test input id to an `int`, `text`, `enum` or `bool` field of the entity) and `roster` (a `list` field of pack keys). Refused by path: `unknown entity`, `"<e>" fills entries twice`, `"<f>" is not a text field of <kind>`, `"<f>" is not a field of <kind>`, `"<g>" is not a field of "<e>"`, `"<g>" does not fit the field`, `"<g>" is not a ref to "<e>"`, `a scene sits under an adventure, not a scene` (children on a scene), `only a scene holds prepared lines`, `"<g>" is not a list field of "<e>"`, `"<g>" is not a value field of "<e>"`. A codex `text` field takes `roll: { tables, join? }` and a kind takes `titleRoll` of the same shape: 1 to 4 table ids rolled in order, the lines' labels joined by `join` (at most 10 characters, a space when it does not say); `unknown table "<t>"`, `"<t>" is dealt, never rolled` (an `owner` table). A codex reminder's `if` (#89) also reads `campaign.<id>`, the campaign fields every seat reads, so the party is sent a reminder that reads only `party` fields and the campaign. Both engines, crafted manifest vectors. **A tree** needs no new key on a pack entry: a `ref` field of a type to its own type says it. **Making one:** `codex.fromPack { kind, entity, key, parentId? }`, permission `codex:write`. The server finds `entity` among the kind's `from` (else `fromPackNotFor`) and the key among the room's served entries of that type that this seat reads, first by pack slug (else `fromPackNoEntry`: a `gm` pack entry is never made into an entry by a seat that does not read it); with `children`, the served entries naming it through that field come along, breadth first, in the packs' order (slug, then key), each once, at most 60 in all, the root included (`fromPackTooMany`, Auspex's `MAX_PLACES_PER_ADD`). Each entry is made by `codex.save` (title the entry's name; `name` and `values` mapped, a value that does not fit its field left for the GM, a `gm` field left out for a seat without `codex:read_secret`; body and GM notes left empty: no printed text, §11.80 and principle 2) and nested by `codex.move` (so the depth of #69 holds, `tooDeep`, and a scene goes under its `parentId` adventure, `sceneNeedsAdventure`). A scene's prep: one Test line per listed Test id (ids `t1`…, the party takes it, its `inputs` only where the Test declares them; a Test the ruleset lacks is kept, shown broken as #73 has it) and one roster line per listed key found among the served actor-type entries this seat reads (the first by pack slug: its slug and key, the count its repeats up to 50, `hidden`, `temporary`); a key nobody serves brings no line; 40 lines of a kind at most. The answer is `{ ok, id: <root>, ids: [<root>, …] }`. **One action, one Undo:** each entry made is logged as a delta with the Go-only target `{ entry }` and kind `create`; `event.undo` (and TURN BACK) deletes them newest first as `codex.delete` does (its refusals hold), passing over one already deleted (`skipped: { entryId, reason: "deleted" }`); like every Undo, only within a running session. `codex.fromPack`, `event.undo` and `event.turnBack` rebroadcast the codex. **Where it came from:** `codex_entry.source` (jsonb, migration 0039) `{ entity, key, cite }`; `codex.save` takes `source` (null forgets it), checked as above, its `cite` read from the served entry, never the client's. The seat view sends it to the codex and secrets sides only. The room document's entry carries `source` and the import sends it through `codex.save` (refused at `codex[i].source`). **The screens:** CODEX has ADD FROM A PACK beside NEW ENTRY when the ruleset declares a `from`: the kind (and the type, with several), a search of the served entries (the ref picker #71 uses), and the made root opened; PREP has SCENE FROM A PACK under each adventure; an entry's editor shows "Made from <key>" and its page, which opens through the book relay when a tablet serves the book; a rolled field and a rolled title have ROLL (each table through `table.roll`) and I ROLLED (a number per table), and the words go into the field for the GM to keep or change before SAVE. **The premise** (Auspex's START FROM A FRAMEWORK, row 9) is a kind made from a premise type with `name` into its text field and its bounds into int fields, and a reminder such as `fields.seasonFrom > campaign.season \|\| fields.seasonTo < campaign.season` words the hint. **Fixtures:** sample-d20 0.1.9 (sites nested through `within`, an incident naming two checks and two foes, a charter checked against a new `season` campaign field, a person's `look` and title rolled on two tables each) with the invented pack `sample-atlas`; without them it hashes as 0.1.8 did; the room document's fixture is `0.1.9-room`, a person made from the bestiary's creature. | Auspex copies a printed place, its tree, a faction, a person and a book event into the codex (`place-picker.tsx`, `npc-picker.tsx`, `faction-picker.tsx`, `scene-picker.tsx:21-58`, `codex.actions.ts:369-396`), and USE on a Framework fills names only (§11.80). Here the server reads the served entry, as Auspex's `codex.addPlaces` reads its own data, so a client never supplies values, and the platform words nothing. Where this differs: Auspex copies printed text into GM NOTES (a place's description and hooks, an event's text and read-aloud, a person's notes) and the cite as its first line; here the text stays empty and the cite is the entry's `source` (owner decision 3). Auspex has no undo of an add (the GM deletes the spare, §11.98); here one Undo takes it all back, in a session. | 98 | Generators: ordered steps the GM runs in prep, ending in entries the server makes | **The manifest:** an optional top-level `generators`, at most 20, each `{ id, label (1 to 80), cite, steps (1 to 12), each?, make }`, a rule entry (`a rule entry needs a cite` outside homebrew), ids unique. A step is `{ id, label, when?, kind }`, `kind` one of: `roll` (`table`: a table of the ruleset, not an `owner` one: `unknown table "<t>"`, `"<t>" is dealt, never rolled`), `pick` (`entity`, `unknown entity` otherwise; `filter?`), `number` (`min`, `max`, -1000 to 1000, `max is below min`), `choice` (`options`, 1 to 20 ids, each once, `"<o>" is there twice`; `labels?` naming only options, `"<k>" is not a value`), `text` (`maxLength?` 1 to 200). Step ids are unique across `steps` and `each.steps` (`duplicate id`). `when` and `filter` are booleans and `each.times` an integer (each refused by path with the expression checker's words) over `steps.<id>` (the steps above: a roll as its last line's id, a pick as its key, a number as itself, a choice as its id, a text as itself), `campaign.<id>`, and for a `filter` `entry.<field>` (the picked type's fields, a list as words). `each: { times, steps (1 to 8) }` asks its steps `times` rounds. `make: { kind, title, from?, fields?, each? }`: `title` and each of `fields` (a codex field of the kind to a step id) name a step (`"<s>" is not a step`; for `make.each` a step of either list), a field the kind declares (`"<f>" is not a field of <kind>`) that the step can fill (`"<s>" can't fill "<f>"`: text takes a roll's line label, a pick's name, a choice's label or a text; an int a number; an enum a choice whose options it has; a ref a pick of its entity); `from` a pick step of a type the kind is made from (house decision #97; `"<s>" is not a pick of a type <kind> is made from`). `each` needs `make.each` (`each round needs what it makes`) and the reverse (`nothing repeats`); a scene makes no nested scenes (`a scene sits under an adventure, not a scene`). Both engines, crafted vectors. **The run** is the GM's, in the browser (CODEX, GENERATE…): each step asked while its `when` holds; a roll goes through `table.roll` (ROLL or I ROLLED), and a line with `next` offers the next table within the step, the chain shown with the tables panel's trail (now `TableTrail`, one component); a pick lists the served entries of its type this seat reads that meet its filter; then the rounds `each.times` reads. Nothing is written until CONFIRM. **The confirm** is `codex.generate { generatorId, values: { <step>: { rows? \| key? \| value? } }, each: [ … ] (at most 60), parentId? }`, permission `codex:write`, checked by one function with a Go twin (`checkRun`, vectors): a step whose `when` holds needs its answer of its kind (`generateMissing`), a roll's lines must be a line of its table and then of each line's `next`, at most 8 (`generateRow`: a forged line is refused), a pick a served entry of its type this seat reads that meets its filter (`generatePick`: a `gm` pack entry is never picked by a seat that does not read it), a number whole and within bounds, a choice one of its options, a text 1 to its length after trimming (`generateValue`), and the rounds exactly what `each.times` reads, 0 to 60 (`generateCount { count }`); a skipped step's answer is ignored; an unknown generator is `noSuchGenerator`. Every refusal names the step by its label. **What it makes:** the root of `make.kind`, titled from its step (the generator's label when that step was skipped, 200 characters at most), its fields from their steps (a value that does not fit its field left out, a `gm` field left out for a seat without `codex:read_secret`), and with `from` made from the picked pack entry exactly as `codex.fromPack` makes one (its mapping, its key and page, a scene's lines; the steps' fields over it); then one entry per round under it, the same way. Each through `codex.save` and `codex.move` (so `parentId`, the depth and the kinds hold), logged as one action Undo takes back as #97 has it. Answers `{ ok, id, ids }`. **Who sees them:** the served manifest carries `generators` to the side that reads GM notes only. **Fixtures:** sample-d20 0.1.10 (a warren: a number of chambers, a roll whose built line goes on to its builders, a creature picked under a filter that reads the roll, a chamber rolled per round; a place with its keeper and chambers inside it), sample-harm 0.1.6 (a portent: one roll, one flat lore entry); without them each hashes as before. | Auspex's hulk generator, loot picker and relic wizard (`hulk-generator.tsx`, `loot-picker.tsx`, `relic-wizard.tsx`, `codex.addHulk`, `codex.addLoot`, `codex.addRelic`) and NEW SCENE FROM A TABLE (`scene-picker.tsx:21-58`) roll book tables in the browser (ROLL, I ROLLED or a tap on a row), let the GM pick from a catalogue, and send only ids that the server checks against its tables before making entries in one transaction (rules-flow §11.101, §11.102, §11.113, §11.116). Here the same is a declaration, and one checker with its twin keeps the browser and the server agreeing. Where it differs: Auspex has no undo of what a generator made; here one Undo, in a session. **What the mechanism cannot say of them:** the hulk's stages in any order and rolled again at will (here in order; a step is answered again before CONFIRM), several origins kept (up to five) beside the rooms (one `each` per generator), hazards deduplicated onto the hulk's hazard rows (a step fills no `rows` field), ROOMS FOR A PLACE (a run always makes its root; the GM moves rooms by hand), and its notes composed from three tables (a text field takes one step); a print gap or two overlapping lines (#49's tiling: the importer writes a "roll again" line); loot's items made loose (here under a root entry), counts rolled by the server from dice in a line (the app rolls only on tables), ammunition and wealth awarded in PARTY rather than made, and a line's own catalogue filter (said as a filter over the line's id, line by line); the relic's Power table chosen by the Form's kind while keeping the Form (a roll is read as its last line), its Power's modifiers copied onto a weapon, and a default name made from the picked entry's; the scene from a table needs the event's pack entry to name its line in a field (`filter: entry.line == steps.event`), and the one entry left is tapped, not taken by itself; the look roll's species columns, +3, "roll twice" with a different second colour and the feature grid (#97). |

}`. `entities` are the attacker types (absent: every actor type); `targets` the types it lands on (absent: every type a `health.harm` profile holds, or every actor type without harm). `means` (1 to 4) are where what it is made with comes from: `{ from: "ledger", lines: "weapons" \| "powers" }` (the saved build's sheet list; needs a `build`) or `{ from: "list", field, entry }` (the pack entries of type `entry` whose keys the attacker's list field `field` holds: a held kind's records, or a creature's attacks copied from its pack entry at spawn), or `{ from: "items", kind }` (the items of #95's kind `kind` the attacker holds, read from their pack entries' values as the other sources are; a hand-written item, with no entry, is no means; `kind` must be a declared kind an attacker type holds: `unknown item kind`, `no attacker holds`). `values` (at most 20) are what it reads of the means, `{ <id>: { key, type: "int" \| "bool" \| "text" } }`, read as `ctx.means.<id>`, 0, false or "" where the means has no such key of that type (needs `means`); a `text` value (a weapon's traits) is shown in the guide beside the means and is typed text in every expression, so one that wants a number and reads it is refused at import. `inputs` are a lookup's (#87) `{ id, label, type, min?, max?, cite? }`, read as `ctx.<id>`; `means`, `target`, `attackerMob`, `targetMob`, `pool`, `beat`, `soak`, `total` and `outcome` are reserved. `pool` (what to roll), `beat` (the number to beat) and `soak` (what the target takes off) are figures `{ label, expr, cite?, adds?: [{ label, by, when?, cite? }] (at most 10) }`: the base, then each addition whose `when` holds and whose amount is not 0, the lines the derived-value breakdown draws (`BreakdownLine`), the total their sum. `shows` (at most 6) are further numbers in the same shape with an `id` (unique in the strike), `{ id, label, expr, cite?, adds? }`: the damage to roll, say; they read what the figures read, and nothing reads them. Every figure reads the attacker's scope plus `ctx`: the inputs, `ctx.means`, `ctx.target` (the target's whole scope) and `ctx.attackerMob` / `ctx.targetMob` (a mob's members standing, 0 for anyone else). `outcomes` (1 to 10) `{ id, label, when, wounds?, shock?, cite? }` also read `ctx.pool`, `ctx.beat`, `ctx.soak` and `ctx.total` (the typed total): the first whose `when` holds is what the total does, its `wounds` and `shock` the hit (a mob's members lost are its wounds, as `hit` takes them). `notes` (at most 10) `{ text (1 to 200, the ruleset's own words), when?, cite? }` also read `ctx.outcome` (the outcome's id, "" before one); they change nothing. `then` is #91's `{ table, inputs? }`, a table input filled from a strike input. **At import**, both engines: a duplicate id, a missing cite outside homebrew, an unknown or non-actor type, a ledger means without a build, a list means whose field is not a list field of an attacker type or whose entry type is unknown, `values` without `means`, a duplicate or reserved input, a switch with bounds, a max below its min, a duplicate outcome, a hit without `health.harm` or shock without its resource, an explicit target type no harm profile holds, an unknown `then` table or input: each refused at its path; every expression compiles over each attacker type and each target type. A ruleset without the block validates, compiles and hashes as before. **One function:** `planStrike` (rules-engine `strike.ts`; Go `CompiledRuleset.PlanStrike`, held equal by 2,500 random vector cases over two games) takes the strike, the attacker's and the target's type and scope, the means as it stands, the inputs and a total (or none), and answers the figures with their lines, the further numbers (`shows`, `{ id, total, lines }` in the strike's order, empty for none), the outcome, the hit and the notes, or refuses: `noSuchStrike`, `strikeNotFor` (attacker or target type), `noMeans` (missing where it takes one, given where it takes none), `badInput` (an input, or a total not whole or outside -1000..1000), `wrongState` (an expression the values cannot answer). Both scopes read unset list fields as empty (#91). **The action** `strike.apply { strikeId, attackerId, means?: { source, ref }, targetId, inputs, total }` (permission `encounter:run`, or for a `byOwner` strike the seat holding the attacker, house decision #113): both sides must be combatants of the running fight (`strikeNotInFight`); the means is read on the server as the attacker has it now, `source` the index into `means` and `ref` the sheet line's index, the entry's key, which the attacker's list must hold and the room's packs serve, or the id of an item of the kind the attacker holds whose entry the room's packs serve (`noSuchMeans`); the server plans with the Go twin, refuses an outcome with no hit (`strikeNoHarm`), and runs `health.harm`'s `hit` with the nonzero amounts, refused as it refuses; a client sends the total, never the harm. without them it hashes as 0.1.3 did. sample-harm 0.1.5: arms a hero carries as items (kind `carried`, a blow's second means), the damage to roll (the arm's damage, its edge dice more, the attacker's might more for a close arm) and the arm's traits, a text one note reads; without them it hashes as 0.1.4 did. sample-duel 0.1.0, a second game: one roll plus skill against the target's parry, a hit doing the blade's flat cut, no soak, no strain, no mobs. | Auspex's attack guide (wrath_glory `apps/web/components/gm/tracker/components/attack-guide/attack-guide.tsx`, `packages/shared/src/attack.ts:9-55`, rules-flow §11.173, §11.176, §11.180) is one fixed component for one game: the pool from the weapon's skill, DN = Defence +1 while the attacker is Wounded +2 per sustained power, a Mob attacker's +⌈Troops/2⌉ dice, Resilience − AP soaking the damage total typed by the GM, Wounds over it, 1 Shock on a tie (none for a Threat with Shock "–"), nothing under; a Mob target is left to the Wounds pad; THEN lines by the attacker's and target's class. Where this differs: the attacker is the GM's selected combatant and the target is picked from the fight, where Auspex took the acting combatant and the selected row; a Mob target is struck through the guide, one member per total over its soak, where Auspex left it to the pad (the ruleset's outcome says what it does); Aim, Called Shot and Multi-Attack are inputs that change numbers where a ruleset declares them so, where Auspex only links them. Auspex's `Damage + ED` line, with Strength added for `addStrength`, is a further number a ruleset declares, and a weapon's traits a text value beside it. Not said yet: a gist beside a chip (Brutal's), and Auspex's OPTIONS sheet of combat actions. |
| 100 | The fight's log and the GM's command line | **The log:** the encounter carries `log` for the combat side only (stripped for every other seat in `internal/view` and the reference view, held equal by the table vectors): the running session's logged actions since the active encounter began, newest first, at most 40, each `FightLogLine { id (the action id), round, actorId, undone, strike?, writes }`. `round` and `actorId` are the round and the actor acting the action left, read from the turn writes as they come (`round`, `currentId`; a taken-back step moves neither); `undone` is true once Undo or TURN BACK took the action's writes back; `strike` is the `strike` event strike.apply writes under the same action id (house decision #99: `{ strikeId, attackerId, targetId, means, total, outcome, wounds, shock }`, the means as its name); `writes` are the logged deltas as they are stored (`target`, `kind`, `before`, `after`). No migration: it is read from `session_event`. **The screen:** LOG under the tracker, by round, then by turn (whoever acted, or between turns), each action one line worded from what it wrote in the platform's words: a strike ("Hal strikes Ogre with Maul: Wounded (8)"), a resource or pool change by its signed amount and label, a condition gained or lost, a field set, whose turn begins or ends, a new round; a write the log does not tell (a turn's bookkeeping: `used`, `barred`, a Test's) is left out, and an action with nothing to tell is too. What Undo or TURN BACK took back stays, struck through and marked "(taken back)". Read-only: no editing; undo and redo by line are HISTORY's, in the GM's header (house decisions #131 to #133, which replaced owner decision 6). **The command line:** ORDER › on the tracker, a pure parser (`apps/web/lib/command`) into the existing actions: `<name> hit|heal|shock|recover [n]` (1 without a number; `hit`/`heal` the harm block's wounds, `shock`/`recover` its shock, through `health.harm` `hit`; without harm, the first track's `damage`/`heal`), `<name> +n|-n` (hit or heal), `+<condition> [name]` / `-<condition> [name]` (the label's first letters, spaces ignored; `condition.add`/`condition.remove`), `next` (`turn.next`, or the sides order's `next`), `hold` and `begin` (the sides order's ops), `back` (`event.turnBack`), `<name> mob n` (`health.harm` `muster`), `reveal|hide <name>` (`encounter.setVisibility` full or hidden), `<pool> +n|-n` (`resource.capped` `adjustPool`). A name is the combatant's, matched whole first, then by the first letters of the name or of any word in it; no name is the selected combatant; a plain number before the verb belongs to the name ("ogre 1 hit 3"). The parsed order is said back as a sentence; Enter sends it through the tracker's `run`, so the server decides and its refusal shows as a tap's would; an order that does not parse says why (`unknown`, `noTarget`, `noMatch`, `ambiguous`, `noCondition`, `needsCount`, `notHere`) and sends nothing. The verbs are the platform's words in the messages (`command.words`, each a space-separated list; Vietnamese keeps the English words beside its own). | Auspex's VOX LOG (wrath_glory `apps/web/components/gm/tracker/tracker.tsx:165-188, 766-788`) is a list held in the tablet's memory, written by each button with its own wording, lost on reload and seen by one tablet; here it is read from the server's own log, so every GM-side seat sees the same fight after a reload, and the words come from what was written, not from a second wording table per button. The recap draft (house decision #47) words a session for the whole table in the api's English and leaves taken-back writes out; the log is the GM's and words on the screen, and it keeps a taken-back action struck so a TURN BACK can be seen. Auspex's ORDER › line (`apps/web/lib/command/command.ts:1-173`) has the same grammar of names by prefix, signed numbers and `+Condition`; here it sends one existing action, is said back before Enter, and its verbs are the platform's rather than the game's (`glory`, `wrath` and `go`, `seize`, `aware`, `ambush` are game and sides words: a pool by its label replaces the first two; the sides ops other than next, hold and begin stay the panel's buttons). Since #224 the orders `go`, `seize`, `aware`, `ambush` and `remove` (house decision #161). Not said yet: Auspex's log lines for reminders (here the reminder lines of house decision #81 are their own panel). Since #224 its warnings (Annihilated, Traumatic Injury) are lines a ruleset words (house decision #190). |
| 101 | The manifest a seat is served compiles | **The rule:** what `GET /api/campaigns/:id/rules` serves a seat (`view.SeatManifest`, twin `seatManifest` in `vectors/reference`) validates and compiles, so a phone works its numbers out (`compiled` was null on every sample for a player: the breakdown of a value said its parts are not shown). The definitions a seat may not read are dropped as before (fields, derived values, resources and conditions that are `gm`, codex fields that are not `party`, the checklist, a secret table's entries); then, only when the room's own manifest compiles, the copy is compiled and each problem answered with one cut, in order of path then message, until it compiles. The compiler already checks every place one definition names another, so its problems are the complete list of what names a dropped one. **A cut:** inside a part scoped to actor types (`resources[i]` with scope `entity`, `tests.<id>`, `health.states[i]`, `health.harm.profiles[i]`, `notices[i]`, `spends[i]`, `spawn.options[i]`, `spawn.adjust[i]`, `tables.<id>`), an `unknown identifier` problem narrows the part's `entities` (absent: every actor type) to the types it compiles for, each tried alone, a type whose narrowing no longer validates left out; none, and the part goes whole. A spend with any other problem goes whole, never left without the cost the seat cannot see (house decision #84: an ask for it reaches the seats that see the pool). A test's offer (the GM side's alone, #80), a condition's or a spawn option's effect, a reminder and a side's out-of-turn or lift cost go whole. A strike (#99) is narrowed to the attacker types it compiles for, else goes whole: its figures, additions, values and outcomes stand together, so its guide never shows a number the server would not plan; a means, a further number or a note it cannot read goes alone. A generator (#98) goes whole: a step's `when` or a pick's `filter` cut alone would ask what the server's run check refuses. A codex kind's `from` (#97) loses only the mapping that names a hidden field. Anything else: the node the problem names goes; a guard (`if`, `when`) takes its part with it, since cut alone the part would hold always; a node an earlier cut left without a required part goes in its turn. So a `build.sheet` resource, a `build.keep` resource, `build.gone` naming a cut state, an item block's `give` reading a `gm` codex field, a layout row's binding go, and a ranked `initiative` whose score reads hidden numbers (the server ranks; the phone never did). A cut only removes or narrows: nothing is served that was not before, and no `gm` definition is kept to make anything compile. A problem nothing answers leaves the copy as it is (a `ponytail:`). **A table with no entry** is a seat's copy of one it may not read: it validates and compiles; an import refuses it (`importProblems`, both engines, run by the ruleset import, the check route and the web's form) with the message it had. **Both engines** and a vector per sample ruleset, seat and table mode (`served-manifests.json`); the reference test holds that each compiles and carries nothing `gm` for a seat off the GM side. **The breakdown:** a source without which a derived value cannot be worked out (a base-phase `override` setting a field the formula reads and the actor leaves unset) is credited with the whole value and the base with what is left, instead of the whole breakdown being null. The breakdown is worked out on the web only; it has no Go twin. **The web:** the spends helper keeps reading the seat's own manifest; comments no longer say it may not compile. | A player's phone read the manifest, could not compile it, and showed no parts for numbers it may see (a browser walk on the ledger sample). Leaking the hidden definitions would fix the compile and break principle 4; a hand-written list of every place that names a definition would drift from the validator, which already knows them all. Narrowing by type keeps a resource, a Test and a health state for the characters a player reads (the d20 creature's numbers are all hidden, so its Vigour bounds and its Test modifier cannot be worked out on a phone anyway). |
| 102 | Acquiring an item by a Test; fitting onto a saved build's line; a fitting that changes its host's numbers | **The manifest:** `items.acquire` (optional): `{ test, kinds?, inputs, picks?, if?, limits?, topUp?, quantity?, load?, cite }`. `test` a Test of the ruleset that is not `reported` (`unknown test`, `a reported Test has no number to reach`), every input of it an `int` the acquisition sets (`the Test's input "x" is not a number`, `the Test's input "x" is not set`, `"x" is not an input of the Test`), declaring no offer `acquire` or `acquireTopUp` (`the Test's offer "x" is the acquisition's`). `kinds` (default every kind) the kinds it grants (`unknown item kind`). `inputs`: per Test input, 1 to 6 parts `{ label, value }`, the input being their sum, so the phone and the GM read the number's breakdown. `picks` (at most 3) `{ label, min, max }` the asker chooses, read as `ctx.<id>`. `if` a boolean: whether it may be asked. `limits` (at most 10) `{ kinds, max }`: an actor takes at most `max` acquisition Tests of those kinds in one session (a call counts open or reported; one the GM closed with no report does not). `topUp: { resource, max? }`: an entity resource every holder carries (`is not an entity resource`, `not every holder carries`), spent one per point short, at most `max` (default 1000). `quantity` (default 1, clamped 1 to 999): a counted kind's grant. `load: { field, quantity }`: a field of every non-counted granted kind's entry type naming a counted entry's key (`<type> has no field`, `no counted kind to load`), granted with it. Every expression reads the asking actor's scope, `item` (the entry's declared fields from its `body`, an unset list as empty, and `tags`: an entry type with a field `tags` is refused) and `ctx` (the picks; a pick's `max` reads no `ctx`). `overlap(a, b)`, a new expression function: how many distinct items of `a` the list `b` holds, so a matched-tags count is `overlap(item.tags, keywords)`. Both engines, the manifest and expression vectors, and `planAcquire` (both engines, 600 vectors): the kind (`itemKindOf`), each input's total and parts, the top-up's resource and most, the quantity, the load; refusals `noAcquire`, `itemNotAnItem`, `acquireNotFor` (a kind it does not grant or the actor's type does not hold), `badInput`, `acquireNotNow`, `wrongState`. **Composed, not new:** the ask is a request (#43, #84: `request.send`, the GM side's approve and deny, one at a time per character's cap (house decision #169), cancelled at session end); the roll is a called Test (`test.call` with the worked-out inputs, `test.report` from the phone, the server resolves, one report stands); the grant is a Test offer (#80: applied once through `test.applyOffer`, recorded in `test_offer`, Undo takes it back and it is offered again); the top-up is that offer's resource change; the item rows, fits and refusals are #95's. **New:** `request.send` takes a third form `acquire: { type, entry, to? \| line? }` with the picks as `inputs` (`requestKind` unless exactly one form); it is planned as it would be called (and refused as above, `itemNoEntry` for an entry the room is not served, `acquireLimit { name, kind, max }`, `itemAlready`, `itemsFull`, a host's fit refusals, `notYourCharacter`), one waits per character (`acquireAsked`), then the GM side approves (the Test is called for the character as the approving seat; the answer's id is the call's) or denies; without a GM it is called at once as the table. Stored as `spend_request.acquire_type`, `acquire_key`, `acquire_host` (`to:<item id>` or `line:<card>/<name>`) (migration 0041). A call made for one carries `test_call.acquire_kind` (counted for the limits; a new session's calls start the count again, as the per-round `used` stamps start with the round, so nothing is cleared) and `test_call.acquire` (the grant worked out when called, read whole). Its taker's state carries one synthetic offer: `acquire` on a pass (label: the entry's name, no change), else `acquireTopUp` on a miss short by 1 to the top-up's most and no more than the taker holds (its change: the resource, `-short`); whichever was applied stays shown applied, and applying either refuses the other (`offerApplied`; `offerNotOffered` when the report no longer gives it). Applying writes, as one logged action: the item (`source: "acquire"`; a counted kind adds `quantity` to its row of the entry or a new one; a fitting fitted at once to the asked host, checked again), the loaded counted item, and the top-up's cost. **What a seat receives:** a request's `acquire: { type, entry, name, to?, line? }` (the asker and the answering side, as a resource ask); a call's `acquire: { name, kind, inputs: { <input>: { total, parts: [{ label, value }] } } }` (the taker's seat and the session side; never the table screen); the offers the GM side only (#80). **A fitting on a saved build's line:** `item.fit { itemId, to: null, line }` (`line` a `<card>/<name>`, 3 to 130 characters) fits an item to a weapon (`weapons/<name>`) or the worn armour (`armour/<name>`) the actor's saved sheet gives, named as the source app finds a host, by name (`character.actions.ts` 222, 668). The line counts as the first kind whose `card` is the line's and that the actor's type holds; the kind's `fits.to` must name it, and #95's limits and refusals hold (`itemNotFound` for a line the sheet does not give, `itemFitsNot` for both `to` and `line` or a kind it does not fit). Stored as `actor_item.fitted_line` (never with `fitted_to`); taken off with `to: null`. Its effects count while it is fitted to a line, since a rebuild drops a fitting whose line it no longer gives, in the same logged action as the build's fields, as the source app drops a fitted upgrade with a weapon the new build does not give (`build.ts` 2402-2411); a rebuild that keeps the line keeps it. The seat's item carries `fittedLine` (absent when none). | 113 | A strike its owner sends, a choice input, and a strike once a round (`byOwner`, `type: "choice"`, a strike's `limit`) | **The flag:** a strike may say `byOwner: true`. **The path:** `strike.apply` checks its caller in its handler (`ACTION_PERMS` null, `ActionPerms` ""), as `item.fit` does. A seat holding `encounter:run` (the GM side, and every player seat of a room without a GM) applies any strike as before. Any other seat must hold the attacker (`character:edit:own` of it, the seat's own characters, never the GM-side fallback) and then, in this order, each its own refusal: the strike says `byOwner` (`strikeNotByOwner`), the attacker is the combatant acting now, `currentId` of the running fight, which in a sides order is the member acting (`strikeNotYourTurn`), a session runs, since a fight with none is paused (`sessionPaused`, the existing code), and the target's combatant is shown in full, `visibility: full` of house decision #75 (`strikeTargetHidden`); a seat that holds no such character is refused `notYourCharacter` (a screen `screenReadOnly`). This check runs before anything else of the strike is read, and every id is read from the room. On this path the hit the server's own plan gives is written as the fight's own write (`Trusted`), so the seat's `editableBy` of the target's wounds is not asked; it stays one logged action with its `strike` event under the seat's user, which the GM's Undo and TURN BACK take back and the fight's log shows. **The choice:** a strike input may be `{ id, label, type: "choice", options: [{ label, value }] (2 to 10, a value a whole number -1000..1000), cite? }`, read as `ctx.<id>`, an int, the first option's value when none is sent; any other value is `badInput`. At import: a choice with bounds (`a choice has no bounds`), without options (`a choice needs options`), options on another type (`only a choice has options`), two options of one value (`duplicate value`). Tables and spends keep their inputs as they were. The guide draws a choice as a row of buttons that wraps. **Once a round:** a strike may carry `limit: { per: "round", max }` (max 1 to 10), counted by house decision #91's mechanism under the id `strike:<strikeId>` (no spend id can take a colon), refused `usedThisRound` past max, written into the turn's `used` with the hit, so Undo and TURN BACK give the use back; a combatant's `used` then lists `strike:<id>`. A cost to the attacker (a Shock-like resource) is a further number (`shows`) the guide shows; nothing pays it. **The phone:** the guide SENDs (the typed total, never the harm) only where the server would take it (`strikeHold` on the web mirrors the refusals, the used count included); otherwise it reads the outcome out as "Tell the GM: …" with the reason. Until the phone's Fight tab draws `phoneTurn` (#215 part B), the guide is on the Sheet tab while the held character is in a fight. **The importer:** the ruleset validator has no warnings channel, so it is a refusal, for `byOwner` strikes only: an import whose `byOwner` strike a player's served copy (house decision #101, a player seat in a room with a GM) does not carry exactly as declared, cut or narrowed, is refused at `strikes[i]`: "a strike sent from a phone reads a value players may not read: <where>", `<where>` the first problem the strike has when put back into that copy. Go's `view.OwnerStrikeProblems` (run by the ruleset import and the check route) and the TypeScript reference `ownerStrikeProblems` agree on every sample and two crafted cases; the web's import form does not run it, so the server's refusal is where it shows. **Fixtures:** sample-harm 0.1.7, a sweep a hero's phone sends at a swarm once a round, its force a choice (more dice, more members thinned, as much strain shown), on the hero's `phoneTurn`; without it it hashes as 0.1.6 did. sample-duel is unchanged (the GM applies). | Auspex's phone (`strike-steps.tsx`, `power-steps.tsx`, rules-flow §11.173, §11.176) only read the damage out and left the GM to apply it; its power level was a fixed three-way pick and its once-a-Round power a combatant mark. Here a player sends what the server works out again, only for its own character on its own turn, and only at a target whose numbers it already sees, so the plan's outcome tells it nothing hidden (open question 1 of #215). A bar-only foe, or a strike that reads GM numbers, stays the GM's, as in Auspex. |
| 114 | Panels: what a phone shows while something holds (`panels`, the `panel` and `spends` widgets) | **The manifest:** an optional top-level `panels` list (at most 20), each `{ id, label, cite?, entities?, when?, holds?, unless?, opensOn?, lines?, figures?, spends?, options? }`. `entities` are the actor types whose phones show it (absent: every actor type). It **holds** while `when` (a boolean in the holder's scope plus `ctx.self` and `ctx.fight`, house decision #116) does, while the health state `holds` holds and `unless` does not (read from the running fight's combatant `states`, so outside a fight no state holds). `opensOn: { rises: <entity resource> }` makes it a sheet: it opens over whatever tab is open when that resource of the held character rises while a fight runs, and goes when an ask it offers is sent, when the value falls, when the fight ends or on CLOSE (Auspex's YOU'RE HIT). `lines` (at most 10) are `{ text (1 to 200, the ruleset's own words), when?, cite? }`, each shown while its `when` holds; `figures` (at most 6) `{ label, expr, cite? }` are numbers read from the holder; `spends` (1 to 4) name the ruleset's spends (#84) it offers, a spend with one input that takes fewer than 13 numbers asking with one button per number (I ROLLED n); `options: { entry, group?, usable?, needs? }` lists the attached packs' entries of type `entry`, read when opened, grouped by the entry field `group` (an enum's own labels), each with the importer's summary (its first text field other than `needs`) and cite; `usable` reads the holder's scope, `ctx` and `entry` (the entry's declared fields, empty where it has none), and an entry it does not hold for sinks below the rest with its `needs` text (Auspex's combat options, F57). A panel changes no number. A panel is a rule entry, so it needs a cite unless the ruleset is homebrew. **At import** (both engines, vectors): a duplicate id, an unknown actor type, `unknown state` for `holds` or `unless`, at `opensOn` an `unknown resource` or one that is not entity-scoped, `unknown spend`, at `options` an `unknown entity`, a `group` that is not a field of it, a `needs` that is not a text field of it, `a panel shows nothing` (no lines, figures, spends or options), and every expression compiled for each actor type it is for. An import also refuses a panel a player's served copy (#101) would lose or cut down (a line, a figure or the options reading a `gm` value), `a panel reads a value players may not read, so their copy loses it` or `loses part of it`, in the same check as #113's owner strikes (`view.OwnerStrikeProblems`, both engines, vectors), since a panel is drawn only on phones. Reminders, not automation: nothing it shows changes a value. |

| 130 | TURN BACK against Auspex's, question by question (#223) | The owner took #223's recommendations (2026-11-26). **Depth:** kept to the running session's log of the running fight (#82); with nothing to take, the button says why: "Turn back: no turn step in this session". **What goes back:** kept: every logged write of the step's actions, pools and an accepted spend's cost included. **Steps:** kept for a hold, an interrupt, a mark, an ambush mark and AWARE (Undo beside the button takes back one such tap); followed Auspex for BEGIN ROUND 1: an action that writes the turn value `begun` as true (the sides order's `begin`, #60) is a step of its own, kind `begin` in `turnBack` (`{ kind: "begin", round }`, no name), worded "the start of round {round}". Before this a begin was no step: it writes `currentId` only as null, and only from someone acting. **Changes made outside the log:** kept refusing (`turnBackRefused`), never overwriting. **Not built:** naming the refusing action in the log's words (the optional part); the refusal keeps "action {n} of {count}, counting back". **Cost:** `LastTurnStep` reads the fight's live deltas since the encounter began; measured on Postgres 18 at about 1 ms with 200 logged actions in one step, 2.5 ms with 1,000 and 5 ms with 3,000 (linear), so no index or bound was added. **Phone:** under 480px the fight bar puts the round on a line of its own and keeps its buttons to two rows, TURN BACK's label wrapping inside its button. | Auspex pushes a step before BEGIN ROUND 1 (`encounter.actions.ts`); without one a GM found TURN BACK disabled straight after the setup round. | 131 | Undo by line (#237) | The owner chose to build all of Auspex's action journal (2026-11-26). **The action:** `event.undo` takes an optional `actionId` (permission `encounter:run`, as Undo); without it Undo takes the newest standing action as before. The id must be a logged action of the running session (`nothingToUndo` otherwise) that stands (`alreadyUndone`). It goes back through the one undo path (`undoAction`), with its author check for a room without a GM, its refusals, an applied offer offered again (#80) and an approved ask reopened (#119). **The conflict:** before anything is written, a later standing action of the session that wrote one of the values this one set (a field, a condition row, a turn value, an item row; a condition is also matched by its condition on the same actor, since an add after an undone add makes a new row) refuses it with `undoLaterFirst { target, actionId }`, naming the value and the action to undo first; HISTORY marks that line. Counted values (a resource, a pool, a clock, a condition's count) are left out of the check: their inverse is a change, so a later change keeps its effect, as Undo always treated them. A change made outside the log is still refused by value (`undoChanged`). **What a refusal tells:** a refusal of Undo, TURN BACK or Redo about a value (`undoChanged`, `undoGone`, `undoLaterFirst`, `redoLaterFirst`, and the reason inside `turnBackRefused`) is cut to the seat that sent it by HISTORY's test of that value (#133): the GM side reads it whole; a seat that does not see the value (a co-GM with the combat scope only, on a character's `gm` condition) gets `changedUnseen`, with no value, no action and nothing it held. A condition's source is never quoted in a refusal. HISTORY marks "Undo this first" only on a line this seat is sent. **Reach:** the running session only, as Undo (#237 question 2): a session end is never in a running session's log, so it is never undone, as in Auspex. **TURN BACK beside it:** a turn step undone by line leaves TURN BACK reaching the step before it; there is no separate stack to keep (Auspex §11.183 keeps the step on its stack). | Auspex's `journal.undo` (`journal.actions.ts`, rules-flow §11.183) undoes any journaled action and refuses "Undo X first" when a later change touched the same rows or columns. Different: Auspex compares per column of every table, counters included; here counted values commute, as everywhere in the platform's undo. Auspex refuses a deleted or seated Agent's undo; here a deleted actor's writes are passed over and noted, as Undo always did, and seats are never logged. |

It can be undone and redone again. Undo without a line still takes the newest standing action by when it was first taken, so a redone older action is reached by it only after the later ones. **Refusals:** `nothingToRedo` when the action is not undone; `redoLaterFirst { target, actionId }` when a later standing action wrote one of the same set values (the check of #131 from the undo's time); `undoChanged` when a value changed outside the log; `cantRedoKind` when the undo deleted codex entries the action made (#97: they are gone). **An approval:** the undo keeps the asks it reopened (`reopened` in its payload; `request.Reopen` answers them). A redo approves each again by the same action while it is still waiting; one answered or withdrawn since refuses the redo with `requestAnsweredSince` before anything is written, since redoing it would spend the cost twice. **An undo held at a limit:** when the undo could not take back the whole of a counted value (Vigour at its max kept a heal from going through, or a deleted actor's or a gone pool's write was passed over), its redo is refused with `redoClamped { target }`: the action's counted writes and its undo's, summed by value, must cancel. The platform's undo event already holds what it wrote with before and after, which is exactly what a redo needs. An ask that was answered again since has a new answer that a redo cannot take back without a second spend; refusing keeps the ask's state the one the GM chose last. |

It is never pushed with the state: the `session:history` socket read answers it (`table.ReadHistory`, Undo's permission, its own rate limit of 120 a minute a seat), asked when HISTORY opens and again after each state while it stays open, then closed. `LiveState.history` stays the field the Seat view cuts, so both engines and the table vectors hold the cut equal. **Who:** the combat side, the seats Undo serves (`encounter:run`); every other seat gets none. | 181 | What the room document carries since #232 | The owner took #232's recommendations (2026-11-27): the document carries what Auspex's file carries and the platform's export dropped. **Sessions:** `startedAt`, `endedAt` and `award` (the XP each character was given, #120), written by the import straight onto the session it made (no action writes a session's dates, as `revealedAt` is kept, #86); a time left out is the import's, an award left out is none. **Actors:** `status` (absent is active; a character whose seat went stays retired, `auth/access.go`), each condition with its `stacks` when more than one (`{ "id", "stacks" }` beside a plain id; the import adds it that many times through `condition.add`, and a condition that would not reach them, one that does not stack or past its most, is refused `roomValueClamped`), and `xpAtAscension` and `packageWaived` (where a ledger build's next advance counts from, as `build.ascend` leaves them on `actor_build`). Every field is optional, so an older document imports as before. **Fresh start:** sessions go with their dates and awards, conditions with their stacks, and every character is active again (the source's `freshStart` clears `annihilated`); `xpAtAscension` and `packageWaived` stay with the build and its XP. **Not inferred:** a session is abandoned only when the document says so (`abandoned`), never from a missing end or award (auspex #327). Still not carried: a condition's source and expiry, a resource's `max_override`, the `temporary` flag (#118). | Auspex's file carries each session's `startedAt`, `endedAt` and `xp`, and a character's `annihilated` (wrath_glory `packages/shared/src/export.ts`), and its importer inserts sessions with their dates (`campaign.service.ts` 381-390). Auspex has no condition stacks and no advance bookkeeping in its file; the owner chose to carry both, since a part-paid advance would otherwise be owed again. ADR 0012 decision 4 is amended. The W&G converter must write the same fields. |
| 182 | A session still running when the room is exported | It is written ended at the export's time (`endedAt` is `exportedAt`), not abandoned, with no award: nothing is live in a new room, and an export is never refused for a running session. | The owner's choice (2026-11-27); Auspex writes a running session's `endedAt` as null and its importer keeps it open. |
| 183 | What names a pack the room is no longer served, in an export | The export writes only what the room is served (`actor.ServedPacks`: attached, not taken down, a private pack only while its owner runs the room). A pack it is no longer served leaves `expects.packs`; an item of a pack entry it is no longer served is written as a hand-written line under the name the sheet shows (its key, #95); what else names such an entry, as the import would refuse it, is left out: an entry's `source`, a codex `ref` field's value, a `tags` word that is no suggestion, a `rows` field's row `from` (the row stays, written by hand) and `ref` cell, and an actor's held record (#88). An actor's own `ref` field is kept: nothing checks it against the packs. Each is counted in the export's `Report` (`{ pictures, packs, items, sources }`, all but packs and items in `sources`), sent as the `X-Room-Export` header (JSON, `RoomExportReport`); the GM's EXPORT and the admin card fetch the file, save it, and say what was left out. **Not handled:** a ledger build whose catalogue pack is gone still stops the import (`buildNotReady`, as the room's own builder refuses it); a scene's roster line of such a pack is kept, shown as broken (ADR 0012 decision 3). | The owner's choice (2026-11-27): the export drops and counts, so a backup never fails to import over a pack detached after the fact. Auspex has no packs. |
| 184 | The picture count where the GM exports | Beside EXPORT, "Includes N pictures" from the pictures the seat's codex names (`pictureCount`), and EXPORT WITHOUT PICTURES only when N is more than 0, as Auspex shows them. The owner of a room without a GM does not see a picture until it is shown, so there the count is not shown and WITHOUT PICTURES always is. After an export, the line says how many pictures the file carries (the report's `pictures`). | Auspex counts the codex's pictures in the browser (`setup.tsx` 39-41, 131-141). |
| 185 | Telling anonymous devices apart in the admin console (#233) | The users list shows, for an anonymous device, its tag: the last six characters of its user id (`deviceTag`), beside "device"; and for every user when it was last seen; the rooms each sits in were already listed with their seats. REMOVE FROM EVERY ROOM asks for an account's name, or for a device's tag, typed; the dialog and its answer name a device with its tag, and so does a room's SEAT AS GM picker. Never an email or a device secret: the user id is not the device's cookie. | The owner's choice (2026-11-27). Auspex confirms by the typed name (`users-tab.tsx` 16-18), which cannot tell two devices named "Player" apart; #162 and #163 would make them fewer, and this does not replace them. |
| 186 | A scene's TAKE BACK and partial reveals (#233) | Kept as #86: TAKE BACK seals again what the begin revealed; an entry shown to some players before the begin and to everyone by it is sealed to everyone, not put back to its earlier readers. Nothing is built. | Auspex has the same limit (its reveal is one flag, `scene-reveal.ts` `undoBegin`); the owner chose to follow it (2026-11-27). |
| 187 | The library card's line (#233) | A ledger ruleset may name, in its build block, the kind whose chosen record names a finished character on its library card: `build.libraryLine`, `"archetypes"` or `"species"` (`LIBRARY_LINE_KINDS`, both engines, held equal by the manifest vectors), the two kinds the resolved sheet names one record of. The name is kept on the row (`library_character.line`, migration 0054) so listing reads no pack: written by a done save from the resolved sheet's `archetype` or `species`, by SAVE TO MY LIBRARY from the room's sheet field, cleared by a draft save, kept by a rename. YOUR CHARACTERS shows it under the name, as imported (a pack's own words). Without the key, or before a character is done, no line. | Auspex's card shows the archetype off a finished build's resolved sheet (`lobby-view.tsx` 62, `library.service.ts` 32-40), resolving each card as it lists; here a ruleset names which kind, since another game's builder may not have archetypes, and the name is kept so the list stays a plain read. |
| 188 | A library draft kept in the browser (#233) | Every change in the library's builder is kept at once in this browser's `localStorage`, per user and character (`it-library-draft:<userId>:<characterId>`, so a shared browser never offers one user another's draft), with the revision the server had answered when it was made. When a draft save goes through, the copy goes if it is the build saved (compared as documents, not text), else it is a later change and is re-stamped with the new revision; a done save and a delete forget it. Opened again, the builder starts from the browser's copy when it was made over the revision the server holds or a later one, saying so when it differs from the server's build ("Restored from this browser"); otherwise the server's build wins. Storage that throws (private mode) leaves the server's autosave only. The builder is drawn once the page is in the browser, so the copy is read once there. Rooms' builders keep no browser copy. | Auspex's builder keeps its draft in `localStorage` and the library too (`builder.tsx` 343, 404-411), and picks between them (`pickDraft`); the owner chose to follow it, with the revision check of #104 deciding which wins. |
| 189 | A room without a GM strikes with the guide its values allow (#224) | In a room without a GM (`gmless`, `solo`) every player seat holds `encounter:run` (house decision #113) and runs the fight from its RUN tab's tracker, and on its own turn from FIGHT: both show the strike guide for the strikes its served copy keeps. That copy is cut as any player's (house decision #101): a strike whose figures, additions, values or outcomes read a `gm` value is narrowed to the attacker types it compiles for, else left out, and a means, further number or note it cannot read goes alone, so the guide never shows a number the seat may not read or the server would not plan. A ruleset that wants the guide in such a room declares the strike over values every seat reads. No code change; a reference test holds it for `gmless` and `solo`. | Owner decision on #224 question 5. Auspex always has a GM, so it has no answer; leaking a `gm` value to make a guide whole would break principle 4. |
| 190 | Warnings in the fight's log, in the ruleset's words (#224) | A strike's outcome (house decision #99) and a health state may each carry **`warn`** (1 to 80 characters, the ruleset's own words), validated by both engines and served to every seat as the rest of the definition. The fight's log (house decision #100) adds a line part `<name>: <warn>`: for a logged strike whose outcome has one, naming the target; for each health state with one that holds for an actor once the line's writes are made and did not before them, naming that actor. The states are worked out on the web with the engine (`healthStatesOf`) over the actor's values with each written resource and field set to the line's first `before` or last `after`, everything else as it stands now (a `ponytail:`: a much older line reads values that may have changed since). A state already held raises nothing again; nothing changes a number. sample-harm 0.1.13: Fallen and a new Gone state (a destroyed hero or brute) warn, and a blow that thins a swarm does. | Owner decision on #224 question 6, following Auspex's chronicle, which appends ANNIHILATED (p.194), TRAUMATIC INJURY (p.193) or MORTAL WOUNDS (p.199) to the hit's line (wrath_glory `tracker.tsx` 72-138, `noteOf`); there each is worked out in code from one game's rules, here the ruleset words which outcome or state warns, so no game rule is written in code. A state is told on entry, not on every hit while it holds: a warning per further wound waits for a ruleset that needs one. |
| 191 | A record's extra rolls and gear (#226) | A held kind (house decision #88) may name **`extraRolls`**, an `int` field of its entry type, and **`gear`** `{ field, kind }`, a `list` field of its entry type naming entries of the item kind `kind` (#95). Refused at import, both engines: `"<entry>" has no int field "<f>"`, `"<entry>" has no list field "<f>"`, `unknown item kind "<k>"`, `<type> holds no "<k>"` for a holder type the item kind is not for. **Granting** a record (a lookup line's `grant`, `table.apply`): when the record's entry has a whole number of 1 or more in `extraRolls`, the answer carries the notice `extraRolls { entry, count }` ("Scales: roll 2 more on this table"); nothing rolls, and an included record (#165) raises none. Each key the record's `gear` list names that the room is served becomes an item of that kind the actor carries, written as `item.add` writes a pack entry's line (`source: hand`), under the same logged action, so one Undo takes the record and its gear back: a counted kind's line goes up by one, a `unique` kind's entry held already is skipped, a key named twice is given once (counted: twice), and a kind its new items would take past its max refuses the whole grant (`itemsFull`). Bonus dice are not built. sample-harm 0.1.14 declares both on growths; a table test grants them. | Auspex's mutations answer "ROLL n MORE MUTATIONS (p.N)" (`character.actions.ts` 520-537, `extraRolls`) and put a mutation's weapons on the sheet (`sheetMutation`); its `gear` and the bonus dice its text describes are only transcribed or shown, never computed, so the owner's rule (#226 question 4) leaves bonus dice out. |
| 192 | A lookup stepper capped at a shared pool (#226) | A lookup's `int` input (#87) may write `max` as an int expression instead of a number, read over **`pools.<id>`** (every resource whose scope is not `entity`, its value as it stands) and `campaign`; nothing else (`unknown identifier`). Refused: on a switch (`a switch has no bounds`, and `only a stepper's max is an expression`), beside `dice` (`a dice amount takes its bounds from its dice`). It is read when a typed result is read or applied (`lookupMaxes`, Go `LookupMaxes`, held equal by the lookup vectors with random pools): one below the input's min takes its min, and an input above it is refused `tableInput` as any input out of bounds; one that cannot be read refuses the line `unreadable`. The GM's stepper stops at it. A strike's inputs keep whole-number bounds. sample-skirmish 0.1.14: Backlash eased by the momentum spent, at most what Momentum holds. | Auspex caps the Glorious Critical's Glory stepper at the pool (`crit-perils.tsx` 38, 131, `maxSpend = Math.max(0, glory)`); a spend's input max is already an expression (#84). | 196 | The GM changes an entry's values for one acquisition (#228) | `request.approve` may carry **`item`** `{ fields?, tags? }` on an acquisition's ask (house decision #102): `fields` maps fields of the entry type to values (a whole number, a word or a yes-or-no, each checked against the field as a field write is, `fieldInvalid`; one the type does not declare is `badRequest`), `tags` replaces the entry's tags (at most 30). The acquisition is planned with the entry's body and tags so changed, so the Test's inputs, and its number, read them; the pack entry, the item granted and its kind stay the pack's: tags that would make the entry another kind of item are refused (`badRequest`), and so is `item` on an ask that is not an acquisition. Only this call reads the change. **The screen:** on the GM's request bar an acquisition ask has CHANGE, which opens one box per whole-number and choice field of the entry type and a tags box (commas), blank keeping the pack's value, and APPROVE WITH CHANGES. The approval bar is where the GM side already decides an ask. |
| 197 | Tags compared folded; write-ins upper-cased where a game prints them so (#230) | A `tags` field's words (house decision #89) are compared folded, ignoring case: "Brutal" and "brutal" are the same word, so a list holding both is `a word is there twice` (both engines' field check), and a word matches a suggestion (an option id or a served entry's name) in any case on the server (`codex.save`). A word is stored as it was sent: a suggestion picked on the screen is the suggestion's own spelling, a write-in what was typed. A tags field may declare **`upper: true`**: the GM's editor upper-cases a write-in as it adds it (the server keeps what it is sent). Absent, a manifest validates and hashes as before. sample-d20 0.1.17: a faction's written-in marks are upper-cased. | Owner decision on #230 question 2: Auspex keeps a faction's keywords as the book prints them, upper-cased (§11.197, `writeInKeyword` in the builder); folding the comparison stops a second spelling of one word without rewriting what the GM typed in a game that does not print its tags so. |
| 198 | A Trait on a card, and a fitting that changes one (#228 Q2) | A Trait is a word with an optional rating, printed "Word (n)" or "Word", in the card's `traits` (an array, or the comma text an entry body writes). A fitting's effect `host.trait.<Word>` changes one on its host's card, for a fitting whose kinds fit to a kind drawn as `weapons` or `armour`: `upgrade` gains it at the value (0 for no rating) or keeps the better rating it has; `add` moves a rating it has (a Trait it lacks is untouched, 0 or less takes it off); `override` sets the rating, and 0 takes it off. The word has no bracket or comma and at most 40 characters; the value is a number that reads nothing and takes no `when`. A Trait is matched by its word in any case, whatever its rating, and written back as the fitting words it; a rating stays at most 999. The effects apply in the order the items are fitted, after the card's numbers, in `withFittedCard` (both engines), so the card, the strike guide and `strike.apply` read the same list, and a `list` strike value reads it. `by` names only an item that changed something. Fixtures: the ledger pack 0.1.4 (a Bow String makes a bow Keen, a Twin Mount takes a point of Slow off) and sample-harm 0.1.15 (Spikes fitted to a carried arm make it Brutal and take its Light off). **Not ported:** a range multiplied, bonus dice, a GM's note on a fitting, and Talents worked into a weapon's line. | Auspex's three Trait mods (`weapon-effect.ts`, `traitMods` in `wargear.ts`): `trait` keeps the better rating, `traitRating` moves one and drops it at 0, `removeTrait` takes it off; set by an override is the one addition, so removal needs no mode of its own. The card already carried Traits as printed words, so no new card shape. |
| 199 | A character made by hand beside the ledger build, and what a build over it keeps (#229) | Creation steps may make the type `build.actor` names (the refusal is lifted). `build.first` (`build`, the default, or `steps`) names the path a character with neither takes first; the build page shows the GM side a BUILD / BY HAND picker starting there, until the first save. Only the GM side makes one by hand (`actor.build` on that type answers `byHandGm` to a player seat), and a player always builds, whatever `first` says. The first save records the path: values, choices or a ledger row (a draft alone claims the character for the ledger) answer `alreadyBuilt` to the other path. A build over a hand-made sheet is the GM side's (the page sends `keepSheet` on its ready saves; the api reads it only for such a sheet). Besides `keep.fields` and `keep.resources` (#96), `keep.greater` names int fields that keep the greater of the hand-made value and the build's (the better armour), and `keep.merge` list fields that take the build's items, then each hand-made item past the copies the build has (a second copy is kept); at most the field's `maxItems`. A field is kept one way only; the steps on a type both make set what its derived values read, as on any type creation makes. One function with a Go twin (`keptValue`, `KeptValue`), held equal by vectors. Fixture: the ledger sample 0.1.7 (a by-hand step, `first: build`, the better armour and merged keywords). **Not built:** a second fixture shape, and the phone showing an override number once. | Owner's answers to #229: the ruleset names the default path and the GM side picks (Q1), only the GM side makes one by hand as in Auspex (Q2), Auspex's merge rules declared (Q3: `keepPlay`'s better armour by rating, `keepSheetWeapons`' merge by name with copies kept; Wealth, stores and Corruption are `keep.resources`). |
| 200 | Where a checklist step's GO TO lands inside a tab (#230 Q3) | `goto.anchor` names a place from a fixed list each GM tab declares (`CHECKLIST_ANCHORS`, both engines): PARTY's `session` (the session band), `characters`, `invite`, `ascension` (opened when folded), `safety` (lines and veils) and `tableMode` (how the table sits); RULES' `settings` (the campaign's own values, where a Tier or a chapter is set), `packs` and `world`; TESTS' `open` calls. A tab not listed has none; the codex keeps its `kind`. A step naming a place its tab lacks is refused at import. The GM's page opens the tab, then scrolls to the place once it is drawn, opens its fold and puts it in focus; it looks for about half a second (30 frames), then gives up, so a place the seat's scopes do not draw is not reached, the tab stays open, and a later visit does not jump there; picking a tab by hand drops a waiting GO TO. The checklist names it "PARTY › Session". Fixture: sample-skirmish 0.1.15 (lines and veils, and the chapter on RULES' settings). | Owner's answer to #230 Q3: fixed per-tab anchors, as Auspex's setup panel fixes them (`setup-panel/constants.ts`: the framework box, Ascension, the agents, the session band), rather than regions a ruleset names: the app owns its screens, so a ruleset cannot point at a box that does not exist. |
| 201 | The order pack entries come in (#231 Q2) | The pack file's entry order is the book's: the importer records each entry's place in the file (`pack_entry.ordinal`, migration 0055), and the served entries (`LoadServedEntries`: `codex.fromPack`'s children, the scene's roster, every served lookup) and the seat's rules read (`GET /api/campaigns/:id/rules`: the RULES reader, the pack adder) come in it, pack by pack (the lookup by pack slug, the rules read by version), the key breaking a tie. The order is not part of the pack's hash, so the same entries in another order re-imported are the same version, which takes the new file's order. The ledger catalogue and the owner's export follow it too. Entries imported before the migration take their file order back from their ids (uuidv7, written in the file's order). | Owner's answer to #231 Q2: Auspex adds a place's children in book order (`codex.addPlaces`); a column, not a field a pack names, so no pack has to declare it. |
| 202 | A generator run with no entry of its own (#231) | `make.root: false` (with `each`): the run makes only its rounds' entries, under the entry the GM names on CONFIRM (`codex.generate`'s `parentId`, the open entry to start with) or each loose without one; `make`'s own title, `from` and fields are not made. One Undo takes them all back; a run of no rounds makes nothing and logs nothing. This covers Auspex's ROOMS FOR A PLACE (rooms under a place that exists, no new hulk) and loot made loose, one entry per piece. Both engines (`checkRun` answers `root: null`), vectors. Fixture: sample-d20 0.1.18 (more chambers for a warren already made). **Not built here (#231, left):** several repeat groups, steps in any order, a step filling a `rows` field, a title template, a table chosen by an earlier result with every roll kept, rolled-field modifiers, "roll twice", a grid, a single-match pick, a default DN, one Test per Skill, a link found by name, dice counts in a row (needs the owner's answer to Q1). | Auspex's `codex.addHulk` with a place named and `codex.addLoot` (`codex.actions.ts` 402-484): what the run makes is the rounds; the root is optional rather than a second kind of generator. |
| 203 | A handout's paper in the dark set (#235) | **A pick, `shapes.handoutPaper`: `follow` (the first, as before) or `light`.** With `light`, a paper handout (every layout but `plain`) keeps the World's light card in the dark set, as Auspex's vox logs and decrees stay a sheet of paper on a night phone: the room's `<style>` declares the light set's palette, every token resolved, on `.handout.parchment`, and the card reads its paper and ink from it. A World with no light set has no light paper to keep, so the pick then draws nothing (not refused: `ponytail:` in `lib/world/world.ts`). | A pick is one value where three more tokens would repeat the light set; the paper is the World's own, never the platform's. |
| 204 | The phone's readouts: scanlines and a clipped corner (#235) | **A pick, `shapes.readout`: `plain` (the band) or `slate`.** `slate` draws thin scanlines across the phone's strip and its fight plate, and clips the fight plate's top-left and bottom-right corners by 10px, as Auspex's dataslate does (`.slate-card`, `.cut`). Buttons keep `corners`: the clipped corner is the readout's, not a control's, so no focus ring is cut. Under every World the strip and the plate lie on the band (part A). **Contrast:** the lines mix 4% of the band's ink into the band under text, so a World that picks `slate` holds `band-ink` on `band` to 5:1 (`WORLD_SLATE_PAIR`, both engines), which keeps at least 4.5:1 on a line. | Auspex clips only its dataslate tiles; one pick carries the slate's two marks together, and any setting with a machine readout can take it. |
| 205 | Emblems and the sealed plate (#235) | **A pick, `shapes.gmSeal`: `none`, `crest` or `skull`.** GM-only content (`.sealed`: the codex entry's GM notes, a scene's closed fields) lies on the page's second surface under a spiked crest bar on a rule (`crest`), or the bar with a winged skull at its middle (`skull`), in the second ink, as Auspex's sealed plate. The skull is carried from Auspex's own drawing (its `.plate::after` mask, byte for byte; auspex_gm `docs/frontend.md` §7: none of its look uses book art), an inline SVG mask, no image file. **No open list of emblems:** a World cannot bring an image (ADR 0010; images wait for image storage, P4), and the platform draws only this one mark until another game needs another. | Ships Auspex's sealed plate now without images; a new mark is one more value on the list. |
| 206 | A sheet's table heads on the band (#235) | **A pick, `shapes.tableHead`: `plain` (the grid's head in the page's ink, the card list's ruled in the accent) or `band`.** `band` lays the header row of a sheet's grid (`.w-grid-head`) and card list (`.w-cards-head`) on the band under the band's ink, in both sets, as Auspex's stat-block heads are iron by day and by night. | The ink head turns light in a dark set; the band stays the chrome's colour, which every World holds to 4.5:1. |
| 207 | Side colours (#235) | **Two colour tokens, `side-ally` and `side-foe`, in both sets; base values Auspex's day blue and orange (`#2f5f9e`, `#b3541e`), tuned for a light card.** Under a World the phone's order marks each row's side with a 6px stripe on its card: the seat's side in `side-ally`, the other in `side-foe`; the acting row keeps the accent's stripe. The base Themes draw no stripe, so a room without a World looks as before. **No contrast pair:** the stripe is never the only sign (the foe's name opens its card, the seat's rows say YOU), so the importer does not refuse a World over it; a dark World sets its own night values (Auspex's `#7fa6e0`, `#e08a4a`). A dark World that leaves them out keeps the light base values, and the blue then reads about 2.7:1 on a night card: still a non-text mark, never the only sign, but a dark World should set both.
