| 208 | The browser's colour, the QR code and the picture ground (#235) | **The browser's theme colour follows the World:** in a room wearing one it is the World's band, per set it brings (one colour for one set; a `prefers-color-scheme` pair for two), as Auspex's is its iron band (`generateViewport` on the room page, `roomBand`). The set the device picked with the scheme switch wins when the World brings it, read from the scheme cookie; the switch refreshes the page, so the browser's colour follows a new pick. With no pick it is a `prefers-color-scheme` pair. Elsewhere it is the base Theme's band. **The QR code keeps ink on paper** from the base Theme (`lib/tokens/tokens.ts`) whatever the World: a scanner needs dark on light, and Auspex's is fixed too. **The picture canvas keeps the base paper:** a picture is saved, and moves between rooms, so its ground is not a World's. | The browser chrome is the room's frame; the QR code and a saved picture must work outside it. |
| 209 | Marker words (#235) | **The six marker colours stay the platform's** (house decision #92: they match the tags on the miniatures). **The words wait for #159:** each will be a term `marker.<colour>` in a World's terms, defaulting to the app's word; the hook is `MarkerSwatch` (`components/ui/marker-swatch`). Nothing is built until #159 gives terms a shape. | The colours are physical; the words are the setting's. |
| 210 | Which of Auspex's look is a World's pick (#235) | **Picks, each one any World may take:** the cold paper ground (`texture: paper`), the quill rule, the torn band, the spiked crest, the double-frame button, square corners, capitals, light handout paper (#203), the slate readout (#204), the sealed GM plate (#205), banded table heads (#206), side colours (#207), the printed stat block (#219) and the chapter rail (#220). **Words, which wait for #159:** the marker words (#209) and the `+++ X +++` stamps (#221). **Not a pick:** the page's layout beyond the GM's navigation (where a tab's content sits, the tracker's side panel), which is the platform's. **Typefaces:** Auspex's three (Cinzel, Crimson Pro, IBM Plex Mono) are open-licensed (SIL OFL) and already on the platform's list, loaded only when a World picks them. | Each piece a World can say is one a second game can use; the rest is the platform's layout or #159's words. |
| 211 | A ruleset's labels in more than one language (#159) | **Shape:** every `label`, the values of `labels`, `inputLabels` and `inputOptionLabels`, may be the label itself (every language) or a map from language (`en`, `vi`) to label, in the one document (decision (a) of 2026-11-26). A map names at least one language. The ruleset's own language is `language` (absent: English). **Reading:** a seat reads each label in its device's language, else in the ruleset's own, else in the first language the map gives (`en` before `vi`), so a label always shows. **Not per language yet:** summaries, notes, titles, cites, decision texts and the ledger builder's `build.params` labels stay one string. `ponytail:` one language for those, a map there too when an importer asks. | The labels are written where they are used, so an author sees both languages side by side; the fallback never leaves a blank on a screen. |
| 212 | The app's terms and the lookup (#159) | **Terms:** a closed list the app reads (`TERM_IDS`: party, foe, gm, player, session, fight, round, turn, character, creature, mob, scene, mission, handout, codex, tableScreen, and the six marker colours of #92, which settles #225 Q7). A term is a word, or a word and its plural as ICU calls them (`{ "one", "other" }`); a word alone is its own plural (decision (b) of 2026-11-27). Classifiers and articles stay in the app's sentences. **Where:** a World's `terms` and a ruleset's `terms`, each term the words for every language or a map per language. **Lookup, per viewer's language:** the room's World in that language; the ruleset's term in that language; the ruleset's term in its own language; the app's own word. A term given only in another language than these is never shown: the app's word is. A ruleset's `terms` keep any id (older rulesets used free ids); the app reads only the closed list, and a World refuses an id outside it. | A missing word falls back to a word the reader can read, never to a third language. The list is short so a World author knows what can change. |
| 213 | How a message names a term (#159) | `[party]` the word as written, `[Party]` with a capital letter, `[PARTY]` in capitals, `*` for the plural (`[party*]`). A message is filled when it is loaded: outside a room with the app's words (the `terms` namespace of each message file), in a room with the room's words (`RoomWorld`). A word is written into the ICU text with its apostrophes doubled, `{ } < >` quoted, and `#` quoted only inside a plural or selectordinal branch (where ICU reads it as the count), so a term can hold any character. Typed command words (`command.words`) and words that mean something else (text length, a generator's rounds, "Quest") are not terms. The Vietnamese messages now use one word per term (giao tranh, buổi, nhóm, sinh vật, sổ tay, đám đông, người chơi; the owner's words of 2026-11-27) where they mixed synonyms. The notebook's MISSION view ("Cốt truyện") and "who goes first" ("Người đi đầu") are words of their own, not terms. A room sends only the messages it words differently, laid over the page's (`RoomMessages`, always mounted). | The messages keep their keys and ICU arguments, so no component changes; a component that needs a word outside a message calls `useTerm`. |
| 214 | Which side reads a ruleset in a language (#159) | The api stores and serves a ruleset with every language of its labels (`Normalized`); the web reads the served copy in the viewer's language (`localRuleset`). What the api words itself (refusal parameters, a pool's or health state's label in a snapshot, the ledger's problem texts) is in the ruleset's own language (`Ruleset.Local`). `ponytail:` the socket does not know a seat's language; send the device's language on the socket if a mixed-language table needs it. | Every screen label the web draws follows the device; the few the api words stay in one language, the ruleset's. |
| 215 | Labels in a seat's copy of the ruleset (#159) | A seat is sent every language of the labels it may read; a definition it may not read (a `gm` resource, field, condition, derived value, a table's entries) goes with every word of its label, in every language. The copy is not cut to the seat's language. | The cut is by definition, as before (#101), so a label can never reach a seat its definition does not; two languages are a few bytes. |
| 219 | The printed stat block (#235) | **A pick, `shapes.statBlock`: `plain` (the sheet's cards, as before) or `printed`.** The platform draws one stat-block panel, `StatBlock` (`components/ui/stat-block`), around the sheet's own widgets (`SheetView`, `SheetWidget`), for every actor that is not a player's character: the GM's sheet of a combatant (the tracker's bottom sheet), the phone's foe card, and the TV's card of a creature the GM shows in full (`ScreenCard`, whose name heads it). `printed` draws Auspex's stat block from tokens: a spiked crest over a title bar in the accent under its ink (the creature's name), each section's head as a ragged band on the band, a widget's label as a ragged bar in the accent (Auspex's `.sb-title`, `.sb-band`, `.tbar`). Every pair is one the validator holds (accent-ink on accent, band-ink on band). The name is the panel's heading for a screen reader; with `plain` and in the base Themes it stays out of sight, since the caller already shows it, so a room without a World looks as before. **Not built:** the codex has no creature stat-block view on `main` (an entry made from a creature is a form of fields); when one is added it wraps its sheet in `StatBlock`. | One panel the platform draws from the sheet's own widgets: a game's ruleset lays the rows out, a World only picks how the panel is dressed. |
| 220 | The GM's chapter rail (#235) | **A pick, `shapes.gmNav`: `tabs` (along the top, as before) or `rail`.** From 900px wide, `rail` stands the GM's tabs in a column down the right edge, as Auspex's chapter rail: the page's second surface with an inner shade, each tab 64px tall with its chapter's number faint behind it (a CSS counter, hidden from screen readers), the open tab on a torn stroke in the accent under its ink and in bold. Narrower, they stay the strip along the top, as Auspex's do under 900px. **It does not fork the shell:** the same `nav` and the same buttons (`.gm-shell`, `.gm-tabs`), laid out by CSS under the pick; the page keeps a 112px gutter for it, and beside the rail the GM's page takes the full width left, as Auspex's GM body does (its single-column builder pages stay a left column of 820px, and the platform's pages outside the GM shell keep their centred column). Auspex's rail holds links and a SETUP foot; the platform's holds its own tabs in its own order. | A layout of the shell a World selects, with no per-game code and no second shell to keep in step. |
| 221 | Stamps (`+++ X +++`, #235) | **Wait for #159.** Auspex's stamps (`+++ HANDOUT +++`, `+++ YOU'RE HIT +++`) are words, so they will be terms in a World's terms (`stamp.<event>`), drawn by the platform in the mono face; nothing is built until #159 gives terms a shape. The hook is the phone's NEW mark on its tabs (`components/player/player-app`). | Words are the setting's and live with #159's terms; the look of a stamp is the platform's. |
| 222 | The GM's PARTY as cards and a drawer (Auspex's PARTY) | **No new layout target: the card reads what a ruleset already declares.** A seated character still `active` (held by a member or a live guest token) is a card; the rest wait under IN RESERVE, folded (open when no one is seated). A card shows its picture, name, a line (the name chosen for `build.libraryLine`, else the first `text` field of its entity with a value), who plays it, a bar per health track (`health.tracks`: its value against its limit) and per harm track (`health.harm.wounds`/`shock`: its limit less the harm taken, as Auspex draws Wounds 16/16), the numbers of its layout's `phoneStrip` (else `trackerRow`) that are not already a bar and its XP where the ruleset keeps XP, the labels of its `health.states` that hold (worked out from the GM side's values, as the Ascension panel does) and its objectives' count (#223). A track whose limit cannot be worked out here draws no bar. The card opens one drawer from the right edge: STATUS (its resources with their steppers, harm through the tracker's hit pad in a fold, as harm moves only by a hit, its conditions with + CONDITION, its objectives, its rests, spends and a called Test), GEAR (its items, `SheetView part="gear"`), ADVANCE (XP, the builder, the rest of the sheet, `part="advance"`) and SEAT (invite, guest QR, whisper, revoke, remove the player). Everything else (invites, screens, new character, prints, the setup checklist, how the table sits, safety, Ascension, the journal, seats, the room) waits under MORE; the band on top holds the session's state, START/END… and NEXT SESSION. | The owner's rule: less buttons, fewer clicks, a better overview at arm's length. Every number already had a declaration the phone strip or the tracker reads; a `partyCard` target would have repeated `phoneStrip` in every ruleset. |
| 223 | A character's objectives (Auspex's per-agent objectives) | **The quests listed to it: no new table.** A character's objectives are the codex's quests revealed (`party`/`public`) to a `listed` audience that includes it, which only the seats holding it receive (the seat view's audience cut) and which its phone already folds under YOUR ORDERS. The drawer's STATUS lists them: the box is the quest's status (`done` / `open`), the select the quest it sits under (`codex.move`, Auspex's story link), × takes the character off the audience (`codex.reveal` without it) or, when it was the only reader, seals the quest back to the GM (`visibility: gm`), so it returns to the codex with its notes, picture and children: nothing is ever deleted from here, as `codex.delete` has no undo. NEW OBJECTIVE + ADD writes an open quest (`codex.save`) and lists it to the character (`codex.reveal`); when the listing is refused, the quest just made (which holds nothing else yet) is deleted again, the text stays in the box and the refusal shows. The card counts them ("3 objectives · 1 done"); a quest listed to several characters counts for each. Editing them needs a GM (a room without one has no private orders to keep, and cannot seal), `codex:write` and `codex:reveal`. | Reuses the one path quests already take, the seat view already cuts and the phone already shows; Auspex's rolled session objective is a W&G table roll, left to its ruleset. |
| 224 | Which tables are dealt (`tables.<id>.deck`) | **A deck is declared.** A table gains an optional `deck: true`; DEAL and RESHUFFLE show on it, and the server takes `table.deal` and `table.reshuffle` on it, only when it says `deck` or its visibility is `owner` (which already means dealt face down). Any other table is refused with `tableNotDeck` ("That table is rolled, not dealt."). Both engines read it (`isDeck` in `packages/rules-engine`, `RandomTable.IsDeck` in `internal/engine`), held equal by the vectors. The sample fixtures that deal say `deck: true` (sample-harvest 0.1.3). | Every table showed DEAL and RESHUFFLE, so a GM could deal a Critical Hit or a loot count as if it were a deck of cards; only the ruleset knows which of its tables are a deck. |
| 225 | Where a table shows on TRACK | **Where it is used.** A table a generator step names (`steps[].table`, `each.steps[].table`) is not listed on TRACK: it is rolled inside its generator in CODEX. A lookup (`isLookupTable`) during a fight sits on the selected combatant's panel, one toggle per lookup table (its title) and then the lookup for that combatant, with no "For" picker. Every other table is listed under "Tables · n" as one row each (title, die, who sees it); a tap opens one side drawer with the die, who sees it and the cite, ROLL, "or I rolled [ ] LOG IT" (only with a die), the last result, this table's recent rolls, and DEAL and RESHUFFLE (a second tap) only on a deck (#224). Outside a fight a lookup stays in the list and its drawer keeps the "For" picker. The section is not drawn when nothing is listed.
